- **`Cond`** needs no codegen. Text ternaries keep both forms: `{if}…{else}…{/if}` (static text, lazy) and `{orbit.Cond(c, "a", "b")}` (one line, eager). Astro's `x ?? "fallback"` is the standard library's `cmp.Or(x, "fallback")` (first non-zero value), documented next to them.
- **`class:list`**: unchanged; its keyed items (`"active": cond`) are the conditional form for classes.
- **Booleans on attributes**: (Q3).
- **`orbit fmt` and the LSP**: both helpers are ordinary Go calls in a Go region: gopls completes and hovers them, `orbit fmt` formats them as expressions, the VS Code grammar and the Chroma lexer need nothing. The lowering is invisible to the editor (tolerant mode keeps the call).

#### (Q) points
- **(Q1) Shape of the conditional attribute.**
  - (a) **Recommended**: `orbit.If[T any](cond bool, v T) any` plus the typed-pass lowering above. Works everywhere a Go expression does (frontmatter, attributes, content, `orbit.Attrs`, `any` props) with no runtime change (the `Attr`/`Expr` rules already omit `nil`); 0 allocations where it's lowered, and otherwise for constants and false conditions. Cost: one recognized call in the typed pass (small, like `class:list`'s `if`s).
  - (b) Non-generic `orbit.If(cond bool, v any) any`, no lowering: the simplest code, but a dynamic `v` is boxed (1 allocation) even when the condition is false.
  - (c) A typed option, `orbit.If[T](cond, v T) orbit.Opt[T]`: type-safe in frontmatter variables, but `Attr`/`Expr` (pass 1, tolerant mode, spreads) can only see an `Opt[T]` through an interface (boxing, plus a new runtime case), and pass 2 must lower it anyway for zero allocations. A pointer result (`*T`, nil when false) is the same, and `Attr` would print a non-nil `*string` with `fmt.Sprint` unless it gets a case too.
  - (d) A template construct, lazy and zero-cost with no helper: a conditional attribute group, `<a {if cur}aria-current="page"{/if}>` (templ has `<hr if p.Big { class="big" } />`). It can set several attributes at once and evaluates lazily, but it's new grammar (parser, `orbit fmt`, the LSP's regions, the VS Code grammar, the Chroma lexer), has no Astro counterpart, and ports `cond ? v : undefined` less directly than (a).
  - (e) Docs only: a three-line user helper (`func If(c bool, v any) any`) in the docs. Nothing to maintain, but every project rewrites it, it boxes, and nothing is lowered.
- **(Q2) The two-branch helper.**
  - (a) **Recommended**: ship `orbit.Cond[T any](cond bool, a, b T) T`. It covers the two-branch attribute values `{if}` can't (`aria-selected`, `title`, `href`, `rel`: 14 in `nyth`) and one-word text, at zero cost through the existing typed writes. Name alternatives: `orbit.Choose`, `orbit.Ternary`.
  - (b) Not shipped: text uses `{if}…{else}…{/if}`, attributes a frontmatter variable or the user's own helper. Keeps the API to one helper, at the cost of more boilerplate in loops.
- **(Q3) Booleans on attributes that aren't HTML boolean attributes (`aria-*`, `data-*`, `draggable`, …).** Orbit writes `true` bare and omits `false` on every attribute (§4). Astro 7.3 (`runtime/server/render/util.js`, `addAttribute`) does that only for HTML boolean attributes (`allowfullscreen async autofocus autoplay checked controls default defer disabled disablepictureinpicture disableremoteplayback formnovalidate inert loop muted nomodule novalidate open playsinline readonly required reversed scoped seamless selected itemscope`, any case, plus `hidden`, `popover` and `download` when the value is a boolean); on every other attribute it writes `"true"`/`"false"`, and on a custom element (a tag name with `-`) it writes `"true"`/`"false"` for every attribute. The trap: `aria-pressed={saved}` (twice in `nyth`), or `aria-hidden={true}`, renders a bare `aria-pressed`, an empty value that ARIA treats as the default (not pressed, not hidden), and `draggable={false}` is omitted (so `auto`) instead of `draggable="false"`. A port that keeps these renders different, wrong ARIA without any error.
  - (a) **Recommended** (rule: Astro's behavior): adopt Astro's rule exactly (the list above, the three conditional names, custom elements). The attribute name is static at every typed call site, so the choice is made at build time (`AttrBool` for the listed names, pieces `="true"`/`="false"` otherwise): zero runtime cost. `Attr`, `Spread` and `SpreadRest` (runtime names) check the name with a switch, for `bool` values only. Visible change: today's presence idiom `data-featured={props.Featured}` (in `components.md` and `styles-and-scripts.md`) becomes `data-featured={orbit.If(props.Featured, "")}`, written `data-featured=""` (Astro writes a bare `data-featured` for `""`; same DOM, so Orbit's `=""` stays, decided by rule: simplest), which is `nyth`'s own idiom (`read ? "" : undefined`), so the 8 presence markers port literally.
  - (b) Only `aria-*` writes `"true"`/`"false"`; everything else keeps today's rule. Fixes ARIA and keeps the `data-*` presence idiom, but is a third behavior (neither Astro's nor today's) and misses `draggable`/`spellcheck`/`contenteditable` and custom elements.
  - (c) Keep today's rule, plus a typed-pass warning on `aria-*={bool}` (`aria-pressed={…} is a bool: true writes a bare aria-pressed, which ARIA reads as empty; write strconv.FormatBool(x) or orbit.Cond(x, "true", "false")`) and a `coming-from-astro.md` note. No behavior change, but ported code still needs editing at each site.
  - (d) Docs only.

#### Cost (prototype `scratchpad/small-specs3/`, the real `orbit.Writer` through a `replace`, Go 1.27.3, `testing.AllocsPerRun`; no timings, the machine was reserved for another agent's profiling)
| Call site | allocs |
|---|---|
| `Attr("aria-current", If(c, "page"))`, generic `If`, constant | 0 |
| same, dynamic string, condition true / false | 1 / 0 |
| same, constant int (`-1`), `bool` | 0 |
| non-generic `If(c bool, v any)`, dynamic string, condition true / false | 1 / **1** |
| lowered `if` (constant folded into `Raw`, or pieces around a dynamic string) | 0 |
| `Opt[T]` passed unlowered to `Attr` | 1 (and prints `{noreferrer true}`: `Attr` can't see inside) |
| `String(Cond(c, a, b))` in content; `aria-selected` pieces with `Cond(c, "true", "false")`; `strconv.FormatBool` pieces | 0 |
| `Attr("title", Cond(…))` (the untyped fallback) | 1 |

- Codegen: one more case in pass 2's site classification (an `*ast.CallExpr` whose callee resolves to the runtime's `If`), no new pass; pass 1 and the dev loop are unchanged. The runtime grows by two small generic functions: they're instantiated in the generated packages that call them (tiny bodies; no type graph reaches `*orbit.Ctx`, §4b).
- (Q3) a: no runtime cost on typed sites; one name switch per `bool` value in `Attr`/`Spread`/`SpreadRest`.

#### Tests (what must exist)
- Runtime: `If`/`Cond` results; `Attr`/`Expr` of an `If` result equal the typed writes; `AllocsPerRun` for the table above.
- Compiler goldens: `If` lowered on an HTML attribute (constant, dynamic string, number, `bool`, URL attribute with a `javascript:` value filtered, `orbit.JSON`), in content, inside a `{for}`; not lowered in a larger expression, in a spread, as a component prop and as a pass-through attribute; a user-defined `If` and a shadowed `orbit` left alone; the typed/fallback byte-identical test with `If` and `Cond` cases; argument evaluation order and a panicking argument behave the same lowered and not.
- (Q3) a: each listed boolean attribute, `hidden`/`popover`/`download` with a `bool` and with a string, `aria-*`/`data-*`/`draggable` with `true`/`false`, a custom element, the same through `Attr`, `Spread` and `SpreadRest`, and the typed/fallback identity.
- `AllocsPerRun` on a page using both helpers: 0 in Orbit's render path (§4b).

#### User-docs changes (with the implementation)
- `reference/template-syntax.md` (attribute values): `orbit.If`, `orbit.Cond`, eager evaluation, `cmp.Or`; (Q3)'s rule in the value table.
- `components.md`: the conditional attributes example; the presence idiom (if (Q3) a).
- `coming-from-astro.md`: `cond ? v : undefined` → `orbit.If(cond, v)`, `c ? a : b` → `orbit.Cond(c, a, b)` or `{if}`, `??` → `cmp.Or`, optional chaining needs a guard; booleans on `aria-*` (per (Q3)).
- `reference/runtime-api.md`: `If`, `Cond`. `performance.md`: what's lowered and what boxes.
- `DECISIONS.md`: the decided-by-rule items above.

#### Out of scope
- A lazy conditional (the template construct of (Q1) d).
- Lowering `If` in spreads, component props and pass-through attributes.
- Astro's `style` object and array forms (`style={{ color: "red" }}`); `orbit.CSS` is §2.7.

### 2.7 Trusted CSS values (`orbit.CSS`)
**Status: Proposed** (2026-10-26, from the `nyth` assessment; (Q) points open).

**Why**: the compiler warns on every `style={expr}` (§2.3, "Security warnings"), because attribute escaping doesn't make a value safe as CSS. `nyth` has 6 `style={…}` expressions, all trusted: `--reader-*` custom properties built by `readerStyle(settings)` from validated reader settings (2 layouts), `background-color:${shown.color}` on a media placeholder, and 3 progress bars (`` `width:${card.track}%` ``). Each would print a warning on every `orbit build`, `orbit dev` regenerate and `orbit check`, with no way to say "this one is fine". `set:html` has `orbit.HTML` and URL attributes have `orbit.URL` for the same purpose. Also, the check is syntactic today (`securityWarning` in `internal/compiler/validate.go`, at parse time): even a constant, `style={"display: none"}`, warns.

#### End-to-end example
```go
// internal/reader/style.go
// Style is the reader settings as CSS custom properties. Every value is a validated number,
// so the declarations are trusted.
func Style(s Settings) orbit.CSS {
	return orbit.CSS("--reader-size:" + strconv.Itoa(s.Size) + "px;--reader-leading:" +
		strconv.FormatFloat(s.Leading, 'f', 2, 64))
}
```
```orbit
---
// src/layouts/ReaderLayout.orbit
import "example.com/nyth/internal/reader"

type Props struct{ Settings reader.Settings }
---
<html lang="en" style={reader.Style(props.Settings)}>
```
renders `<html lang="en" style="--reader-size:18px;--reader-leading:1.60">`, with no warning. `style={"width:" + strconv.Itoa(card.Track) + "%"}` still warns; `style={orbit.CSS(…)}` or a helper returning `orbit.CSS` doesn't.

#### Rules
- **Type**: `type CSS string` in the runtime: "CSS declarations (or a CSS value) from a trusted source. Orbit writes it escaped like any string and never filters it." Exact-type rule as for `orbit.URL` (§3.3): after `types.Unalias`, only `orbit.CSS` itself counts; `type ReaderCSS orbit.CSS` is a new type and warns (decided by rule: the existing exact-type rule).
- **The warning** (`style={expr}` on an HTML element, as today):
  - none when `expr`'s static type is `orbit.CSS`;
  - none when `expr` is a **constant** (go/types gives it a constant value: `"display: none"`, a `const`, `"a" + "b"`), for `style` and for `on*` alike (decided by rule: §2.3's `set:html` precedent, where untyped string constants are accepted "since the author wrote them");
  - otherwise the same message as today.
  - **Where it's computed**: the parser keeps recording the candidate sites; the warning is emitted by the typed pass (§3.3), which knows the type. A unit that isn't type-checked (type errors, unreadable export data) warns for every candidate, as today (decided by rule: simplest, never silent). Editor: (Q2).
- **Pass-through attributes**: `style={x}` and `on*={x}` collected into a component's `orbit.RestAttrs` (§2.5) get the same warnings, at the call site's attribute (decided by rule: they're written as HTML attributes; today a component tag never warns, so `<Button style={s}>` is silent, a gap). A declared `Style orbit.CSS` prop carries the trust into the component, where `style={props.Style}` doesn't warn.
- **Writing it**: like a `string` everywhere, so no new escaping code: attribute-escaped (`&` and `"`; the browser decodes them before the CSS parser runs, so `content:"x"` survives), HTML-escaped in content, and **still scheme-filtered in a URL attribute** (CSS isn't a trusted URL; only `orbit.URL` skips the filter). Typed write: `String(string(x))` as pieces (§3.3's table gains the `orbit.CSS` row next to `orbit.URL`); `Expr` and `Attr` get a `case CSS` next to `URL`, so the untyped fallback doesn't reach `fmt.Sprint`; `RestAttrs` classifies it as `RestString`.
- **Valid on any attribute** and in content; beyond `style`'s warning it means nothing (decided by rule: simplest; no "CSS-only" attribute list to maintain). `<style>` content is opaque (§2.3), so there's no CSS interpolation context to apply it to.
- **No sanitizer** (decided by rule: Go stdlib): `orbit.CSS` is a trusted-content marker, like `html/template`'s `template.CSS` ("encapsulates known safe content that matches any of: 1. The CSS3 stylesheet production… 2. The CSS3 rule production… 3. The CSS3 declaration productions… 4. The CSS3 value production…"; "use of this type presents a security risk"), which `html/template` writes without filtering. `html/template` also filters *untrusted* strings in a CSS context (`cssValueFilter`, which replaces a value with `ZgotmplZ`), but that filter is written for a single value: it rejects `;`, `(`, `)`, `/`, quotes and `--`, so 2 of `nyth`'s 6 values (both layouts' custom-property declarations) would be blanked, and so would any `rgb(…)`, `url(…)` or multi-declaration string. Orbit keeps its build-time warning for untrusted strings and adds the marker; filtering at runtime would change today's output for every `style={string}` (Q1 b).

#### (Q) points
- **(Q1) Shape.**
  - (a) **Recommended**: the `orbit.CSS` marker type above, typed-pass warnings, constants exempt. Mirrors `orbit.HTML`/`orbit.URL` and `template.CSS`; no runtime cost.
  - (b) Also filter plain-string `style` values at runtime like `html/template`'s `cssValueFilter`, with `orbit.CSS` bypassing it (exactly the stdlib model, as Orbit does for URL attributes). Safer for values that really are untrusted, but it blanks most real declaration strings (above), changes the output of existing sites, and costs a scan per value.
  - (c) A per-site opt-out instead of a type (a `{/* orbit:trusted */}` comment, or `style:trusted={…}`): no new type, but the trust doesn't travel with the value (a helper can't vouch for what it returns) and it's new syntax.
- **(Q2) The warning in the editor.** Today the LSP shows it from the tolerant parse. With the warning in the typed pass:
  - (a) **Recommended**: the LSP shows it from its background typed check (the strict copy it already type-checks at startup and after `orbit_gen/` changes for Markdown diagnostics, §9, `internal/lsp/mdtyped.go`), tied to the attribute's source text so it follows edits and disappears when the attribute is edited, until the next check. Same answer as the CLI, a little later.
  - (b) The editor shows no `style`/`on*` warnings; they're CLI-only. Simplest, but the editor loses a security hint it has today.
  - (c) The editor keeps today's syntactic warning (every non-literal expression): false positives on `orbit.CSS` values, so the editor and the CLI disagree.

#### Cost
- Runtime: one type and one `case` in `Expr`/`Attr`; no render cost (a `CSS` value writes like a `string`).
- Typed pass: one type comparison and one constant check per `style`/`on*` site. No pass-1 or dev-loop change measurable.

#### Tests (what must exist)
- Compiler warnings golden: `style={x}` with `x` a `string` (warns), an `orbit.CSS` (no), a function returning `orbit.CSS` (no), `type S orbit.CSS` (warns), a string literal, a `const` and a constant concatenation (no), `on*={"…"}` (no), an unchecked unit (warns as today), a pass-through `style={x}` on a component with an `orbit.RestAttrs` field (warns) and a `Style orbit.CSS` prop (no).
- Output: `orbit.CSS` with `"` and `&` escaped; in `href` still filtered; content; `Expr`/`Attr`/typed identity; `RestAttrs`.
- LSP (per (Q2)): the warning appears after the typed check and goes away when the value becomes `orbit.CSS`.

#### User-docs changes (with the implementation)
- `components.md` "Event handler and style attributes": `orbit.CSS`, constants, what "trusted" means (no untrusted input without validating it), the pass-through case.
- `reference/runtime-api.md`: `CSS`; `reference/template-syntax.md`: the value table row and the warning rules; `coming-from-astro.md`: Astro doesn't warn, Orbit does, and `orbit.CSS` is the answer.
- `DECISIONS.md`: the decided-by-rule items above.

#### Out of scope
- A trusted JavaScript type for `on*={…}` (`html/template` has `template.JS`); scripts and `orbit.JSON` stay the recommended way to pass data.
- A CSS sanitizer or builder API; Astro's `style` object form.

## 3. Compilation

### 3.1 Generated package layout
Each `.orbit` file becomes **two Go packages**, in two separate trees under `orbit_gen/`:

| Package | Path | Contents | Imports |
|---|---|---|---|
| declaration | `orbit_gen/decl/components/card` (package `card`) | `Props` and its transitive dependencies: methods on them, and the hoisted types, consts and funcs they reference. Plus the hook `var Orbit__Render func(*orbit__rt.Ctx, *orbit__rt.Writer, Props, orbit__rt.Slots) error` and the exported wrapper `func Render(orbit__ctx, orbit__w, orbit__props, orbit__slots) error`, which panics with `orbit: src/components/Card.orbit not linked; import example.com/blog/orbit_gen/app` if the hook is nil (e.g. in a user test that skipped the app). For a **page**, also the generated `type Params` (§2.2); the page's hook takes it as a fifth argument (`func(…, orbit__rt.Slots, Params) error`), while its `Render` wrapper keeps the uniform signature and builds `Params` from `orbit__ctx.Param` calls (§3.2). | User Go packages used by `Props`, plus the runtime as `orbit__rt` (and as `orbit` only if `Props` code uses it) |
| implementation | `orbit_gen/impl/components/card` (package `card_impl`) | Everything else that's hoisted: lowercase helpers, other types. Plus `func orbit__render(...)` (the frontmatter body and template) and the link `var _ = func() int { Orbit__Render = orbit__render; return 0 }()`. | Its own declaration package via dot-import (`import . ".../decl/components/card"`), so `Props` and `Render` resolve unqualified. Other components' **declaration** packages only. The runtime as `orbit__rt` (and as `orbit` only if user code uses it). |

How this works:
- **Calls**: a call site `<Card …>` compiles to `Card.Render(orbit__ctx, orbit__w, Card.Props{…}, orbit__rt.Slots{…})`, where `Card` is the alias for the declaration package. That's `orbit_gen/` (the hook flavor, what `orbit dev`, `go test` and the LSP see); `orbit build` overlays a build flavor that calls the callee's implementation directly where the import graph allows it (§3.5).
- **No cycles**: implementation packages import only declaration packages, so Go never sees an import cycle, even with mutual recursion. The build flavor adds impl → impl imports only between strongly connected components of the call graph, which can't form a cycle (§3.5).
- **Self**: `<Self>` compiles to `Render(…)`, the dot-imported wrapper (a direct self-call `Orbit__RenderImpl(…)` in the build flavor, §3.5).
- **Package names**: the implementation package is named `<name>_impl` (`card_impl`), distinct from the declaration package `card`. Go then prints the declaration type as `card.Props` instead of `"example.com/blog/orbit_gen/decl/components/card".Props`; the CLI rewrites it further to the user's alias (§3.3, error output).
- **Linking**: each implementation package sets its hook in a package-level var initializer, not in `init()`: package variables are initialized before any `init` function runs, so the hook is set as early as possible and never depends on the order of `init` functions. `orbit_gen/app` imports `orbit_gen/link` (as `_`), which imports every implementation package (as `_`; the route table calls each page through its declaration package's `Render`), so every hook is set before `main` runs. Rendering *another* component during package initialization is unsupported (sibling packages initialize in import-path order), and a frontmatter `func init` is rejected (§2.2).
- **Unexported names in decl**: a name that only `Props` (and other declaration-package code) uses may stay unexported. A dot-import only brings exported names, so implementation code can't see an unexported name that `Props` pulled into the declaration package. Codegen reports this clearly instead of leaving Go's "undefined": `src/components/Card.orbit:5:3: item is used by Props, so it lives in the component's declaration package and must be exported (rename it to Item)`.
- **Clashes with decl exports**: an `.orbit` import alias, or a name hoisted into the implementation package, that equals an exported name of the component's own declaration package is an Orbit error at that name (`Item "./Item.orbit"` or an impl-side `type Item` when `Props` pulled an `Item` into decl), instead of Go's `Item redeclared` / `Item already declared through dot-import` at a generated position: `Item clashes with Item in the component's declaration package (Props uses it); rename one`. The same applies to the generated `Props`, `Render` and a page's `Params`: `Props clashes with the generated Props of the component's declaration package; rename it` (both decided by rule: simplest). Shadowing a decl export inside the body (`Item := 42`) is legal Go and allowed.
- **Verbatim user code**: the user's Go code is copied without renaming, so source maps stay trivial. Each frontmatter import, and the implicit `orbit` import, is placed in whichever package(s) use it. Usage is decided with object resolution: an `ast.SelectorExpr` whose `X` is an `*ast.Ident` with `Obj == nil` (not a local that shadows the import name) and the import's name. The scan covers the hoisted part, the body, and every template expression.
- **Cost**: one indirect function call per component render, which is negligible in time, but escape analysis can't see through it, so slot content that captures parent variables is heap-allocated. `orbit build` removes that with direct calls (§3.5); dev keeps the hooks so an edit recompiles only the edited component.

Path sanitizing (the same relative path in both trees; `src/` is dropped):
- `src/components/Card.orbit` → `orbit_gen/decl/components/card` (package `card`) and `orbit_gen/impl/components/card` (package `card_impl`)
- Each segment is lowercased; `-`, `.` and any other non-identifier character become `_`.
- `src/pages/blog/[slug].orbit` → `pages/blog/slug_param`
- `[...rest]` → `rest_rest`
- A segment that is a Go keyword (`default`, `type`, `func`, …), `main`, `init`, or starts with a digit gets a `p_` prefix: `404` → `p_404`, `default.orbit` → `p_default`.
- Sanitized paths that collide (e.g. `my-card.orbit` and `my_card.orbit`) are an error naming both files.

Extra generated files:
- `orbit_gen/manifest/manifest.go`: per-page asset URLs (prod; dev reads `manifest.json`, §5).
- `orbit_gen/app/app.go`: the route table (with an adapter from each page's typed `Render` to `Route`, §4), the blank import of `orbit_gen/link`, and `func New(opts ...orbit.Option) *orbit.Site`.
- `orbit_gen/link/link.go`: a blank import of every implementation package. It's a leaf package so that a template or frontmatter edit recompiles only that impl package and `link` (8–12 ms at 200 files), while `app`, which imports every page's decl package (21–38 ms to compile), and the server entry stay build-cache hits: Go stops recompiling one level above a package whose compiled output is unchanged. Sharding `link` (16 shards by path hash: a 3 ms recompile) was measured and dropped: within noise of the whole `go build`.
- `orbit_gen/cmd/server/main.go`: the default server entry, generated only if the user has no `cmd/server`.

Each generated file starts with `// Code generated by orbit. DO NOT EDIT.`. Stale dirs are removed only if they carry that marker.

### 3.2 Worked example
`src/components/Card.orbit`:
```orbit
---
import "example.com/blog/internal/posts"
type Props struct {
    Post     posts.Post
    Featured bool
}
func href(p posts.Post) string { return "/blog/" + p.Slug }
url := href(props.Post)
---
<article class="card" data-featured={props.Featured}>
  <a href={url}>{props.Post.Title}</a>
  <slot />
</article>
<style>.card { border: 1px solid #ccc }</style>
```
→ `orbit_gen/decl/components/card/decl.go` (declaration package: `Props` and `posts`; not `href`, which `Props` doesn't use; no `orbit` import, because `Props` doesn't use it):
```go
// Code generated by orbit. DO NOT EDIT.

package card

import (
	orbit__rt "github.com/orbit-z/orbit"
//line ../../../../src/components/Card.orbit:2:8
"example.com/blog/internal/posts"
//line decl.go:10:1
)

//line ../../../../src/components/Card.orbit:3:1
type Props struct {
    Post     posts.Post
    Featured bool
}
//line decl.go:18:1

var Orbit__Render func(*orbit__rt.Ctx, *orbit__rt.Writer, Props, orbit__rt.Slots) error

func Render(orbit__ctx *orbit__rt.Ctx, orbit__w *orbit__rt.Writer, orbit__props Props, orbit__slots orbit__rt.Slots) error {
	if Orbit__Render == nil {
		panic("orbit: src/components/Card.orbit not linked; import example.com/blog/orbit_gen/app")
	}
	return Orbit__Render(orbit__ctx, orbit__w, orbit__props, orbit__slots)
}
```
→ `orbit_gen/impl/components/card/render.go` (implementation package `card_impl`):
```go
// Code generated by orbit. DO NOT EDIT.

package card_impl

import (
	orbit__rt "github.com/orbit-z/orbit"
	. "example.com/blog/orbit_gen/decl/components/card"
//line ../../../../src/components/Card.orbit:2:8
"example.com/blog/internal/posts"
//line render.go:12:1
)

//line ../../../../src/components/Card.orbit:7:1
func href(p posts.Post) string { return "/blog/" + p.Slug }
//line render.go:17:1

var _ = func() int { Orbit__Render = orbit__render; return 0 }()

func orbit__render(orbit__ctx *orbit__rt.Ctx, orbit__w *orbit__rt.Writer, orbit__props Props, orbit__slots orbit__rt.Slots) error {
	if orbit__err := orbit__w.Enter("src/components/Card.orbit"); orbit__err != nil {
		return orbit__err
	}
	defer orbit__w.Leave()
	ctx, props, slots := orbit__ctx, orbit__props, orbit__slots
	_, _, _ = ctx, props, slots
/*line ../../../../src/components/Card.orbit:8:1*/url := href(props.Post)/*line render.go:28:98*/
	_ = url
	orbit__w.Raw("<article class=\"card\" data-o-3fa2c1d0")
	orbit__w.AttrBool("data-featured",
/*line ../../../../src/components/Card.orbit:10:38*/props.Featured/*line render.go:32:91*/,
	)
	orbit__w.Raw(">\n  <a data-o-3fa2c1d0 href=\"")
	orbit__w.URL(
/*line ../../../../src/components/Card.orbit:11:12*/url/*line render.go:36:80*/,
	)
	orbit__w.Raw("\">")
	orbit__w.String(
/*line ../../../../src/components/Card.orbit:11:18*/props.Post.Title/*line render.go:40:93*/,
	)
	orbit__w.Raw("</a>\n  ")
	if orbit__s := orbit__slots.Default; orbit__s != nil {
		if orbit__err := orbit__s(orbit__w); orbit__err != nil {
			return orbit__err
		}
	}
	orbit__w.Raw("\n</article>\n")
	return orbit__w.Err()
}
```
The typed writes (`AttrBool`, the `URL` pieces, `String`) come from go/types (§3.3). Card never uses `locals`, so its `render` doesn't declare it and doesn't import `src/locals`; a component that uses it gets `locals := orbit__rt.LocalsOf[orbit__locals.Locals](orbit__ctx)` after the bindings (§3.3, Locals). In tolerant/LSP mode the same spans are emitted as `orbit__w.Attr(…)` and `orbit__w.Expr(…)`.

A parent call to `<Card post={p} featured><em>hi</em></Card>` at `src/pages/index.orbit:20:3` generates the code below (`…` stands for the generated file's own line:col). Unknown attributes were already rejected by Orbit's pre-check (§2.3). The field keys still carry their own directives, so the Go errors that remain (e.g. `unknown field` when the pre-check had to be skipped) land on the attribute name. Glue passes `orbit__ctx`, never the shadowable `ctx`:
```go
if orbit__err := /*line ../../../../src/pages/index.orbit:20:4*/Card.Render/*line render.go:…*/(orbit__ctx, orbit__w, Card.Props{
	/*line ../../../../src/pages/index.orbit:20:9*/Post/*line render.go:…*/: /*line ../../../../src/pages/index.orbit:20:15*/p/*line render.go:…*/,
	/*line ../../../../src/pages/index.orbit:20:18*/Featured/*line render.go:…*/: true,
}, orbit__rt.Slots{
	Default: func(orbit__w *orbit__rt.Writer) error { orbit__w.Raw("<em>hi</em>"); return orbit__w.Err() },
}); orbit__err != nil {
	return orbit__err
}
```
Slot content is compiled in the parent, so it naturally gets the parent's scope attribute.

A component call with no children passes the zero `orbit__rt.Slots{}`: no map, no closure, no allocation (§4b). Named slots (`<p slot="footer">`) compile to `Named: []orbit__rt.NamedSlot{{Name: "footer", Render: …}}`. An outlet `<slot name="footer" />` compiles to `orbit__slots.Get("footer")`, a linear scan over usually 0–3 entries.

**A page with route params.** For `src/pages/blog/[slug].orbit` (declaration package `slug_param`, §3.1), the declaration package also gets the generated `Params`, and the page's hook and `Render` wrapper carry it:
```go
type Params struct {
	Slug string
}

var Orbit__Render func(*orbit__rt.Ctx, *orbit__rt.Writer, Props, orbit__rt.Slots, Params) error

func Render(orbit__ctx *orbit__rt.Ctx, orbit__w *orbit__rt.Writer, orbit__props Props, orbit__slots orbit__rt.Slots) error {
	if Orbit__Render == nil {
		panic("orbit: src/pages/blog/[slug].orbit not linked; import example.com/blog/orbit_gen/app")
	}
	return Orbit__Render(orbit__ctx, orbit__w, orbit__props, orbit__slots, Params{
		Slug: orbit__ctx.Param("slug"),
	})
}
```
The implementation's `orbit__render` takes a fifth parameter, `orbit__params Params`, and binds it with the other user names:
```go
	ctx, props, slots, params := orbit__ctx, orbit__props, orbit__slots, orbit__params
	_, _, _, _ = ctx, props, slots, params
```
The route table's entry is unchanged: `Render: func(c *orbit.Ctx, w *orbit.Writer) error { return slug_param.Render(c, w, slug_param.Props{}, orbit.Slots{}) }`. A `[...path]` page gets `Path: orbit__ctx.Param("path")`; a page without params gets `type Params struct{}` and passes `Params{}`. Building `Params` allocates nothing: each `Param` call returns a string the router already stored on the pooled `Ctx` (§4).

### 3.3 Codegen rules
- **No `gofmt`**: formatting would shift offsets and break source maps. Codegen emits readable code by construction.
- **Line directives** (verified in the spike, milestone 0):
  - **File names are relative** to the generated file's directory (`../../../../src/components/Card.orbit`), with `/` separators on every OS. A relative name is resolved against the directory of the file that contains the directive: `go/scanner` (so `go vet` and gopls's parser) always does this, and `cmd/compile` does from Go 1.27 (it joins `filepath.Dir(file)` with the name; cmd/go passes files relative to its cwd and prints the result relative to the cwd, so `go build` and `go vet` print the same `./src/…orbit:L:C` from the module root, `../src/…` from `src/`, and so on). Go 1.23.4 and 1.24.0 print the relative name unresolved, which is why `go 1.27` is a hard minimum (Context). Relative names keep `orbit_gen/` reproducible and committable and let checkouts share the build cache; absolute names would only have helped on Go ≤1.24.
  - **Form**: always `:L:C`. Columns are 1-based bytes (a tab counts as 1).
  - **Inline spans** (frontmatter body statements, `{expr}`, attribute expressions, control headers, field keys, tag names): an inline `/*line <file>:<line>:<col>*/` right before the span, and right after it a reset `/*line <genfile>:<line>:<col>*/` naming the generated file's bare basename (`render.go`) and the true position of the next character, so generated glue (`)`, `{`, `,`, conversions) never inherits an `.orbit` position. Codegen tracks its own output line and column to emit these.
  - **Hoisted declarations** (imports, types, consts, funcs): framed by **own-line** `//line <file>:<line>:<col>` before and `//line <genfile>:<line>:1` after, with the declaration starting at the beginning of the next line. Inline `/*line*/` comments there become part of the declaration's doc or line comment, so gopls hover shows `line decl.go:13:24`; `go/ast` strips own-line `//line` directives from docs. Multi-line declarations keep correct positions on their continuation lines, because user code is copied verbatim.
  - A page's generated `Params` type has no `.orbit` source (its fields come from the file path), so it gets no directive. It's always valid Go; the errors about it (a collision, a bad param name) are Orbit's own, at the file (§2.2), and a misspelled `params.X` is reported at the use, which is user code.
  - Generated field keys (`Post` for attribute `post`) get a directive pointing at the attribute name. A component call gets one before `Card.Render`, pointing at the tag name.
  - **`-trimpath` doesn't trim directive file names**: stack traces of a `-trimpath` binary show absolute `.orbit` paths (`/abs/…/src/components/Card.orbit:25`), while generated-file frames are trimmed. Document this (`reference/cli.md`).
  - Test (milestone 4): a golden project with deliberate errors (frontmatter, hoisted helper, a continuation line of a multi-line declaration, `{expr}`, control header, unknown prop with the pre-check skipped) asserts that `go build` and `go vet` report the same `src/…orbit:L:C`, and that the CLI's cleaned-up output matches a golden file. gopls ignores line directives (§9), so the LSP integration test checks Orbit's *mapped* diagnostics at the same positions (milestone 12).
- **Error output** (`orbit build`, `orbit dev`'s overlay, `orbit check`): the CLI post-processes `go build`/`go vet` output, which already points at `.orbit` lines.
  - It strips the `[<gen>:L:C]` part of related positions: Go prints "other declaration" lines as `./src/components/A.orbit:7:6[/abs/…/decl.go:10:53]`.
  - It rewrites qualified generated names to the user's names: `card.Props`, or a fully quoted `"example.com/blog/orbit_gen/decl/components/card".Props`, becomes `Card.Props`, using the alias the reporting `.orbit` file gives that component (plain `Props`, and a page's `Params`, in the component's own file). Together with the `_impl` package names (§3.1), messages read in the user's terms.
- **Typed writes**: codegen type-checks template expressions with `go/types` and emits a typed write for each, avoiding `any` boxing and the runtime type switch: `w.String(s)`, `w.Int(int64(n))`, `w.Uint`, `w.Float`, `w.Float32`, `w.Bool`, `w.HTML(h)`, `w.Bytes(b)`, `w.Error(e)`, `w.Stringer(s)`, `w.Render(r)`, and for attributes `w.AttrBool`, with URL attribute values written as pieces through `w.URL(s)` (escaped and scheme-filtered, §4; user decision, milestone 9; `w.AttrURL` stays for other callers). A string or number attribute value (`data-id={n}`, `title={s}`, not a URL attribute) is written **as pieces** (milestone 9): its ` name="` and closing `"` are merged into the neighbouring `Raw` constants and the value is a plain `w.String`/`w.Int`/`w.Uint`/`w.Float`/`w.Float32`, byte-identical to `Attr`, with no boxing (`Attr(name, int)` allocated for values ≥ 256). `uintptr` and named numbers with `Error`/`String`/`Format` methods stay on `Attr`. An `orbit.JSONValue` attribute value is `WriteAttrJSON` (§5c): pieces ` name='` and `'` around `w.JSONAttr("<path>:L:C", <expr>)` (the value's `{`), byte-identical to `Attr`. Pass 2 also produces the components' **client-module types** as a by-product (§5c): per unit, the `data-*`/`set:json` keys (`Output.JSONSites`, found by pass 1, typed by pass 2's `types.Info`) and the named Go types they reach, mapped to TS (`internal/compiler/tstypes*.go`), and the generate-time errors for `orbit.JSON` arguments and `set:json` values encoding/json can never encode; `project.GenFiles` emits them as `orbit_gen/types/*.d.ts`. Typed writes are on in `orbit build`, `orbit generate` and `orbit dev`.
  - The choice mirrors `Expr`'s runtime behavior exactly (§4), so typed and fallback output are byte-identical; a golden test renders the same templates both ways and compares (the spike's 55 expression kinds are its seed).
  - **Exact-type rule**: `Expr`'s type switch matches *exact dynamic types*, so `type Slug string` does **not** hit `case string`; it falls through to `fmt.Sprint`. Call `types.Unalias` first, then map the static type:

    | Static type | Typed write |
    |---|---|
    | untyped `nil` | nothing |
    | constants | by their default type; may be folded into the `Raw` constant |
    | `orbit.HTML` | `HTML` |
    | `string` (or an alias of it) | `String` |
    | `orbit.URL` | `String(string(x))` |
    | `[]byte` | `Bytes` |
    | ints, untyped rune | `Int(int64(x))` |
    | uints, `byte` | `Uint(uint64(x))` |
    | `float64` | `Float` |
    | `float32` | `Float32` (formatted with bitSize 32: `0.1`, not `0.10000000149011612`) |
    | `bool` | `Bool` |
    | `orbit.Renderer`, unnamed `func(*Writer) error` | `Render` |
    | a static interface that implements `error`, or a concrete type implementing `error` | `Error` |
    | a concrete `fmt.Stringer` (value or pointer receiver, `time.Time`, `type Status int` with `String`) | `Stringer` |
    | a named type with no such methods, underlying `string`/int/uint/`bool`/`float64`/`float32` | the matching typed write, with a conversion (same bytes as `fmt.Sprint`) |
    | static `fmt.Stringer`, `any` and other interfaces; named `[]byte` or func types; `T` with `String` only on `*T`; `*string`; slices; `uintptr`; complex | **`Expr`** (the generic path) |

  - **Engine** (no `golang.org/x/tools/go/packages`, which compiles every impl package twice: once for its export data, again in `go build`):
    1. Pass 1 generates all packages with `Expr`/`Attr` fallbacks, **in memory**, recording each expression's offset.
    2. One `go list -export -e` (with the build's `-tags`, `-mod` and `GOFLAGS`) over the non-`orbit_gen` imports: user packages, the runtime and std. Their export data is read with `importer.ForCompiler(fset, "gc", lookup)`. These are compiles `go build` reuses from the cache right after. It's **started in the background** (`Checker.Preload`) as soon as the `.orbit` files are parsed, over the packages parsing predicts (the runtime, `src/locals`, every frontmatter Go import, `time` when there are Markdown files), so it runs during pass 1 and esbuild; the check uses it when it covers every import of the pass-1 output, and otherwise loads again (a wrong guess costs time, never correctness). It's the critical path of `orbit generate` (about 27 ms of 42 at 200 files).
    3. Decl and impl packages are type-checked **from the pass-1 source**, in parallel, behind a memoized importer.
    4. Pass 2 finds each expression by its recorded offset, picks the write from `types.Info.Types`, and **re-emits** the file (rather than patching it), so directive columns stay correct. Then the files are written.
  - **Fallback** to `w.Expr(any)`/`w.Attr`: in tolerant/LSP mode (no type check at all), for a package with type errors (the later `go build` reports them), and, with a warning, when export data can't be read.
  - **Dev**: the checker stays **resident in the dev process**, hot and incremental. A body edit re-checks one impl package; a `Props` edit re-checks that decl package and its importers; a user `.go`, `go.mod` or `orbit.json` edit reruns `go list -export` and re-checks everything. That `go list` starts when the edit is seen (`Incremental.Invalidate` preloads the last load's packages), so it overlaps the front and esbuild.
  - **No persistent type cache**: a full check is at most about 70 ms, mostly `go list`.
  - **Cost** (spike C, Ryzen 7 9700X, 16 threads unless noted; s200 = 200 components, about 5.8k expressions; ±15% noise). The type check is never the bottleneck; `go build` and the link are.
    - Full check, warm: 46 ms at s200 (go/packages: 110 ms).
    - Hot re-check of one edited component: about 0.3 ms (0.24 ms at 16 threads, 0.29 ms on 4 cores).
    - `orbit build` with std cached, s200: 705 ms typed vs. 752 ms all-`Expr` (on 4 cores: 1200 vs. 1308 ms), i.e. within noise. With an empty GOCACHE, about 3.7 s either way.
    - Dev body edit with the resident checker: the all-`Expr` total (236 ms, including the rebuild) plus about 0.3 ms.
    - User `.go` edit that every `Props` depends on: the check step is 69 ms (go/packages: 647 ms), out of a 0.9 s total dominated by `go build` (§7).
    - Typed code and `Expr` code compile at the same speed.
- **Static HTML**: adjacent static text, static attributes and scope attributes are merged at compile time into one `Raw` string constant per run, with static attribute values pre-escaped (§4b).
- **Whitespace** (`compressHTML`, §8, user decision): applied to template text at codegen time, so the `Raw` constants already hold the compressed HTML. `"jsx"` (default, Astro v7's; user decision 2026-10-26) applies Babel's JSX line rule per text; `"html"` collapses each whitespace run to one space or newline and drops whitespace-only text whose neighbors are all block boundaries (looking through `{if}`/`{for}`; components, slots and HTML comments count as inline); `false` is verbatim. `<pre>`, `<textarea>`, `<script>`, `<style>`, HTML comments, attribute values and `{expr}` output are never altered. The exact rules, and the neighbor scan, are in `internal/compiler/DESIGN.md` (settled gap 2) and `internal/compiler/codegen_ws.go`; the user-facing ones in `docs/reference/template-syntax.md#whitespace`.
- **`</head>` marker**: where a template's static HTML contains `</head>` (usually in a layout), codegen splits the `Raw` there and emits `orbit__w.HeadEnd()`, which records the buffer offset for asset injection (§4).
- **Locals** (user decision, milestone 9): `render` declares `locals` **only when the component's code references it** (the same object-resolution scan as imports, §3.1), so a request whose pages and components never read `locals` does no lookup and no allocation. The type is `src/locals.Locals`, or `orbit__rt.NoLocals` without one. (Before milestone 9 it was declared in every `render`.)
- **Source map**: each copied span is recorded as `{kind, orbitStart, orbitEnd, goStart, goEnd}`.
  - `kind=exact` means a 1:1 byte copy: frontmatter statements, expressions, control headers, the import alias.
  - `kind=span` means the whole range maps to the whole range, e.g. attribute name `is-open` → field key `IsOpen`, or a component tag → its `Render` call.
  - `kind=echo` marks the generated `_ = name` lines (§2.2). They map to no `.orbit` range; an LSP rename drops gopls's edits there (§9).
- **Tolerant mode** (for the LSP): the parser recovers. Unclosed tags close at EOF, an unterminated `{` ends at end of line, and errors are collected rather than aborting. No type-checking; every expression uses the `Expr` fallback. Each expression sits on its own line inside `w.Expr(...)`, so broken input stays local and gopls's error-tolerant parser can still complete `props.Po|`.

### 3.4 `server:defer` and `client:*` call sites
**Component IDs**: `<Name>-<hash8>`, the file's base name plus the 8 hex chars of its scope hash (§5): `src/components/Comments.orbit` → `Comments-9b1e44a2`. They're the same on every OS and in every build, and are used in island URLs and lazy markers (decided by rule: Astro names island endpoints after the component; the hash keeps two `Comments.orbit` files apart).

**`server:defer`**: the call site doesn't call `Render`. For
```orbit
<Comments server:defer postID={post.ID}><p slot="fallback">Loading comments…</p></Comments>
```
codegen emits:
```go
if orbit__err := orbit__rt.Defer(orbit__ctx, orbit__w, "Comments-9b1e44a2", /*line ../../../../src/pages/blog/[slug].orbit:14:4*/Comments.Props/*line render.go:…*/{
	/*line …*/PostID/*line render.go:…*/: /*line …*/post.ID/*line render.go:…*/,
}, func(orbit__w *orbit__rt.Writer) error { orbit__w.Raw("<p data-o-5c7d1e2f>Loading comments…</p>"); return orbit__w.Err() }); orbit__err != nil {
	return orbit__err
}
```
and `Defer` (§4) writes:
```html
<orbit-island src="/_orbit/island/3f9a2c1d7e4b5a60/Comments-9b1e44a2" props="<token>"><p data-o-5c7d1e2f>Loading comments…</p></orbit-island>
```
- The props literal is type-checked by Go and pre-checked for unknown props (§2.3), as in a normal call. The fallback is compiled in the parent, like slot content, so it carries the parent's scope attribute. With no fallback, `nil` is passed.
- **Sealing** (`internal/islandcrypto`, about 30 lines, stdlib only; a deterministic SIV construction):
  - **Keys**: two 32-byte keys derived from the master island key (§4) with `crypto/hkdf` over SHA-256, using the info strings `"orbit island mac"` and `"orbit island enc"`. They're derived once per `Site`, and the AES-GCM cipher is created once too.
  - **Nonce**: `HMAC-SHA256(macKey, lenprefix(componentID) ‖ lenprefix(buildID) ‖ propsJSON)[:12]`, where `propsJSON` is `Defer`'s `encoding/json` output.
  - **Token**: `nonce ‖ AES-256-GCM(encKey).Seal(nonce, propsJSON, ad)`, as base64url with no padding. The associated data `ad` is `lenprefix(componentID) ‖ lenprefix(buildID)`. Both values come from the island URL's path (§5b), so a token can't be moved to another component or to another build's path. Length prefixes keep the concatenation unambiguous.
  - **Opening**: verify and decrypt with the GCM tag, then recompute the HMAC nonce from the plaintext and compare it with the token's nonce using `subtle.ConstantTimeCompare`. A mismatch is rejected.
  - **Deterministic**: the same props, component and build always give the same token, so the same island URL across renders and instances. That makes island URLs cache-ready (HTTP caching of islands comes after the MVP, §12). A nonce repeats only for identical inputs, which give identical ciphertexts, so there's no nonce-reuse hazard; an observer learns only whether two tokens hold equal props, which a cacheable URL reveals anyway.
  - All primitives are FIPS-approved (HKDF, HMAC-SHA256, AES-GCM).
  - **Measured**: about 0.42 µs to seal 128 B of props, 0.94 µs for 1 KB and 4.9 µs for 8 KB (a vendored pure-Go AES-GCM-SIV, the alternative considered, took 0.75 / 3.1 / 21.7 µs).
  - **Confidential and tamper-proof, but not bound to a user or a time**: anyone who has a token can replay it. Authorization therefore belongs in the component (cookies, `locals`), never in props. Document this in `lazy-loading.md`.
- **JSON-serializable props, checked at generate time** in the go/types pass (§3.3, pass 2). Orbit walks the target's whole `Props` type (every field is encoded, whether the call site sets it or not) using `encoding/json`'s rules, and reports an error at the `server:defer` attribute, naming the field path:
  - funcs (so also `orbit.Renderer` and `orbit.Slots`), channels, `unsafe.Pointer`, complex numbers;
  - interface types, including `any` and `error`: a decoded value can't be verified to have the same dynamic type;
  - map keys other than strings, integers or `encoding.TextMarshaler`s.

  Types that implement `json.Marshaler` together with `json.Unmarshaler` (on the pointer), or the `encoding.TextMarshaler`/`TextUnmarshaler` pair, are accepted as they are (e.g. `time.Time`). Fields tagged `json:"-"` and unexported fields of nested structs are skipped, as `encoding/json` skips them (decided by rule: Go stdlib convention). Example: `src/pages/blog/[slug].orbit:14:13: <Comments server:defer>: Props.OnLoad has type func(), which can't be sent to a deferred component; props must be JSON-serializable`.
  - The walk covers only the `Props` types of deferred call sites, so its cost is expected to be well under 1 ms; it's measured with the rest of pass 2.
  - Where the type check doesn't run (tolerant/LSP mode, a package with type errors, unreadable export data), `Defer`'s `json.Marshal` error becomes a render error at the `.orbit` position.
- **The island table**: `orbit_gen/app` registers every component that appears with `server:defer` anywhere in the project, and only those, so no other component can be rendered by URL:
  ```go
  orbit.NewIsland("Comments-9b1e44a2", "src/components/Comments.orbit", comments.Render)
  ```
  `NewIsland` (§4) is generic over `Props`: it decodes the opened JSON into a `Props` with `json.Unmarshal` and calls `Render` with no slots. Unknown JSON fields are ignored and missing ones stay zero (`encoding/json` defaults), so a page rendered by the previous deploy still loads its islands when `Props` gains a field.

**`client:*`**: the call site renders the component normally, wrapped in a marker element. The marker is folded into the surrounding `Raw` constants, so it costs only its bytes at render time:
```html
<orbit-lazy component="Counter-1c2d3e4f" client="visible" opts="200px">…component output…</orbit-lazy>
```
- It's a wrapper element, like Astro's `<astro-island>` (decided by rule: Astro behavior. Observers need an element, and a component's output can have zero or several root elements). Head injection adds `orbit-island,orbit-lazy{display:contents}` once (§5), so the wrapper doesn't affect layout, but it is in the DOM: a child selector such as `.list > .item` doesn't match across it. Document this.
- `client:visible` observes the wrapper's element children, as Astro does, because a `display:contents` element has no box of its own.
- The wrapper is always emitted for a lazy directive, even on a page where the component's scripts end up eager (eager wins, §5). The loader ignores IDs that aren't in the page's lazy map.

### 3.5 Direct calls in production builds
**Status: Implemented** (2026-10-26; `internal/compiler` `Input.Direct`, `internal/project/direct.go`, `internal/build`; deviations under "Implementation" below; accepted 2026-10-26, user decision: `orbit build` calls components directly where the import graph allows it, `orbit dev` keeps the hooks). The user chose the recommended answer to every (Q) point: (Q1) the build flavor reaches `go build` through `-overlay` from `.orbit/build/`, `orbit_gen/` unchanged; (Q2) `<Self>` is a direct self-call; (Q3) tests and benchmarks measure it with `go test -overlay=.orbit/build/overlay.json`; (Q4) no depth cap.

**Why**: every component call goes through the callee's declaration-package hook (§3.1). Go's escape analysis can't see through an indirect call, so slot content that captures parent variables (`<Card>{p.Title}</Card>` in a loop) heap-allocates one closure per slot, which is almost all of a typical page's allocations (§4b). A direct call to the callee's implementation lets the compiler use the callee's escape summary (exported in its export data), and the closures stay on the stack. In dev, direct calls would make a component body edit recompile every component that imports it (a layout edit: every page; cmd/go recompiles direct importers of a changed package, §7), so `orbit dev` keeps the hooks and its ~0.2 s save loop.

**In short**:
- Every implementation package exports its render function as **`Orbit__RenderImpl`** (renamed from `orbit__render`, in **both** flavors, so a callee's file is the same in dev and build). **Implemented differently**: only in the build flavor; `orbit_gen/` keeps `orbit__render`, and direct callees are overlaid too (see "Implementation").
- **Hook flavor** (today's shape, written to `orbit_gen/` by `generate`, `dev`, `build`, `check`, and the only shape `doctor`, the LSP and `go test` ever see).
- **Build flavor**: for each call site whose edge isn't inside an import cycle, the caller imports the callee's **implementation** package as `orbit__<Alias>` and calls `orbit__<Alias>.Orbit__RenderImpl(…)`. `orbit build` writes only the impl files that change (those with at least one direct call site) to **`.orbit/build/`**, with `.orbit/build/overlay.json`, and runs `go build -overlay=.orbit/build/overlay.json` (Q1). `orbit_gen/` on disk stays byte-identical to `orbit generate`'s output.

#### Both shapes
`src/pages/index.orbit` loops over posts and passes a capturing slot to `Card`. Hook flavor, `orbit_gen/impl/pages/index/render.go`:
```go
import (
	orbit__rt "github.com/orbit-z/orbit"
	. "example.com/blog/orbit_gen/decl/pages/index"
//line ../../../../src/pages/index.orbit:3:2
Card "example.com/blog/orbit_gen/decl/components/card"
//line render.go:9:1
)

var _ = func() int { Orbit__Render = Orbit__RenderImpl; return 0 }()

func Orbit__RenderImpl(orbit__ctx *orbit__rt.Ctx, orbit__w *orbit__rt.Writer, orbit__props Props, orbit__slots orbit__rt.Slots, orbit__params Params) error {
	…
		if orbit__err := /*line ../../../../src/pages/index.orbit:9:4*/Card.Render/*line render.go:20:104*/(orbit__ctx, orbit__w, Card.Props{…}, orbit__rt.Slots{
			Default: func(orbit__w *orbit__rt.Writer) error { … p.Title … }, // escapes: 1 alloc per iteration
		}); orbit__err != nil {
```
Build flavor, `.orbit/build/impl/pages/index/render.go` (same number of lines):
```go
import (
	orbit__rt "github.com/orbit-z/orbit"; orbit__Card "example.com/blog/orbit_gen/impl/components/card"
	. "example.com/blog/orbit_gen/decl/pages/index"
//line ../../../../../src/pages/index.orbit:3:2
Card "example.com/blog/orbit_gen/decl/components/card"
//line render.go:9:1
)
…
		if orbit__err := /*line ../../../../../src/pages/index.orbit:9:4*/orbit__Card.Orbit__RenderImpl/*line render.go:20:131*/(orbit__ctx, orbit__w, Card.Props{…}, orbit__rt.Slots{
			Default: func(orbit__w *orbit__rt.Writer) error { … p.Title … }, // does not escape
		}); orbit__err != nil {
```
and `.orbit/build/overlay.json`: `{"Replace": {"orbit_gen/impl/pages/index/render.go": ".orbit/build/impl/pages/index/render.go", …}}` (paths relative to the project root, where `go build` runs, so the file is the same in every checkout). `Card`'s own impl file has no direct call site, so it isn't overlaid.

#### Codegen rules
- **Exported name**: `Orbit__RenderImpl`, in the reserved `Orbit__` namespace (§2.2), so no user name can clash. It can't be `Orbit__Render`: the impl dot-imports its decl package, which declares the hook under that name. Same signature as the hook (a page's has the fifth `Params` argument). The link initializer becomes `Orbit__Render = Orbit__RenderImpl`. Renaming it in both flavors changes every impl golden file once, and stack traces show `card_impl.Orbit__RenderImpl` (decided by rule: simplest; one shape per callee keeps overlays to the callers).
- **Import of the callee's impl package**: alias `orbit__` + the `.orbit` import alias (`orbit__Card`). Import aliases always start with an uppercase letter (§2.2) and Orbit's own `orbit__*` names are all lowercase after the prefix (`orbit__rt`, `orbit__ctx`, `orbit__locals`, …), and aliases are unique per file, so it can't clash. The decl import stays (the call still builds `Card.Props{…}`). The impl import is added only when the file has at least one direct call site to that callee, so a callee used only with `server:defer` doesn't leave an unused import.
- **Line alignment**: the impl imports go on the `orbit__rt` import's line, separated by `;` (generated code is never gofmt'ed, §3.3), so a build-flavor file has exactly the hook-flavor file's lines; only columns differ on call lines. cmd/go records an overlaid file under its original path (`orbit_gen/impl/…/render.go`, verified, with and without `-trimpath`), so a production stack frame in glue points at the right line of the on-disk file.
- **Directive file names** in build-flavor files are computed for their real location, `.orbit/build/impl/…` (one more `../`: `../../../../../src/pages/index.orbit`). cmd/compile resolves a relative `//line` name against the directory of the file it reads, which for an overlay is the replacement file (verified: with `orbit_gen`'s names, errors printed `./.orbit/src/pages/index.orbit:21:10`; with recomputed names, `./src/pages/index.orbit:21:10`, as the hook build). The bare-basename resets (`/*line render.go:…*/`) need no change: they resolve to the overlay file, which cmd/go's own path rewriting maps back to `orbit_gen/…`.
- **`<Self>`**: a direct self-call `Orbit__RenderImpl(…)` in the build flavor (no import; a call within one package is never an import cycle). Verified: Go's escape analysis analyzes a recursive function together with itself, so a recursive `<Self>` with capturing slot content stays on the stack (`func literal does not escape`); through the hook it escapes. Dev keeps `Render(…)` (the dot-imported wrapper).
- **Pages as callees** (a `.orbit` file under `src/pages/` imported as a component, rare): keep the hook, through the decl `Render` wrapper, which builds `Params` (decided by rule: simplest; a direct call would have to build `Params` at the call site).
- **`.md` components** (§2.4): an `.orbit` → `.md` call is a normal call site, so it's direct like any other. A `.md` file's call to its layout keeps the hook: its slot is the static `orbit__content` func, so there's nothing to keep on the stack, and a layout that imports a `.md` using it as layout would otherwise need the SCC rule in `internal/markdown` too (decided by rule: simplest). `.md` impls get the `Orbit__RenderImpl` rename.
- **`server:defer` call sites**: unchanged (`orbit__rt.Defer`, no render call, no impl import). The island table keeps `NewIsland(…, comments.Render)`.
- **`client:*` call sites**: a normal call inside the marker, so direct.
- **Unchanged and still hook-based**: the decl `Render` wrapper and the hook (set as today), used by `orbittest`, `RenderComponent`, user Go code, the island table and the **route table** (`app.go` keeps calling `<page>.Render` with zero `Props` and `Slots`: no slot content, so nothing to gain from a direct call, and `app` keeps importing only decl packages; decided by rule: simplest). Tolerant/LSP output, source maps and the segments of the hook flavor are unchanged.
- **Typed writes**: the build flavor reuses the hook flavor's typed writes (`Input.Typed`): call-site keys don't depend on the call shape (§3.3, "only their Go spans move"), so pass 2 emits a second file per overlaid unit, with no second type check.

#### Cycles: which edges stay on the hook
- **Graph**: one node per component; an edge X → Y for each component Y that X's template calls with a render call site (not `server:defer`, not `<Self>`, not a page callee). Only these edges can become impl → impl imports.
- **Rule**: compute the strongly connected components (Tarjan, as `internal/project/cycles.go` already does for decl cycles). An edge X → Y is **direct iff SCC(X) ≠ SCC(Y)**; edges inside an SCC (mutual recursion A ↔ B, longer cycles) keep the hook. The direct edges then form a subgraph of the SCC condensation, which is acyclic, so the impl → impl imports can't form a cycle. SCCs are computed over the whole project and sorted, so the output is deterministic (`orbit build` always loads the whole project; dev never needs this).
- **No other cycle is possible**: declaration packages import only user packages, the runtime and other decl packages (Props cycles are already an error, §3.1), never an impl package; `src/locals` and user packages don't import impl packages (and `src/locals` must not import `orbit_gen/app`, a `doctor` check); `orbit_gen/link` and `orbit_gen/app` are imported by nothing generated. So the only new edges in the package graph are impl → impl, and they're acyclic. A user package that imports `orbit_gen/app` and is imported by a frontmatter is already a cycle today, in both flavors.
- **Partial breaking of cycles** (hooks on a feedback edge set only, the rest direct) gains nothing: measured on A ↔ B, a member called through the hook leaks its slots, and a leaked `orbit__slots` makes every closure that captures it leak too, around the whole cycle. Out of scope.

#### When escape analysis actually wins
The caller's closure stays on the stack only if every callee on its path doesn't leak `orbit__slots`. Verified with `-gcflags=-m` on build-flavor code from a fixture project (one component per case; `examples/esc` in the prototype):

| Case in the callee | Result (build flavor) |
|---|---|
| `<slot />` outlet, named outlets, `slots.Get`/`slots.Has`, `{slots.Default}` (`orbit__w.Render`) | no escape |
| Forwarding its own slot to a **direct** callee (`<Inner><slot /></Inner>`, named too) | no escape: the gain is transitive along direct calls |
| A slot renderer passed as a prop to a direct callee that renders it (`<Prop r={slots.Default} />`) | no escape |
| `server:defer` fallback containing `<slot />` | no escape (`Defer` doesn't retain the fallback) |
| `client:*` call site | no escape |
| `<Self>` recursion with capturing content | no escape |
| User Go code that only reads the renderer (`h.Peek(slots.Default)`) | no escape (its escape summary comes from export data) |
| User Go code that stores it (`h.Keep(slots.Default)` into a package var) | **escapes** (correct: it outlives the call) |
| Forwarding into a component in a cycle (`ToA` → `A`, with `A` ↔ `B` on the hook) | **escapes**, and so does the page's closure passed to `ToA` |
| Members of a cycle (`A` ↔ `B`) | **escape**, as in dev |
| `Enter`/`Leave` depth guard | no effect (doesn't touch slots) |

On real projects, every slot closure the compiler reported as escaping in the hook flavor stays on the stack in the build flavor: `examples/blog` 7 → 0, `bench/orbit-site` 5 → 0, `docs/site` 5 → 0, `bench/go` 4 → 0, the synthetic 200-file project 703 → 0 (chain shape) and 547 → 0 (layered shape). Cycles and stored renderers are rare (tree menus; user code keeping a renderer), so the spec does nothing more for them: it's documented as "slot content passed into mutually recursive components still allocates".

A knock-on effect: values that only flow into a closure also stay on the stack, e.g. `bench/go`'s `/post/hello-world` page went from 3 allocations to 1 (the slot closure **and** a small string concatenation it captured).

#### Which flavor each command uses
| Command / tool | Sees | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `orbit generate` | hook, written to `orbit_gen/` | unchanged; the canonical, committable output |
| `orbit dev` | hook | unchanged |
| `orbit build` | hook in `orbit_gen/` **plus** build flavor in `.orbit/build/`, compiled with `-overlay` | `.orbit/build/` is removed and rewritten on every build (simplest; `.orbit/` is gitignored and dockerignored); with `--embed`, `orbit_gen/static/` as today |
| `orbit check` | hook (`go vet ./orbit_gen/...`) | the flavors differ only in generated glue, covered by codegen tests (decided by rule: simplest) |
| `orbit doctor` freshness check | hook | unchanged: `orbit_gen/` always equals `orbit generate`'s output, even right after `orbit build` |
| LSP startup typed check | hook | unchanged: no new overlays after an `orbit build` |
| `go test` with `orbittest` | hook (whatever is on disk) | same bytes; allocation counts are dev's (Q3) |
| CI from a fresh clone (§6) | `generate`, `vet`, `build` | unchanged steps |
| Committed `orbit_gen/` | hook | `orbit generate && git diff --exit-code orbit_gen` still works; plain `go build ./...` gives a correct but hook-flavor binary; `orbit build` gives the production one |

- `-overlay` in the user's `GOFLAGS`: `orbit build` stops with `orbit build: -overlay in GOFLAGS isn't supported (orbit build passes its own)`, because the command-line flag would silently replace the user's (decided by rule: simplest).

#### (Q) points
1. **(Q1) How the build flavor reaches `go build`**:
   - (a) **`go build -overlay`** with the build-flavor files in `.orbit/build/` (recommended): `orbit_gen/` never changes flavor, so the LSP, `doctor`, committed `orbit_gen/`, `go test` and a concurrently running `orbit dev` are untouched; measured at the same build time as writing the files into `orbit_gen/` (below); the build cache works (a repeated build is a 32 ms no-op). Costs: directive names computed for the overlay location, `-overlay` reserved.
   - (b) `orbit build` writes the build flavor into `orbit_gen/`: no overlay, but after a build the disk differs from `orbit generate`, so `doctor` reports stale code, the LSP's typed check falls back to overlays for ~160 of 200 impl files until the next `generate`/`dev`, `git diff --exit-code` fails when `orbit_gen/` is committed, and a running `orbit dev` (which rewrites only files it changes) could keep build-flavor files on disk and lose the hook isolation. Switching itself is cheap thanks to the content-addressed cache (below).
   - (c) Build tags: both files in `orbit_gen/` (`render.go` with `//go:build !orbit_direct`, `render_direct.go` with `//go:build orbit_direct`), `orbit build` adds the tag. Generate time is not doubled (one type check, pass 2 emits a second file per caller: about 4.6 ms to re-emit all 200 units sequentially, `BenchmarkPass2`, plus ~160 more files written), and `go build -tags orbit_direct ./...` would give production code without the CLI. But it changes `orbit_gen/`'s layout (`project-structure.md`), doubles the impl files gopls, `doctor`, the LSP's comparison and `git diff` see, and gopls shows "excluded by build tags" for the direct files.
   - (d) Direct calls everywhere and only `orbit dev` uses hooks: `orbit_gen/` would then flip flavor whenever `dev` runs, with (b)'s problems during the main editing time.
2. **(Q2) `<Self>`**: direct self-call in the build flavor (recommended: verified to keep recursive slot closures on the stack, no import, no cycle) vs. always the hook (simpler codegen, but recursive components such as trees allocate per level).
3. **(Q3) Measuring the build flavor in tests and benchmarks**: `orbit build` leaves `.orbit/build/overlay.json`, and `go test -overlay=.orbit/build/overlay.json ./...` runs tests against the build flavor (recommended: no new CLI surface; `bench/go` uses it to publish production numbers, its README gets the two commands) vs. `orbit generate --build-flavor` writing the overlay without building (one more flag) vs. nothing (`bench/go` and user allocation tests keep measuring the hook flavor, which isn't what production runs).
4. **(Q4) Deep component graphs**: direct imports serialize compilation along the longest impl → impl chain (measured ~13 ms per extra level). No cap (recommended: real projects are 2–3 levels deep, `examples/blog`, `bench/orbit-site`, `docs/site` all ≤ 3, where the measured cost is +2–17% on a full rebuild of the generated code and about +50 ms on a layout edit) vs. a cap (edges beyond a depth such as 16 keep the hook: bounded build time, more complex rule, runtime cost appears silently on deep graphs) vs. an `orbit.json` switch to turn direct calls off.

Decided by rule (to log in `docs/DECISIONS.md`): the `Orbit__RenderImpl` name in both flavors (simplest); pages as callees and `.md` → layout calls keep the hook (simplest); route and island tables unchanged (simplest); `.orbit/build/` rewritten per build (simplest); `orbit check` vets the hook flavor only (simplest); `-overlay` in `GOFLAGS` is an error (simplest).

#### Cost (measured with a prototype: a script rewriting `orbit generate`'s output into the build flavor, `scratchpad/directcalls/`; Ryzen 7 9700X, 16 threads, Go 1.27.3; the machine was shared, load average given per row)
**Runtime** (`bench/go`, `go test -bench`, 6 runs, benchstat):

| Benchmark | Hook | Build flavor | Load |
|---|---|---|---|
| Render, 500 cards, 16 threads | 57.2 µs, 40,063 B, 501 allocs | **51.4 µs (−10%), 13 B, 0 allocs** | 0.6–1.4 |
| Serve `/cards`, 16 threads | 57.6 µs, 40,240 B, 502 allocs | **52.7 µs (−9%), 59 B, 1 alloc** (`Content-Length`) | 0.6–1.4 |
| Serve `/post/hello-world`, 16 threads | 372 ns, 104 B, 3 allocs | **338 ns (−9%), 48 B, 1 alloc** | 0.6–1.4 |
| Render, 500 cards, `-cpu 1` | 59.1 µs | **49.5 µs (−16%)** | 1.2–6.3 |
| Serve `/cards`, `-cpu 1` | 60.3 µs | **51.1 µs (−15%)** | 1.2–6.3 |

templ and `html/template` are unchanged (within noise). The output is byte-identical (`bench/go`'s `TestEquivalent` passes on the build flavor; the fixture project's page hashes match).

**Build time**, `go build` of the server at 200 files (`bench/devloop`'s project). Two shapes: the **layered** one (each component calls two components 40 positions further, `SYNTH_STRIDE=40`: impl → impl depth 6, median 3, closer to real sites) and devloop's default **chain** (each component calls the next two: depth 157, a worst case no real site has). "Warm std" = std and user packages cached, every generated package new; "edit" = one body edit of the `Base` layout, then a rebuild.

| Scenario | Hook | Build flavor | Load |
|---|---|---|---|
| layered, warm std, 16 threads | 617 ms | 691 ms (+12%); with `-overlay` 722 ms (+17%; an earlier run: 650 / 711 / 700 ms) | 2.0–4.2 |
| layered, warm std, `GOMAXPROCS=4 taskset -c 0-3` | 1,161 ms | 1,245 ms; with `-overlay` 1,253 ms (+8%) | 4.2–8.6 |
| layered, cold cache, 16 threads | 3,783 ms | 3,870 ms (+2%) | 5.8–7.0 |
| layered, cold cache, 4 cores | 6,142 ms | 6,332 ms (+3%) | 8.4–8.9 |
| layered, layout body edit, 16 threads | 237 ms | 284 ms (+47 ms: the pages that import it recompile) | 9.0–9.6 |
| layered, layout body edit, 4 cores | 269 ms | 317 ms | 6.5–6.8 |
| chain, warm std, 16 threads | 707 ms | 2,645 ms (3.7×) | 9.6–11.6 |
| chain, warm std, 4 cores | 1,182 ms | 2,754 ms | 6.8–8.5 |
| chain, cold cache, 16 threads | 3,774 ms | 5,778 ms | 7.0–9.8 |

**Binary size**: −150 KB on the layered project (12,063,887 → 11,913,603 bytes, −1.2%), −200 KB on the chain (−1.6%): fewer closure allocation paths.

**Generate**: under (Q1 a), `orbit build` emits a second file for each impl unit with a direct call site, reusing the typed writes (≤ 4.6 ms sequential for all 200 units, `BenchmarkPass2`), and writes them to `.orbit/build/` (~10 ms for 200 files): about 1–2% of a warm `orbit build`. `orbit generate`, `dev`, `check` and the LSP: no change.

**Switching** between `orbit dev` and `orbit build` on one checkout (layered project, load 2.4): under (Q1 a) `orbit_gen/` never changes flavor, so there is nothing to switch. Under (Q1 b), measured by alternating dev-flag hook builds and prod-flag direct builds with the files rewritten each time: first dev build 613 ms, first prod build 837 ms (each would be paid anyway: dev and prod builds use different `-gcflags`, so they never share compiled packages), every later switch 50 ms (rewriting 400 files: 11 ms) because both flavors stay in Go's content-addressed cache. The hook-only baseline is the same (523 / 933 / ~52 ms). The real costs of (b) are the LSP and `doctor` effects in (Q1), not compile time.

**Dev loop**: none (dev keeps the hooks; the `Orbit__RenderImpl` rename changes no package structure).

#### Tests (what must exist)
- **Codegen goldens** (`internal/compiler/testdata/`, `-update`): build-flavor output next to the hook flavor for a direct call, named and default slots, `<Self>`, a page callee (hook), an `.md` callee (direct) and an `.md` page (layout on the hook), `server:defer` (no impl import), `client:*`, and a callee used only with `server:defer` (no unused import). Each build-flavor file has the hook file's line count.
- **Cycles** (`internal/project`): A ↔ B (both edges hook), `<Self>` (direct), a three-component cycle A → B → C → A, and a cycle plus an outside caller (`ToA` → `A` direct); each builds with `-overlay` and passes `go vet -overlay=…`. A table test of the SCC rule, and that edges are emitted in sorted order.
- **Byte-identical output**: an integration test builds `examples/blog` and the fixture project in both flavors and compares every route's response body (via `orbittest.Get` under `go test` with and without `-overlay`).
- **Allocations**: a generated fixture page with capturing default and named slots, forwarding and `<Self>` renders with **0 allocations** under `-overlay` (`AllocsPerRun`), and with the expected count on the hook flavor (so the test notices if the hook flavor ever stops allocating, which would mean the test lost its point). The existing runtime alloc tests (`site_alloc_test.go`, static slots, hand-written hooks) are unchanged.
- **Positions**: the §3.3 error golden project, built through `orbit build` (overlay), reports the same cleaned-up `src/…orbit:L:C` as the hook build; a panic in a build-flavor binary logs the same `.orbit` position (§4, Errors).
- **Disk stays canonical**: after `orbit build`, `orbit_gen/` equals `orbit generate`'s output (so `doctor` is ✓ and the LSP's typed check opens no overlays); two `orbit build` runs produce identical `.orbit/build/` trees and `overlay.json`.
- **Flags**: `-overlay` in `GOFLAGS` is an error; `--embed` and `GOFLAGS=-tags=…` still combine with the overlay.
- `go build ./... && go vet ./... && go test ./...` as always.

#### User-docs changes (with the implementation)
- `performance.md`: the slots bullet ("allocates one small closure per slot" → only in dev, `go test` and plain `go build` builds, and for content passed into mutually recursive components or stored by user code), "What still allocates", the `bench/go` table (Orbit's row: ~51 µs, 0 allocs) and its 501-allocations paragraph, the small param page (1 allocation), and a short "dev and `go test` use indirect calls" note with the build-time cost.
- `components.md` (the "costs almost nothing" paragraph about capturing slot content), `reference/template-syntax.md#slots` (the allocation sentence) and `reference/runtime-api.md` (`Slots`).
- `dev-server.md`: dev renders through hooks so a component edit recompiles only that component; allocation counts and timings in dev differ from production; profile with `orbit build && orbit preview`.
- `testing.md`: `orbittest` renders the hook flavor (same HTML); `go test -overlay=.orbit/build/overlay.json` after `orbit build` for production allocation counts (if Q3 is accepted).
- `reference/cli.md` (`orbit build`: `.orbit/build/`, the overlay, `-overlay` in `GOFLAGS`), `project-structure.md` (`.orbit/build/`; `orbit_gen/` contents unchanged; plain `go build` from a committed `orbit_gen/` is correct but not the production flavor), `deploy.md` (build with `orbit build`).
- `docs/DECISIONS.md`: the "decided by rule" items above. `bench/go/README.md`: how the published numbers are produced.
- In this file, with the implementation: §3.1 (Calls, No cycles, Self, Cost), §4b's slots bullet, §6's build pipeline (steps 4 and 6), §7 (dev keeps the hooks), and the "Slots" bullet in `CLAUDE.md`.

#### Implementation (2026-10-26)
- **Deviation: the rename is build-flavor only.** With `Orbit__RenderImpl` exported in `orbit_gen/`, each impl package's export data carries the function's signature, and so its decl package's `Props` and the runtime types; `orbit_gen/link` imports all 200 impl packages and reads it. Measured with `-debug-actiongraph` on devloop's project: link's compile went from 7.8 to 22.4 ms, so a dev body edit went from 163 to 179 ms (go build only, 9 alternating runs) and `bench/devloop -dev` from 174/167/200 ms to 190/183/218 ms (body/frontmatter/props). That breaks the user principle (`orbit dev` no slower), so the hook flavor is exactly today's code (byte-identical `orbit_gen/` to the previous CLI, checked on `examples/blog`), and the build flavor renames the function (and its link initializer) in every file it overlays. The overlaid set is the files with a direct call site **plus their direct callees** (an empty `DirectCalls`); in practice almost every impl file (200 of 200 on the layered project, 5 of 9 in `bench/go`). Cost: a third emit for those units, about 4.3 ms at 16 threads and 4.5–5 ms on 4 cores for 200 files including the writes (`BenchmarkBuildFiles`).
- **Deviation: `.orbit` → `.md` calls keep the hook.** A Markdown file's render ignores its props and slots (`_ Props, _ orbit__rt.Slots`), so a direct call keeps nothing on the stack, and making an `.md` impl a direct callee would need a build flavor in `internal/markdown` (directive names, the rename). `.md` impls are never overlaid (rule: simplest).
- **Small choices** (logged in `docs/DECISIONS.md`, "Direct calls"): `<Self>` in a page keeps the hook (like a page callee); `overlay.json` is always written and `-overlay` always passed, with an empty `Replace` when nothing is overlaid; the `-overlay`-in-`GOFLAGS` error carries the CLI's usual prefix (`orbit: -overlay in GOFLAGS isn't supported (orbit build passes its own)`) and is checked before generating; `orbit build` runs `go env GOFLAGS` once; a build-flavor codegen inconsistency (an impl import list that doesn't match the direct call sites, guarded in `genImpl`) is an internal error that fails the build.
- **Shape**: `compiler.Input.Direct *DirectCalls{Callees map[path]bool; Self bool}` selects the build flavor of `Impl`: impl imports on the `orbit__rt` line (`; orbit__Card "…/impl/components/card"`, sorted by alias), `orbit__Card.Orbit__RenderImpl` / `Orbit__RenderImpl` at the call sites, directive names via `DirectiveFile(BuildPath(path), …)`. `compiler.BuildDir`, `OverlayFile`, `BuildPath`. `project.DirectCalls` (the SCC rule, reusing `stronglyConnected`), `BuildFiles` (sorted files + `overlay.json`, paths relative to the root), `WriteBuildFiles`. `build.Run` writes them after `public/` and builds with `-overlay=.orbit/build/overlay.json`.
- **Tests**: compiler goldens `<pkg>.render.build.go.golden` for every strict codegen case (27 files; the new `direct` case covers default and named capturing slots, forwarding, `<Self>`, a page callee, `server:defer`-only and `client:*` callees), each with the hook file's line count, positions read back from the overlay location, `go build`/`go vet -overlay` and the same `want.html`; `TestDirectAllocs` (0 allocations under `-overlay`, 13 through the hooks); `TestLineDirectivePositionsBuildFlavor` (the §3.3 position project, same cleaned errors with and without the overlay); `internal/project` `TestDirectCallsRule` (A ↔ B, C → D → E → C, `ToA` → `A`, `<Self>`, page and defer-only callees), `TestBuildFlavorCycles` (build, vet, byte-identical responses, deterministic sorted `BuildFiles`, `orbit_gen/` untouched), `TestBuildFlavorMarkdown`; `internal/build` `checkBuildFlavor` and `checkFlavors` in `TestExampleBlog` and `TestE2E` (orbit_gen/ equals `orbit generate`'s output after `orbit build`, two builds write the same `.orbit/build/`, every route's body identical under `go test` with and without `-overlay`, the example's own tests under `-overlay`, the `[slug]` panic position from an overlaid file), `TestBuildOverlayInGOFLAGS`, `TestBuildFlavorTagsEmbed` (`GOFLAGS=-tags=…` + `--embed` + overlay).
- **Measured** (Ryzen 7 9700X, 16 threads, Go 1.27.3; shared machine, load given): `bench/go` render of the 500-card page 57.8 → 50.2 µs (−13%), 40 KB → 13 B, 501 → 0 allocs; serve `/cards` 57.9 → 50.9 µs, 502 → 1 alloc; `/post/hello-world` 378 → 340 ns, 3 → 1 alloc (load 1.5–1.9); `-cpu 1`: 60.5 → 50.4 µs (−17%). `orbit build` at 200 files, layered shape, warm std, every generated package new: 650 → 732 ms (+13%) at 16 threads, 1,044 → 1,147 ms (+10%) with `GOMAXPROCS=4 taskset -c 0-3`; a layout body edit 348 → 412 ms (load 3.7–7.5). `examples/blog`: 316 → 321 ms (16 threads), 337 → 337 ms (4 cores). Binary: 12,072,205 → 11,920,113 bytes (−1.3%) on the layered project, `examples/blog` −96 bytes. Escaping slot closures (`-gcflags=-m`): layered project 547 → 0, `examples/blog` 7 → 0. Dev loop (`bench/devloop -dev`, body/frontmatter/props, alternating, load 3–25): unchanged within noise (e.g. 174/166/184 vs. 176/170/190 ms), as the hook flavor is byte-identical.
