- **Re-run** on a quiet machine (2026-10-26, commit 65cfe93, load average < 1.5; `docs/performance.md` Results): `/cards` Orbit at `GOMAXPROCS=1` 8,518 → 9,749 req/s (+14%, CPU per page 0.11 → 0.093 ms), all cores 33,012 → 39,746 req/s (+20%, p99 12.7 → 6.2 ms, RSS 37 → 27 MB); Astro unchanged (669 / 698 req/s), so 14.6× per CPU (was 12.5×) and 57× at all cores (was 47×). `bench/go` (16 threads): Orbit 49.6 µs, 0 allocs.

#### Out of scope
- Direct calls in `orbit dev` (the user decision this spec implements), or an opt-in for it.
- Direct calls inside import cycles (it would need merging an SCC's components into one package).
- Direct calls from the route table, the island table, `RenderComponent` and `orbittest` (no slot content to keep on the stack).
- Removing the hook or the decl `Render` wrapper.
- PGO-driven devirtualization and inlining of component bodies (`Orbit__RenderImpl` has a `defer`, and dev builds disable inlining in `orbit_gen/` anyway).

#### Risks
- **Deep graphs** build slower (chain shape: 3.7× at warm std), see (Q4).
- **Two flavors to keep correct**: a codegen bug that only shows in the build flavor isn't caught by `orbit dev`, `orbit check` or the LSP. The byte-identical, cycle and golden tests above are the guard; `orbit build` errors from generated glue would be Orbit bugs, not user errors.
- **`-overlay`** is a stable `go build` flag (since Go 1.16) but less used; its interaction with relative `//line` names (resolved against the replacement file) is why the directive names are recomputed, and a test pins it.

### 3.6 Concurrent rendering of slow components
**Status: Implemented** (2026-10-26; accepted the same day; user decision: an opt-in directive; automatic concurrency was rejected; implementation notes, deviations and measurements under "Implementation" at the end of this section). User answers: (Q1) the name is **`server:concurrent`** (not the recommended `server:parallel`); (Q2) on the call site only (a component-level default may come later, after the `nyth` port); (Q3) the cap is **configurable now** (a `Site` option, default 16, inline past it); (Q4) no cancellation on error, like Astro. (Q5) decided by the coordinator with the recommendation (deterministic, the in-order result): status and header writes merge at the join in output order.

**Why**: Astro 7 overlaps sibling components that await. In `runtime/server/render/any.js` (`renderArray`) and `astro/render-template.js` (`RenderTemplateResult.render`), when one child of a template (or of an array, such as a `.map()` result) returns a promise, **every following child of that template starts at once**, each into its own `BufferedRenderer` (`util.js`), and the buffers are flushed in order. Astro component factories are async, so after the first component in a template, the rest of that template's expressions render concurrently. Children before the first async one render in order. Nested templates apply the same rule recursively. Orbit renders in order into one buffered response (§4), so two sibling components that each wait 10 ms for their own data take 20 ms in Orbit and about 10 ms in Astro. Sites written in that style, where components fetch their own data (`nyth`, the port that prompted this), would have to restructure their fetches into the page to get the overlap back.

Go can't know in advance whether a component will block, so automatic concurrency would charge every sibling: a goroutine, a pooled buffer and an in-order copy cost about 170 ns and 2 allocations each, roughly 10× a small component's render (coordinator's measurement: 500 cards went from 9 µs to 87 µs). Orbit therefore makes it **opt-in**: the author marks the slow call sites, and only those pay.

#### End-to-end example
```orbit
---
import (
    Weather "../components/Weather.orbit"       // frontmatter: ~10 ms weather API call
    LatestPosts "../components/LatestPosts.orbit" // frontmatter: ~10 ms DB query
    Base "../layouts/Base.orbit"
)
---
<Base title="Home">
  <Weather server:concurrent city="Paris" />
  <LatestPosts server:concurrent limit={5} />
  <footer>…</footer>
</Base>
```
- **Without the directive**: `Weather` renders, then `LatestPosts`: about 20 ms before the response can be written.
- **With it**: each marked component starts in its own goroutine when the page's render reaches it, the page keeps rendering (the `<footer>`, the rest of `Base`), and the response is assembled in source order once both are done: about 10 ms. The HTML is byte-identical.

Measured with the prototype (`scratchpad/concurrent-spec/`, hand-written generated code over a patched copy of the runtime; a full `Site.Handler` request, 10 ms `time.Sleep` per component, 20 runs; Ryzen 7 9700X, 16 threads, Go 1.27.3, load average 30–60 on a shared machine):

| Slow components on the page | In order | `server:concurrent` |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 10.7 ms | 10.4 ms |
| 2 | 20.8 ms | 10.6 ms |
| 3 | 31.8 ms | 10.5 ms |

#### Semantics
- **When a marked component starts**: when the parent's render reaches the call site, after its props and slots are evaluated in the parent, in source order, as for an unmarked call. Orbit doesn't hoist marked calls to the start of the parent: props can depend on `{if}`/`{for}` and on earlier output, and the gap is the parent's synchronous rendering, microseconds. This matches Astro, where the siblings after the first async child start "at once" when the parent reaches it.
- **What runs concurrently**: the marked component's whole render (its frontmatter, its template and its subtree, including slot content passed to it) in one goroutine, against the rest of the request's render: the parent continues after the call site, and other marked components run in their own goroutines. Everything not marked still renders in order on the goroutine that reaches it. So an **unmarked** slow component still blocks its goroutine: `<A server:concurrent /><B />` overlaps A and B, `<A /><B server:concurrent />` doesn't (B starts only after A). The docs say: mark every slow component.
- **Where the output goes**: into the marked component's own pooled `Writer` and buffer. The parent's `Writer` records a **hole**: the current offset in its buffer and the child `Writer`. The parent keeps writing into its own buffer.
- **Join**: once, at the end of the request's render (the page, an island request, a `RenderComponent` fragment, `orbittest`), not when the parent component returns. Two marked components in different parents (one in the header, one in the body) overlap too, and a parent never waits for its children. The join waits for every goroutine of the request, including nested ones, then copies the root buffer and the child buffers, recursively and in output order, into one pooled buffer, which is written as today (one `Write`, `Content-Length`). Output order is strictly the source order, whatever finished first.
- **Nesting**: a marked component inside a marked component's subtree forks from the child's `Writer`; its hole is in the child's buffer. Slot content forwarded into a marked component runs in that component's goroutine.
- **Head injection**: the page's tags still go at the first `</head>` in output order. A root `Writer` with no holes yet writes them inline at `HeadEnd` as today (the usual case: the layout's `</head>` comes before any body component). Otherwise `HeadEnd` only records its offset, and the join inserts the tags while copying, which costs nothing extra. `finishHead` (no marker) runs on the assembled buffer.
- **Only under Orbit's entry points**: a `Writer` from `orbit.NewWriter` (user Go code calling a component's `Render` with its own buffer) has no request group, so `server:concurrent` renders inline there, in order. Output is always correct, never missing a hole.

#### Codegen
A marked call site (hook flavor; the props literal, field keys and line directives are those of a normal call, §3.2):
```go
if orbit__err := /*line ../../../../src/pages/index.orbit:9:4*/orbit__rt.Concurrent/*line render.go:…*/(orbit__ctx, orbit__w, Weather.Render, Weather.Props{
	/*line …*/City/*line render.go:…*/: "Paris",
}, orbit__rt.Slots{}); orbit__err != nil {
	return orbit__err
}
```
- `Concurrent[P any](c *Ctx, w *Writer, render func(*Ctx, *Writer, P, Slots) error, props P, slots Slots) error` is generic over `Props`, like `NewIsland`, so the props stay a typed literal evaluated in the parent, with no closure written by codegen. It returns the parent's sticky error (and starts nothing) when the parent already failed, and otherwise `nil`: the child's errors come out at the join.
- **Build flavor** (§3.5): the render argument is `orbit__Weather.Orbit__RenderImpl` for a direct edge (the hook inside an SCC, the decl `Render` for a page callee, as for other calls). The goroutine makes props and slots escape anyway, so a marked call site loses §3.5's stack win for its own slot closures (accepted: one heap closure per marked instance is the price of the goroutine).
- **Locals before the first fork**: `LocalsOf` caches the `*L` on the `Ctx` lazily (§4). If a child resolved it on its copy of the `Ctx` (below), two goroutines could allocate two `Locals`. So a render with a marked call site emits `orbit__rt.LocalsOf[orbit__locals.Locals](orbit__ctx)` right before the call, when the project has a `src/locals.Locals` (with `NoLocals` there's nothing to share). It's idempotent and appended to the call's line, so line numbers don't move. Cost: a `new(L)` on pages that use the directive while no middleware attached locals and nothing read them.
- **Slot content rebinds the Ctx**: slot content is compiled in the parent and captures the parent's `orbit__ctx`, but it can run in another goroutine (passed to a marked component, or forwarded by an unmarked one into a marked one). So **every** slot closure whose content uses `orbit__ctx` (a component call, `Defer`, `LocalsOf`) or the user's `ctx` binding starts with `orbit__ctx := orbit__rt.CtxOf(orbit__w, orbit__ctx)` (and `ctx := orbit__ctx` when it uses `ctx`), on the closure's first line. `CtxOf` returns the `Ctx` of the goroutine that owns the writer (`w.ctx`, set on child writers), or its second argument for the root writer. That's a field load and a branch, no allocation; measured within noise on 500 capturing slots (below). It's emitted in every project, whether or not it uses the directive, so a file's output doesn't depend on others (decided by rule: simplest; per-file codegen in `orbit dev` stays per file). The `ctx` scan is the syntactic object resolution already used for `locals` (§3.3), so tolerant mode and the type-check fallbacks need no `types.Info`; a user who shadows `ctx` keeps their own variable.
- **`{for}`**: a marked component in a loop forks once per iteration, up to the cap (below), so `{for _, p := range posts}<Card server:concurrent post={p} />{/for}` is allowed. Go 1.22 per-iteration variables keep each iteration's props and captured variables apart. It's rarely a good idea: one query in the page beats 500 fetches. The docs say so.
- **`<Self server:concurrent>`** is allowed (a recursive tree whose levels fetch). The depth guard keeps working: a child `Writer` starts with a copy of its parent's component stack (`Enter`/`Leave`), so depth and the "naming the stack" error span goroutines.
- **`client:*`**: allowed together. The `<orbit-lazy>` marker is static `Raw` written by the parent around the hole, so it ends up around the component's output.
- **`server:defer`**: combining them is a compile error, `server:defer and server:concurrent can't be combined; a deferred component already renders in its own request` (decided by rule: simplest; there's nothing to overlap). A `server:defer` call site inside a marked subtree works: `Defer` writes its placeholder into the child's buffer, and the island sealer is already safe for concurrent use (shared across requests).
- **Compile errors** (same shape as `server:defer`'s, §2.3): `server:concurrent only works on .orbit components` (on an HTML element, `<slot>` or `<Fragment>`), `server:concurrent takes no value`. The unknown-directive message lists it: `(server directives: server:defer, server:concurrent)`. On a `.md` component it's allowed and useless (its HTML is a constant); no special case (decided by rule: simplest).

#### Shared state and safety
Each marked component runs with its **own `Writer`** (pooled) and its **own `Ctx`** (pooled, a copy of the forking goroutine's), so most state needs no lock, and the unmarked path gains no atomics:

| State | Concurrent rendering |
|---|---|
| `Writer` buffer, sticky error, component stack, head offset, JSON encoder | per goroutine (the child's `Writer`); the stack is copied at the fork |
| `Ctx.Request`, `URL`, `Site`, route params, dev flag, island keys | copied pointers and slices, read-only. Param values live in the root `Ctx`'s storage, which is released only after the join |
| `Ctx` locals | the same `*L` (resolved before the first fork, above); its **fields** are user data (below) |
| `ctx.SetStatus`, `ctx.Header()` | per `Ctx`: a child's status and headers start empty and are **merged at the join in output order**, after the root's own: a child's non-zero status replaces the page's, and each header key a child set replaces the page's values for that key. So a later component in output order wins, as in order rendering, except that a marked component's writes are applied after those of the unmarked components that follow it (decided by rule: simplest deterministic option; logged) |
| `ctx.Redirect`, `orbit.ErrNotFound` | plain error values, nothing shared: they come out at the join like any error (below) |
| The request group (a `sync.WaitGroup`, the running count for the cap, the abort flag) | the only shared, synchronized state; an unexported value embedded in the pooled root `Writer`, touched only by `Concurrent`, the goroutines and the join |

Cost on the unmarked path: `Writer` gets four fields (holes, group pointer, owner `Ctx`, the embedded group) reset with the rest; `servePage` checks `len(holes) > 0` once; `HeadEnd` checks it once; slot closures that use the ctx call `CtxOf`. No allocation, no atomic: measured, the existing `TestRenderAllocs` (0) and `TestServeAllocs` (1, `Content-Length`) pass unchanged on the patched runtime. The group's `sync.WaitGroup` and atomics sit behind an unexported field, so the generated packages' view of `Writer` stays light (§4b; verified in the implementation by the object-size check).

**User data races** stay the user's responsibility, as with any goroutine. Only request-scoped data is newly shared, because package-level state is already shared between concurrent requests. Documented, with `go test -race` advice:
- props passed by reference (slices, maps, pointers) that a marked component, or the parent, mutates while the other reads them;
- `locals` fields written by a marked component and read or written elsewhere (`locals.Count++` from two components); use a `sync` type in `Locals` for such fields;
- a frontmatter closure that captures `ctx` and calls `SetStatus`/`Header()`, called from code running in a marked component;
- reading `ctx.Request.Body` (read it in the page's frontmatter).

#### Errors, panics, redirects, cancellation
- **The first error in output order wins**: the join walks the holes in order (recursively), and the first child error wins over everything after it, including the parent's own error (which happened after the holes created so far, since `Concurrent` refuses to fork once the parent failed). This gives exactly the error the in-order render would have given. A redirect or `ErrNotFound` from a marked component is handled like one from an unmarked component (`renderFailed`, §4; on an island request §5b). Errors keep their `.orbit` position (`ErrorAt`).
- **The request always waits for every started goroutine**, even after an error: children write into pooled buffers and read the root `Ctx`, which can't be released before they're done. Until the join, the parent and other components keep rendering (microseconds) and keep their side effects: a component after the failing one may already have run its fetch, which the in-order render would have skipped. Documented.
- **Panics** are recovered in each goroutine (a panic there would crash the process) and become a `panicError` with the innermost `.orbit` frame of that goroutine, like `callRender` today (§4, Errors): the same production log line and 500, the same dev overlay. `http.ErrAbortHandler` is recorded and re-panicked on the request goroutine after the join, as net/http expects.
- **No cancellation of the others** (Q4).

#### Limits
- **A per-request cap** on marked components running at once, counted across nesting (Q3). Past the cap, `Concurrent` renders the component **inline** on the calling goroutine, in order. Inline, not a blocking wait: a nested fork waiting for a slot held by its own ancestor would deadlock. The output is the same either way.

#### Placement and name
- **On the call site**, like `server:defer` and `client:*`: the cost is visible where it's paid, and a component can be parallel in one place and not in another (Q2).

#### (Q) points
1. **(Q1) Name**:
   - (a) **`server:parallel`** (recommended): the phrase web developers use for overlapping fetches ("fetch in parallel", `Promise.all`), short, and next to `server:defer` in the reserved namespace. It overstates the CPU side: what matters is overlapped waiting, and with `GOMAXPROCS=1` it still helps.
   - (b) `server:concurrent` (**chosen**): the exact Go term ("concurrency is not parallelism"), longer, less familiar to Astro users.
   - (c) `server:async`: reads like Astro's async components, but in Astro every component can be async, so it would suggest the opposite of opt-in.
   - (d) `server:eager` / `server:prefetch`: describes starting early, but the component doesn't start earlier than its position, it only stops blocking the rest.
2. **(Q2) Placement**:
   - (a) **On the call site** (recommended): explicit, consistent with `server:defer`/`client:*`, per use, and the compiler errors are at the use.
   - (b) Declared in the component (a frontmatter marker such as `//orbit:parallel`), so every use is parallel: matches "the component knows it fetches" (`nyth`'s style) and can't be forgotten at one call site, but hides a goroutine per use, including in loops, and needs a new frontmatter construct (LSP, `orbit fmt`).
   - (c) Both: more surface; (b) can be added later without breaking (a).
3. **(Q3) The cap**:
   - (a) **A fixed 16 per request** (recommended), past it inline: bounds goroutines, buffers and concurrent backend calls per request (a marked component in a 500-item loop); 16 covers every realistic page of slow siblings. An `orbit.WithConcurrencyLimit(n)` `Option` can be added later without breaking.
   - (b) Configurable now (`WithConcurrencyLimit`, or `orbit.json`; **chosen**, as a `Site` option): more surface before anyone needs it.
   - (c) No cap (Astro's behavior: every following sibling starts): 500 goroutines and buffers per request for a marked loop; the backend's own pool (`database/sql`'s `MaxOpenConns`) then queues them.
   - A process-wide cap is out of scope (backend pools already bound it).
4. **(Q4) Cancellation on error**:
   - (a) **None** (recommended; Astro behavior: its siblings keep running, promises can't be cancelled): the reported error is exactly the in-order one, nothing allocates, and `ctx.Request.Context()` is still cancelled by net/http when the client goes away. Cost: a failing page waits for its slowest started sibling.
   - (b) Derive a cancellable context for the request when the first component forks (`context.WithCancel` plus `Request.WithContext`: about 2–3 allocations and a `Request` copy, only on pages that fork) and cancel it at the first error in time; errors that are `context.Canceled` after Orbit cancelled are skipped when choosing the error in output order. Faster failure for code that honors the context, slightly non-deterministic reporting when two components fail.
5. **(Q5) Status and header writes from marked components**: merged at the join in output order (recommended, above: deterministic, no lock, the child's `ctx.Header()` starts empty so it doesn't see the page's) vs. one shared, mutex-protected `Ctx` response state (time order, non-deterministic; and `Header()` returns a map that can't be locked, so it would race anyway) vs. a compile/runtime error when a marked component sets them (too strict: a component setting `Cache-Control` or `Vary` is plausible).

Decided by rule (to log in `docs/DECISIONS.md`): start at the call site, not hoisted (Astro); join once at the end of the request's render (simplest that keeps Astro's overlap across parents); first error in output order (Astro: its buffers flush, and so reject, in order); `server:defer` + `server:concurrent` is an error, `client:*` + `server:concurrent` is allowed, `<Self>`, loops and `.md` components are allowed (simplest); inline under `NewWriter` and past the cap (simplest, deadlock-free); `CtxOf` emitted in every project (simplest); header merge semantics (simplest deterministic).

#### Cost (prototype: a patched copy of the runtime, `scratchpad/concurrent-spec/orbit/parallel.go`, with hand-written generated pages in `parallel_test.go`; full `Site.Handler` requests with a reused `ResponseWriter`; Ryzen 7 9700X, 16 threads, Go 1.27.3, 6 runs, medians; the machine was heavily shared, load average 28–62 throughout, so the 16-thread rows are noisy)

| Benchmark | In order | `server:concurrent` | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 small cards, `-cpu 1` | 279 ns, 1 alloc | 1.48 µs, 4 allocs (384 B) | ≈ 0.4 µs and 1 alloc (~90 B, the goroutine's arguments) per marked instance, join included |
| 3 small cards, 16 threads | 223–562 ns | 1.2–4.7 µs | cross-CPU wake-ups |
| 20 small cards, `-cpu 1` | 924 ns | 4.7 µs, 17 allocs | 16 forked + 4 inline (cap) |
| 20 small cards, 16 threads | 1.0–2.6 µs | 8.5–16 µs | |
| 500 small cards, cap 16, `-cpu 1` | 20.9 µs | 25.8 µs | the cap keeps a marked loop near the in-order cost |
| 500 small cards, no cap, `-cpu 1` | 20.9 µs | 138 µs, 501 allocs, 56 KB | why (Q3) recommends a cap |
| ~34 KB page, 2 marked components, 16 threads | 16.9 µs | 18.2 µs | the assembly copy plus 2 forks: ~1.2 µs |
| 500 capturing slots, direct calls, with vs. without `CtxOf` | 25.9 µs | 26.3 µs (`CtxOf`) | within noise; 0 extra allocations |
| Typical page (`TestServeAllocs`, unmarked) | 1 alloc | 1 alloc | unchanged; `TestRenderAllocs` stays 0 |

So a marked component costs a few hundred nanoseconds and one small allocation, nothing against the 10 ms it's meant to hide, and nothing when unmarked. All prototype tests pass under `-race` (same output as in order, error, panic and status/header merge cases).

**Build and dev loop**: one new directive in the parser and codegen; `Concurrent` is a generic instantiation in each package with a marked call site only (like `NewIsland`'s in `app`). No new packages or imports, except `src/locals` in a component that has a marked call site but never used `locals` (a `src/locals` edit already recompiles nearly everything). Expected cost: within noise; measured in the implementation with `bench/devloop`.

#### Tests (what must exist)
- **Runtime** (`parallel_test.go`, run with and without `-race` in CI): byte-identical output vs. in order for siblings, nested marked components, slots forwarded into a marked component, `client:*` around a hole, a marked component in a loop past the cap; head injection with `</head>` before any hole, inside a marked component, and with no `</head>`; first error in output order (a later component failing first in time, a parent failing after a hole); a panic in a child (500, `.orbit` position in the log line) and `http.ErrAbortHandler` re-panicked on the request goroutine; redirect and `ErrNotFound` from a child, on a page and on an island request; status and header merge order; the depth guard across goroutines (`<Self server:concurrent>` past depth 1000); `NewWriter` renders inline; the latency test (2–3 components sleeping 10 ms finish in well under 2× 10 ms); every pooled object returned (a pool-count check).
- **Allocations**: `TestRenderAllocs` and `TestServeAllocs` unchanged (0 and 1); a page with one marked component serves with the expected small count (`Content-Length` + 1 per fork).
- **Codegen goldens** (`-update`): a marked call site in both flavors (hook, direct, SCC member, page callee), with `LocalsOf` before it when `src/locals` exists; `CtxOf` in slot closures that use `orbit__ctx`/`ctx`, absent in those that don't; `ctx` shadowed by the user; each build-flavor file keeps the hook file's line count.
- **Compile errors**: on an HTML element, `<slot>`, `<Fragment>`, with a value, combined with `server:defer`; the unknown-`server:`-directive message lists `server:concurrent`.
- **Integration**: an `examples/blog` (or fixture) page with two marked components that sleep, served through `orbittest.Get`: same body as without the directive, and the latency bound.
- **LSP**: `server:concurrent` in directive completion.
- `go build ./... && go vet ./... && go test ./...` and `go test -race ./...`.

#### User-docs changes (with the implementation)
- `reference/template-syntax.md`: the directive (rules, combinations, errors) next to `server:defer`.
- `rendering.md`: a "Slow components" section: buffered responses wait for the slowest part; `server:concurrent` overlaps slow siblings in the same response; `server:defer` takes them off the first byte; fetching in the page with Go's own concurrency (`errgroup`-style) remains an option.
- `lazy-loading.md`: **when to use which**. `server:defer`: the part can arrive after the page (below the fold, personalised, slow third-party APIs); the first byte doesn't wait for it, but it costs a second request and shows a fallback. `server:concurrent`: the part must be in the HTML (SEO, above the fold, no layout shift); the page still waits for the slowest part, but slow parts no longer add up. They combine on one page, not on one tag.
- `performance.md`: the cost table above, the cap, "mark every slow component, not fast ones", "a marked loop is a smell: one query in the page".
- `components.md`: a short note on data fetching in components, with the race rules (props by reference, `locals` fields, `ctx.Request.Body`) and `go test -race`.
- `coming-from-astro.md`: Astro overlaps awaiting siblings automatically; Orbit needs `server:concurrent` on them.
- `reference/runtime-api.md`: `Concurrent`, `CtxOf` and `WithConcurrencyLimit` as generated-code-only functions, like `Defer`.
- `docs/DECISIONS.md`: the "decided by rule" items above. Editors: directive completion in the LSP, and the VS Code grammar and docs Chroma lexer if they list directives.

#### Out of scope
- Automatic concurrency (rejected above: it charges every component).
- Streaming or flushing early (responses stay buffered, §4); `server:defer` is the first-byte tool.
- Running unmarked siblings of a marked component concurrently (Astro's "everything after the first async child"): only marked components leave the calling goroutine.
- A component-level marker (Q2 b), until asked for.
- Cancellation (if Q4 a), and a process-wide cap.
- Making frontmatter code itself concurrent (`await`-style); users can use goroutines inside a frontmatter as in any Go code.

#### Risks
- **Data races in user code** that ran safely in order (props by reference, `locals` fields). Mitigation: opt-in, the docs' rules, `-race` advice; Orbit's own state is per goroutine or synchronized, and its tests run under `-race`.
- **Side effects after an error**: components after a failing one may already have run (above). Documented.
- **Overuse**: marking fast components costs ~0.4 µs each; marking a loop hits the cap. The docs and the cost table say when not to.

#### Implementation
**Files**: runtime `concurrent.go` (`Concurrent`, `CtxOf`, `WithConcurrencyLimit`, fork, join, assembly), `Writer` fields and `HeadEnd` (`writer.go`), the join in `servePage`, `serveIsland`, `RenderComponent`, `testRender` (orbittest) and `RenderHTML`; compiler `ServerConcurrent` and `Component.Concurrent` (`ast.go`, `validate.go`), `Analysis.CtxRefs`/`CtxUnknown` (`analyze_impl.go`), the call site, `LocalsOf` and `ctxPrologue` (`codegen_template.go`, `codegen_gen.go`); LSP completion (`internal/lsp/complete.go`). The VS Code grammar (`server:*` generically) and the docs Chroma lexer (no directive list) needed no change; neither did `orbit fmt` (directives are plain attributes there). Tests: `concurrent_test.go` (every case of the Tests list, with and without `-race`, a stress test, the pool-count check through a test-only fork hook), codegen fixture `testdata/codegen/concurrent` (hook, direct, `<Self>`, an SCC Ping ↔ Pong, a page callee, `client:idle`, shadowed `ctx`; built and run in both flavors, through a `Site` and under `NewWriter`, same HTML), `locals` (`LocalsOf` before the call) and `tolerant` fixtures, `errors_directives` parse golden, `orbittest/testdata/project` (`/concurrent` vs `/sequential` through `orbittest.Get` and `Render`, 2 × 30 ms components under 60 ms, headers merged), LSP completion tests.

**How it works**: `Writer` got four fields: `ctx` (the owning `Ctx`, also set on root writers by the entry points, so `CtxOf` is one load and a branch), `limit` (`int32`; 0 = inline, set by the entry points from the `Site`), `grp` (`any` holding a `*group`: a `sync.WaitGroup`, the running count, the abort flag; from a pool, on the first fork only) and `holes`. Fork, join and assembly are plain functions, not `Writer` methods, and `grp` is an `any`: methods and typed fields put `sync.WaitGroup` and the atomics into the export data of every generated package (measured +3.6 KB per decl package with methods; +0.5 KB, 0.5%, as shipped: the field names and the `hole` type). `reset` clears the four fields. A child is a pooled `Writer`, buffer and `Ctx` (a copy of the request fields, locals, params slice and keys; empty status and headers); its stack is a copy of the parent's, and it inherits `capture`.

**Deviations and choices** (logged in `docs/DECISIONS.md`, "Concurrent rendering"):
- The runtime names are **`Concurrent`** and **`WithConcurrencyLimit`** (the sketch said `Parallel`/`WithParallelLimit`), matching the directive. `WithConcurrencyLimit(0)` renders every marked component inline, a negative value means no limit (`errgroup.SetLimit`'s convention). The limit counts goroutines running now.
- **`RenderHTML` captures join their own holes** before returning, so the captured HTML is complete; a capture has its own group and its own limit count (sharing the request's group would deadlock: a capture inside a marked component would wait on itself). Its marked components' status and headers merge into the capture's `ctx`.
- `RenderComponent` and `orbittest` use the default limit (they have no `Site`).
- **Inside an attribute value** (`Attr` formats a `Renderer` into a scratch buffer), `Concurrent` renders inline: the attribute path sets `limit` to 0 while formatting.
- Status and headers merge on failure too (so a redirect keeps the headers set by marked components).
- `CtxOf` is emitted when slot content contains a component call (any depth) or references the implicit `ctx`; `ctx := orbit__ctx` is added for the latter, with `; _ = ctx` when every reference is inside a nested slot (and in Tolerant mode, which also gets `_ = orbit__ctx`). Implicit-`ctx` references come from the analysis' body file (unresolved `ctx` identifiers in the template part, mapped back to `.orbit` offsets); when the code doesn't parse, a word match is the fallback.
- `src/locals` is imported by a file with a marked call site even when it doesn't use `locals`, for the `LocalsOf` before the call.

**Measured** (Ryzen 7 9700X, 16 threads, Go 1.27.3, medians of 6, load average 1–3):
- Runtime (`BenchmarkConcurrent`, full `Site.Handler` requests, `-cpu 1`): 3 small cards 0.17 µs, 1 alloc → 1.0 µs, 4 allocs (288 B); 20 cards 0.45 → 4.2 µs, 17 allocs (16 forked, 4 inline); 500 cards with the limit 8.1 → 19.6 µs, 17 allocs; without a limit 123 µs, 501 allocs, 40 KB; a ~34 KB page with 2 marked cards 3.9 → 5.0 µs. With 16 threads within 10%, except the 500-card loop (37 µs). So ≈ 0.3 µs and 1 allocation (~80 B, the goroutine's arguments) per marked instance. A page with one marked component serves with 2 allocations (`TestConcurrentServeAllocs`).
- `CtxOf` on 500 capturing slots (`BenchmarkCtxOf`): 11.51 vs 11.49 µs, 0 allocations either way.
- Unmarked path: `TestRenderAllocs` 0 and `TestServeAllocs` 1, unchanged. `bench/go` (build flavor, interleaved A/B, 6 runs each): render 48.9 → 50.4 µs (+3%), serve `/cards` 49.5 → 50.7 µs (+2.5%), serve `/post/hello-world` 354.6 → 343.8 ns (−3%). The cards page gains only one `CtxOf` (its layout slot) and its per-card path is unchanged, so the +3% is most likely code layout; reported as measured.
- Dev loop (`bench/devloop -dev -kinds body,props`, 200 files): body 173 → 174 ms, props 185 → 186 ms (binary ready), within noise. Export data of a generated decl package +0.5 KB (0.5%), impl package +0.14 KB.


## 4. Runtime package (`github.com/orbit-z/orbit`, repo root)
The runtime package depends only on the standard library (plus the framework's own stdlib-only `internal/islandcrypto`, §3.4, §10).
```go
type Ctx struct {
    Request *http.Request
    URL     *url.URL        // in a deferred island: the island request's URL; the page URL is Request.Referer() (§5b)
    Site    *url.URL        // from config
    // unexported: route params (pooled name/value storage the router fills), locals (any, holding *L),
    // status, header, dev flag
}
// Ctx is pooled per request: don't retain it after the request ends.
func (c *Ctx) Param(name string) string  // route param by its bracket name ("slug" for [slug], "path" for [...path]);
                                         // "" when the route has no such param, like Request.PathValue. Pages
                                         // use the typed params (§2.2); components and generic code use Param.
// Typed locals (§5b). Generated code calls LocalsOf; users call EnsureLocals, WithLocals and LocalsFromRequest.
func LocalsOf[L any](c *Ctx) *L                                 // lazily allocates new(L) on first use
func EnsureLocals[L any](r *http.Request) (*http.Request, *L)   // middleware: the attached *L, or attaches a new(L);
                                                                // returns r unchanged when one is already attached
func WithLocals[L any](r *http.Request, l *L) *http.Request       // attaches a given *L
func LocalsFromRequest[L any](r *http.Request) *L                // custom handlers: the attached *L, or nil if none
type NoLocals struct{}                                         // used when there's no src/locals.Locals
func (c *Ctx) SetStatus(code int)
func (c *Ctx) Header() http.Header
func (c *Ctx) Redirect(to string, code int) error   // returns *RedirectError
var ErrNotFound error
type RedirectError struct { Location string; Code int }
func (e *RedirectError) Error() string

type HTML    string                                // trusted markup, written raw
type URL     string                                // trusted URL, skips scheme filtering
type Attrs   map[string]any

type Writer struct { /* writes straight into a pooled *bytes.Buffer; sticky error; depth counter; component stack */ }
func NewWriter(buf *bytes.Buffer) *Writer
func (w *Writer) Raw(s string)
func (w *Writer) Expr(v any)               // fallback; escaping rules below
func (w *Writer) String(s string)          // typed writes, chosen by codegen (§3.3)
func (w *Writer) Int(n int64)
func (w *Writer) Uint(n uint64)
func (w *Writer) Float(f float64)
func (w *Writer) Float32(f float32)         // formatted with bitSize 32 (0.1)
func (w *Writer) Bool(b bool)
func (w *Writer) HTML(h HTML)              // also set:html; the param type rejects plain strings
func (w *Writer) JSON(pos string, v any)   // set:json: encoding/json + script-data escaping (</script>, <!--, U+2028/9); errors carry pos
func (w *Writer) JSONAttr(pos string, v JSONValue) // orbit.JSON attribute value (single-quoted pieces, §5c)
func (w *Writer) Bytes(b []byte)           // escaped, as a string
func (w *Writer) Error(e error)
func (w *Writer) Stringer(s fmt.Stringer)
func (w *Writer) Render(r Renderer)
func (w *Writer) Attr(name string, v any)  // fallback
func (w *Writer) AttrString(name, s string)
func (w *Writer) AttrURL(name, s string)   // scheme-filtered
func (w *Writer) URL(s string)             // a URL attribute's value only (codegen writes name=" and " as Raw): escaped, scheme-filtered
func (w *Writer) AttrBool(name string, b bool)
func (w *Writer) Spread(a Attrs)
func (w *Writer) SpreadRest(a *RestAttrs, withClass bool) // pass-through attributes, §2.5
func (w *Writer) RestClass(a *RestAttrs)
func (w *Writer) HeadEnd()                 // marks the asset-injection offset (§3.3)
func (w *Writer) Err() error

func (w *Writer) Enter(component string) error  // depth guard, always on: error past depth 1000, naming the stack
func (w *Writer) Leave()

type Renderer func(w *Writer) error
type NamedSlot struct { Name string; Render Renderer }
type Slots struct {               // passed by value; the zero value means "no children" (no allocation)
    Default Renderer
    Named   []NamedSlot
}
func (s Slots) Get(name string) Renderer   // "default" → Default; otherwise a linear scan of Named
func (s Slots) Has(name string) bool
func RenderHTML(ctx *Ctx, r Renderer) (HTML, error)  // render r to a string, e.g. a slot for a fragment cache (§4d)

type Asset struct { Kind string; URL string; ID string }  // Kind: "css" | "js" | "lazy" (ID: the component ID whose marker loads it, §5) | "modulepreload" (a chunk the page's module imports, §5e)
type Route struct {
    Pattern     string                      // "/blog/[slug]"
    File        string
    Render      func(*Ctx, *Writer) error   // generated adapter: calls the page's Render with zero Props (its wrapper fills Params, §3.2)
    Assets      []Asset                     // css/js/modulepreload URLs for head injection (in that order of tags); lazy entries for the loader's map
    Islands     bool                        // the page's graph has a server:defer call site: inject the islands loader (§5)
}

// Deferred islands (server:defer, §3.4, §5b)
type Island struct { /* ID, File, decode-and-render closure */ }
func NewIsland[P any](id, file string, render func(*Ctx, *Writer, P, Slots) error) Island  // generated in orbit_gen/app
func Defer(c *Ctx, w *Writer, id string, props any, fallback Renderer) error              // generated call sites: seal, write placeholder

// Concurrent rendering (server:concurrent, §3.6)
func Concurrent[P any](c *Ctx, w *Writer, render func(*Ctx, *Writer, P, Slots) error, props P, slots Slots) error // generated call sites
func CtxOf(w *Writer, c *Ctx) *Ctx                                                       // generated at the start of slot content

// Concurrent loads in a frontmatter (§4f), called by users
func All(c *Ctx, loads ...func(context.Context) error) error // first error in time, positioned at its load; panics re-panicked

type Option func(*siteOptions)
type Site struct { /* routes, segment trie, islands, island cipher, manifest, options */ }
func NewSite(routes []Route, islands []Island, opts ...Option) *Site  // called by generated orbit_gen/app.New()
func WithStaticDir(dir string) Option               // precedence (decided by rule: simplest, so orbit dev keeps working): $ORBIT_STATIC > WithStaticDir > embedded FS (--embed) > dist/client next to the binary
func WithStaticFS(fsys fs.FS) Option                // used automatically by --embed builds (§6 step 6)
func WithKey(key []byte) Option                     // 32-byte island key; overrides $ORBIT_KEY and the build key
func WithSite(rawURL string) Option                 // absolute site URL for ctx.Site; app.New() passes orbit.json's "site" first, so a caller's option wins; panics if not absolute
func WithErrorHandler(h func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, err error) bool) Option // answers 500-bound render errors first (§4c)
func WithConcurrencyLimit(n int) Option             // server:concurrent components running at once per render (default 16; 0 inline, < 0 no limit; §3.6)

func (s *Site) Middleware(next http.Handler) http.Handler  // THE integration point (§5b)
func (s *Site) Handler() http.Handler                      // = Middleware(NotFound())
func (s *Site) NotFound() http.Handler                     // renders 404.orbit
func (s *Site) ServeError(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, err error) // answers err as a page's would be (§4c)
func RenderComponent[P any](w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request,
    render func(*Ctx, *Writer, P, Slots) error, props P) error   // HTML fragment, e.g. for htmx: buffered; on success
                                                                  // 200, text/html; charset=utf-8, Content-Length; on
                                                                  // error nothing is written; locals come from r

func Addr() string                         // $ORBIT_ADDR, else ":"+$PORT, else ":4321"
func ListenAndServe(h http.Handler) error  // listens on Addr(); logs "orbit: listening on http://localhost:4321" (bound address;
                                           // no host → localhost) via log, except under ORBIT_DEV=1;
                                           // graceful shutdown on SIGINT/SIGTERM, then returns nil
                                           // (call it as `if err := orbit.ListenAndServe(h); err != nil { log.Fatal(err) }`:
                                           // log.Fatal exits 1 even for nil).
                                           // ReadHeaderTimeout 10s, IdleTimeout 120s, no Read/WriteTimeout,
                                           // 5s shutdown grace period. No command-line flags, so it never
                                           // clashes with the user's own.
```

**Component testing (`github.com/orbit-z/orbit/orbittest`)**, so users can unit-test components and pages with plain `go test`:
```go
func Render[P any](t testing.TB, render func(*orbit.Ctx, *orbit.Writer, P, orbit.Slots) error,
    props P, opts ...Option) string                            // returns the HTML; t.Fatal on render error
func Get(t testing.TB, h http.Handler, path string, opts ...Option) *httptest.ResponseRecorder  // e.g. site.Handler(); no redirect following
func WithSlot(name, html string) Option                      // "default" or a named slot (raw HTML)
func WithLocals[L any](l *L) Option
func WithRequest(r *http.Request) Option                    // cookies, headers, query
func WithParam(name, value string) Option                   // sets one route param (ctx.Param, a page's params); repeatable
func Snapshot(t testing.TB, name, html string)              // compares with testdata/<name>.golden; -update rewrites;
                                                            // a missing golden file fails (hint: -update)
```
- Tests import a component through its declaration package (`card "example.com/blog/orbit_gen/decl/components/card"`) and link everything with `import _ "example.com/blog/orbit_gen/app"`. Without that import, the `Render` wrapper panics with its "not linked" message (§3.1), and `orbittest` repeats the hint.
- Tests live in normal Go packages (e.g. `internal/views_test/`, or next to `cmd/server`), not in `src/`.
- `orbittest` renders with the same pooled `Writer` and escaping code as SSR, so the output equals production output.
  - **`Render`** calls the component's `Render` directly, with `httptest.NewRequest("GET", "/", nil)` unless `WithRequest` is given. No middleware runs and **no head injection** happens (a component on its own has no page assets, like `RenderComponent`). `server:defer` call sites write their placeholder, sealed with a **fixed test island key and the build ID `0000000000000000`**, so placeholders are identical on every run and snapshot tests of pages with islands are stable (decided by coordinator: simplest).
  - **Test detection (milestone 8, user decision)**: `testing.Testing()`; measured +38 KB on a minimal server binary (+68 KB on a small `net/http` program), and it registers no test flags.
  - **`Get`** sends a GET through the given handler, so routing, the user's middleware and head injection all apply, and islands use that site's own key and build ID. Under `go test`, `app.New()` uses the same fixed test key and build ID `0000000000000000` as `Render` (detected with `testing.Testing()`, decided by rule: consistent with `Render`), unless `ORBIT_KEY` or `WithKey` is set, so `Get` snapshots of pages with islands are stable too. Only `WithLocals` (attached to the request, so `EnsureLocals` returns it) and `WithRequest` (a copy with method GET and URL `path`) apply to `Get`.
  - **`WithParam`** applies to `Render` only (with `Get`, the router sets params from the path). It's repeatable, one call per param, and a later call for the same name replaces the earlier value (decided by rule: Go stdlib, like `http.Request.SetPathValue(name, value)`; replaces the removed `WithParams(orbit.Params)`). A page rendered with `Render` gets its typed `params` from these values through its `Render` wrapper (§3.2); a param the page's route doesn't have is ignored by `params` but still visible to `ctx.Param`.
  - An option passed to a function it doesn't apply to fails the test rather than being ignored (decided by rule: simplest).
  - `orbittest` owns the `-update` flag; a test package that imports it must not define its own (`flag.Lookup("update")` reads it).
- **HTTP layer**: stdlib `net/http` only, with no web framework dependency.
  - Orbit's file-based router is its own matcher, because Go's `ServeMux` can't express `[...rest]` and Astro's priority rules.
  - **Method rule**: static files (`/_orbit/*` assets, `public/` files) answer **GET and HEAD only**; pages answer **all methods** (§5b). The island endpoint `/_orbit/island/…` answers GET, HEAD and POST; any other method gets a 405 with `Allow: GET, HEAD, POST` (decided by rule: Astro also answers 405, and `ServeMux` sets `Allow`).
  - Static files are served with `http.ServeContent` (ETag, Range). `/_orbit/*` files have hashed names and get `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (dev: `no-cache`).
  - Page responses get `Content-Type: text/html; charset=utf-8` unless the page set one (`ctx.Header()`).
  - Everything is a standard `http.Handler` or middleware, so it composes with chi/Gin/Echo and any ecosystem middleware.
- **`Expr` escaping**, checked in this order against the value's *exact* dynamic type (a `type Slug string` isn't `string` and falls through to `fmt.Sprint`; typed writes mirror this, §3.3):
  - `nil` → nothing
  - `HTML` → raw
  - `string`, `URL` → HTML-escaped
  - `[]byte` → HTML-escaped as a string
  - ints, floats, bools → strconv (`float32` with bitSize 32, so `float32(0.1)` prints `0.1`)
  - `Renderer` / `func(*Writer) error` → called
  - `error` → escaped `Error()`
  - `fmt.Stringer` → escaped string
  - slices of these → each element
  - anything else → `fmt.Sprint`, escaped
- **`Attr`** (proposed: Astro's rule for booleans on attributes that aren't HTML boolean attributes, §2.6 (Q3)):
  - `true` → bare attribute
  - `false` / `nil` → omitted
  - anything else → attribute-escaped and quoted
  - **URL attributes** (`action`, `background`, `cite`, `codebase`, `data`, `formaction`, `href`, `icon`, `longdesc`, `manifest`, `poster`, `profile`, `src`, `usemap`, `xmlns`: `html/template`'s list, plus `xlink:href`; decided by rule: Go stdlib): a value whose scheme, after trimming whitespace and control characters and ignoring case, is `javascript:` or `vbscript:` is replaced with `about:invalid#orbit`, unless it has type `orbit.URL`. This applies to `Attr`, `AttrURL` and `Spread`.
- A slice in an attribute value is formatted as in content (elements concatenated, no separator).
- **`Spread`**: each attribute name must match `[A-Za-z_:][-A-Za-z0-9_:.]*`; an invalid name (spaces, quotes, `>`, `/`, `=`) is a render error. Entries are written sorted by name, so output is stable, at the spread's position. Duplicates of attributes written on the same element aren't merged: both are written, and browsers use the first.
- **Buffered responses**: SSR responses are always buffered, never streamed. A page renders into a `sync.Pool`'d `bytes.Buffer`; then the response gets `Content-Length` and is written once. Middleware that must rewrite a response wraps the `http.ResponseWriter` in standard net/http style (§5b).
- **Head injection**: the page's `<link rel=stylesheet>` and `<script type=module>` tags, then a `<link rel=modulepreload>` per shared chunk of the module (§5e), and the loader, are built **once per route** in `NewSite` (per request in dev with a dev manifest) and written by the first `HeadEnd()` (the first `</head>`) during the render, so the page body is never moved (milestone 9: −10% on a small page with assets). With no marker, they go right after a leading `<!DOCTYPE …>` (never before it, which would trigger quirks mode), otherwise at the start. Injection happens before the response is written, so wrapping middleware sees the final HTML. Pages that need it also get the inline loader and its `display:contents` style here (§5).
- **Router**: routes are compiled into a segment trie (no regexp): static, `[param]`, and `[...rest]` (matches 0+ segments). Priority is static, then param, then rest; ties go to more segments, except that an exact route beats a rest param matching zero segments (`/docs` beats `/docs/[...rest]` at `/docs`; decided by rule: Astro). `index.orbit` is the directory root, and `404.orbit` is used for not-found.
  - **`_` files aren't routes** (decided by rule: Astro): a file under `src/pages/` whose name, or a directory on its path, starts with `_` is a component (`src/pages/blog/_parts/Byline.orbit`). So `src/pages/_orbit/…` is never a route, and no page can sit under the reserved `/_orbit/` prefix; the runtime router still rejects such a pattern at startup.
  - Malformed brackets are build errors at the file: `invalid route pattern "/[slug": segment "[slug" has unbalanced brackets`, `invalid route pattern "/blog/[]": segment "[]" has an empty parameter name` (decided by rule: simplest).
  - Param values are percent-decoded, like `ServeMux`'s `PathValue`; a rest param is the matched segments joined with `/`, and `""` when it matches none.
  - **Param storage**: the router writes each match into the pooled `Ctx`'s param storage (a reused slice of name/value pairs: names are the route's own strings, values are substrings of the request path), read back with `ctx.Param`. There's no map, so a param match allocates nothing (a map cost 2 allocs / 336 B); only a value that needs percent-decoding allocates its decoded string. The storage is reset when the `Ctx` goes back to the pool.
  - Route param names must match `[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_-]*` and give distinct `Params` field names; both are build errors at the file (§2.2).
  - **Unsupported route shapes are build errors** at the file (user decision for the MVP): a segment that mixes text and a parameter (`post-[id].orbit`, `[lang]-[version].orbit`), and a rest param that isn't the last segment (`[...path]/edit.orbit`). Both messages suggest a whole-segment `[param]`: `src/pages/blog/post-[id].orbit: route segment "post-[id]" mixes text and a parameter; use a whole-segment parameter such as [id].orbit and parse it in the page`.
  - Two files with the same route (`blog.orbit` and `blog/index.orbit`, or `[slug].orbit` and `[id].orbit` in one directory) are a build error naming both files.
  - Without a `404.orbit`, not-found responses are a 404 with an empty body.
- **Trailing slashes: none** by default (user decision; `orbit.json` `"trailingSlash": "always"` is the mirror image, §4e, implemented: `/blog/hello` gets a 308 to `/blog/hello/`). A page's canonical URL has no trailing slash: `/blog/hello`, and `blog/index.orbit` is `/blog`. `/` stays `/`. The router answers `/blog/hello/` with a **308 to `/blog/hello`**, keeping the query string (`/search/?q=go` → `/search?q=go`), as `net/http`'s `ServeMux` does for its redirects. Same in dev and prod. Paths for files (`/_orbit/…`, `public/` files) are never redirected.
  - The redirect is issued only when the path without its trailing slash matches a page route; otherwise the request falls through to `next` untouched, so a user route such as `/api/items/` keeps working in Orbit-first setups. *Decided by rule* (simplest option that never steals the user's routes; log it in `docs/DECISIONS.md`).
- **Errors**: panics are recovered per request.
  - Prod (user decision): a minimal built-in page (`500 Internal Server Error`), never showing the error. The error and its `.orbit` position are logged to stderr with the `log` package (`orbit: GET /blog/hello: src/pages/blog/[slug].orbit:14:5: …`): for a returned error, the position of its frontmatter `return` (§2.2, `orbit.ErrorAt`); for a panic, the innermost `.orbit` line on the stack. A custom `src/pages/500.orbit` comes after the MVP (§12). An app-supplied error handler (`WithErrorHandler`) answers first (§4c, implemented).
  - Dev: error overlay with file:line.
- **Locals helpers**: `EnsureLocals` is what middleware uses. It returns the attached `*L` (and `r` unchanged), or attaches a `new(L)` with `WithLocals` and returns the new request, so every middleware in the chain can call it and they all share one value. `LocalsFromRequest` returns `nil` when nothing is attached, rather than a detached zero value whose writes would be silently lost (decided by rule: Go stdlib convention; context accessors such as `httptrace.ContextClientTrace` return nil when absent). Pages never see nil: `LocalsOf` allocates. `WithLocals` on a request that already carries locals shadows them for handlers further down only, like `context.WithValue`; middleware that ran earlier keeps its pointer (decided by rule: Go stdlib).
- **Island key** (resolved once, in `NewSite`), in this order:
  1. `WithKey`, if given.
  2. `$ORBIT_KEY`: standard base64 of exactly 32 bytes. Anything else panics at startup with a clear message (decided by rule: Go stdlib convention for constructors without an error result, like `ServeMux.Handle` with a bad pattern).
  3. The key that `orbit build` embeds in the binary: fresh random bytes per build, set with `-ldflags=-X` (§6), so no key is written to `orbit_gen/` (which stays deterministic) or to the repo. Astro also generates a key per build and embeds it.
  4. Otherwise (plain `go build` or `go run`), a random key per process, with a one-line warning on stderr.

  All instances of one build share the embedded key, so horizontally scaled instances open each other's tokens. A new build has a new key, so during a **rolling deploy**, islands on pages from the old version get 400s from new instances and keep their fallback. Production deploys with rolling updates (or several builds behind one load balancer) need a stable `ORBIT_KEY`, created with `orbit create-key` (§6). Document this in `deploy.md` and `lazy-loading.md`. `RenderComponent` fragments seal with the same key resolution, minus `WithKey`.
- **Build ID**: 16 hex chars (64 random bits), embedded by `orbit build` with `-ldflags=-X` next to the key, or random per process otherwise (fixed to `0000000000000000` under `go test`, per `testing.Testing()`). It's part of every island URL path (`/_orbit/island/<buildID>/<id>`, §5b) and of the associated data (§3.4), so each deploy gets fresh island URLs (future cache keys, §12). The endpoint accepts any build ID that authenticates, not only its own, so with a stable `ORBIT_KEY`, rolling deploys keep working (decided by rule: simplest option that doesn't break rolling deploys).
- **Loader**: the client loader (§5) is TypeScript in the framework repo (`loader/`). `go generate` minifies it with esbuild into a committed Go string constant, so the runtime embeds it with no dependency.

## 4b. Performance design
The SSR hot path is built for speed. There's no fixed target number; the results are measured and published (see Benchmarks below).
- **Static HTML is precomputed**: adjacent static text and attributes are merged into one `Raw` string constant per run (§3.3), so most of a page is a few `copy`s.
- **Static attributes are pre-escaped** at compile time, including the scope attribute.
- **Zero-alloc escaping**: the escaper scans for characters that need escaping and, if there are none, writes the original string directly.
- **No bufio layering**: the `Writer` writes straight into the pooled `bytes.Buffer` (§4).
- **No reflection on the hot path**: typed writes (§3.3) cover expressions; `Expr`'s type switch handles the common types, and reflection appears only in its last-resort `fmt.Sprint` and generic-slice cases.
- **Slots are a value struct**, not a map: `Default` plus a small `Named` slice. A call with no children allocates nothing, and neither does slot content that uses no parent variables (a static func value). Slot content that captures parent variables (`<Card>{p.Title}</Card>`) allocates one closure per such slot, because the call goes through the decl package's hook and escapes. **Revised (user decision, 2026-10-26; implemented, §3.5)**: `orbit build` calls components directly outside import cycles (a build flavor through `go build -overlay`), keeping the closures on the stack, while `orbit dev`, `go test` and plain `go build` keep the hooks so a component body edit doesn't recompile its parents. Measured (500 capturing slots, `GOMAXPROCS=1`): hook 13.0 µs, 500 allocs, 24 KB; direct 7.6 µs, 0 allocs; `bench/go`'s 500-card page 57.8 → 50.2 µs, 501 → 0 allocs (§3.5, Implementation).
- **Zero allocations in Orbit's own render path** is a tested rule (like the router's 0-alloc match): from milestone 5, `testing.AllocsPerRun` tests assert that a typical page (typed writes, static or no slot content, no locals, no `server:defer`) renders with 0 allocations in Orbit's code, and is served with exactly 1: the `Content-Length` value (string and `[]string` in one 48-byte object), which can't be pooled because `net/http` may read header values after the handler returns. Measured (milestone 5): render 0 allocs, serve 1 alloc / 48 B, about 200 ns for a small param page. The floor that remains is `net/http`'s own: measured on Go 1.27.3 at about 14 allocs (1.4 KB) per request for an empty handler and about 19 (2.2 KB) for a 1 KB page; a test measures Orbit's handler on top of that with a reused `ResponseWriter`. What may still allocate: locals (`new(L)`, and `EnsureLocals`' context copy; not pooled, since a retained `locals` would then be silently reused), slot closures that capture, the `Expr` fallback and user `String()`/`Error()` methods, percent-decoded params, `server:defer` props, one allocation per `server:concurrent` instance (§3.6), `orbit.JSON` values (encoding/json's own allocations only: boxing a non-pointer value, and its internal copy of a non-addressable one; Orbit's reused per-Writer encoder adds 0, asserted by `TestJSONAttrAllocs`, §5c), and user code.
- **Response headers** use shared, read-only `[]string` values for constant headers (`Content-Type: text/html; charset=utf-8`) instead of `Header().Set`, saving one allocation per request (measured 20.7 → 18.9 allocs/request).
- **Route matching** walks a precompiled segment trie, with no regexp. Matched params go into the pooled `Ctx`'s storage, not a map, so a param match is **0 allocations** (it was 2 allocs / 336 B with `map[string]string`); a page's typed `Params` is a stack value built from `ctx.Param` calls (§3.2).
- **`Ctx` is pooled** per request (with its `Writer` and buffer).
- **Locals are allocated lazily**, on the first `LocalsOf`.
- **gzip is left to the user's middleware**; Orbit never compresses.
- **`server:defer` keeps slow parts off the first byte.** Responses are always buffered (§4), so a page's first byte waits for its slowest part. `server:defer` is the way to keep slow parts (DB queries, third-party APIs) from delaying it: the page renders the fallback and is sent at once, and the slow component renders in its own island request (§3.4, §5b). Cost on the page: one `json.Marshal` (reflection, off the template hot path) and one seal (HMAC-SHA256 plus AES-GCM, about 1 µs for 1 KB of props, §3.4) per deferred instance, plus the inline loader (a few hundred bytes). Pages without islands pay nothing. Documented in `rendering.md`, `performance.md` and `lazy-loading.md`.
- **`client:*` markers are static**: the `<orbit-lazy>` wrappers are folded into `Raw` constants (§3.4), so lazy scripts cost nothing at render time.
- **The exported runtime types stay light for importers** (dev-loop compile time): every generated package mentions `*orbit.Ctx`, and the compiler loads the type graph it reaches, including unexported fields and method signatures, and compiles any generic instantiation found there (with its type descriptors) again in each importer. So `Ctx`'s and `Site`'s keyring holds the `*islandcrypto.Sealer` as `any` (read with the `sealerOf` function, not a method), which keeps its `sync.Pool`'s `atomic.Pointer[poolChainElt]` out of every generated package: a package mentioning `*orbit.Ctx` went from 196 symbols to 6 (object 149 → 94 KB, mostly export data). Same allocations and island benchmark numbers.

**Benchmarks** (results published in `docs/performance.md`; no CI regression gates):
- **Head-to-head vs. Astro**: two dedicated, mirrored sites, `bench/orbit-site` and `bench/astro-site` (kept separate from `examples/blog` so the example stays clean and the comparison is easy to audit). The Astro one is an equivalent project served by `@astrojs/node` in standalone mode, as a single process (no cluster, no Bun). Scenarios, driven by a load tester such as `oha`:
  1. a render-heavy page: a list of 500 cards with nested components;
  2. a realistic page with a simulated 10 ms data fetch in the frontmatter;
  3. Orbit run at `GOMAXPROCS=1` and at all cores, to show per-core and whole-machine numbers.
  4. the 10 ms-fetch page with the slow part moved into a `server:defer` component (`src/pages/fetch-deferred.orbit`; Astro's mirror uses `server:defer` too): page **TTFB** (p50/p99), plus the island request's latency on its own.

  All benchmark pages render per request in both sites (`src/pages/cards.orbit`, `src/pages/fetch.orbit` and `src/pages/fetch-deferred.orbit` in `bench/orbit-site`; the `.astro` mirrors in `bench/astro-site` use `output: 'server'`).
- **Metrics**: req/s, p50/p99 latency, TTFB (scenario 4), RSS.
- **Go comparison** (`bench/go`): Orbit vs. templ vs. `html/template` rendering an equivalent page, as Go benchmarks.
- **Micro-benchmarks** for the `Writer` and the escaper (`b.Loop()`, `b.ReportAllocs()`) live next to the runtime as dev tools.

## 4c. Error → response hook
**Status: Implemented** (2026-10-26; accepted the same day). User answers, as recommended: (Q1) a `Site` option, `orbit.WithErrorHandler(func(w, r, err) bool)`; (Q2) only errors that would become the built-in 500 reach it, recovered panics included; (Q3) `site.ServeError(w, r, err)` for custom handlers.

**Implementation notes**: the handler is called from `renderFailed`'s default case (pages, the 404 page, `ServeError`) and from `islandFailed`'s (islands, after setting the two island headers on `w`; only when a handler is set, so island 500s without one are unchanged). `panicError` gained `Unwrap`. `ServeError(w, r, nil)` writes nothing, and treats any request as a page request (decided by rule: simplest; `DECISIONS.md` "Error hook"). The success path is untouched: one nil check on the failure path. Tests: `errorhandler_test.go` (pages, nested components, `errors.Join`, panics, control flow, the 404 page's own failure, HEAD, dev, last option wins, islands with the 400/413 and undecodable props, `ServeError` with `RenderComponent`), `orbittest.Get` through a site with the option, and the orbittest integration project (`app.New(orbit.WithErrorHandler(…))`). No deviations.

**Why**: in `nyth`, every catalogue or reader call (16 `catalog` and 19 `reader` uses in templates, plus layouts) can fail with one of three typed errors, from the page or from any component: `AccessRefused` becomes a 303 to `/signin/?next=…` or `/waiting/`, `CatalogUnavailable` a 503 with `Retry-After: 60` (and a log line), `ReaderBlocked` a 403 with a short text body. Astro lets middleware catch what the page threw (`try { return await next() } catch (e) { … }`, `nyth`'s `handleRequest`). In Orbit, a returned error that isn't `ErrNotFound` or a `*RedirectError` becomes the built-in 500 inside `site.Middleware` (§4), and middleware around it can't intercept, since the 500 has been written. Only the redirect has a workaround today (an error that `Unwrap`s to a `*orbit.RedirectError`).

#### End-to-end example
```go
// cmd/server/main.go
site := app.New(orbit.WithErrorHandler(answerError))

// answerError maps the app's data errors to responses. It returns false for
// any other error, which then gets Orbit's built-in 500.
func answerError(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, err error) bool {
	var refused *catalog.AccessRefused
	switch {
	case errors.As(err, &refused):
		http.Redirect(w, r, refused.Page(pageURL(r)), http.StatusSeeOther)
	case errors.Is(err, catalog.ErrUnavailable):
		log.Printf("%s %s: %v", r.Method, r.URL.Path, err) // "src/pages/fiction/[bookSlug].orbit:12:3: catalog: …"
		w.Header().Set("Retry-After", "60")
		http.Error(w, "The library is unavailable right now. Try again in a minute.", http.StatusServiceUnavailable)
	case errors.Is(err, reader.ErrBlocked):
		http.Error(w, "Requests from your network are refused.", http.StatusForbidden)
	default:
		return false
	}
	return true
}

// pageURL is the page the reader was on: for an island request, its Referer (§5b).
func pageURL(r *http.Request) string {
	if strings.HasPrefix(r.URL.Path, "/_orbit/island/") {
		return r.Referer()
	}
	return r.URL.RequestURI()
}
```
```orbit
---
// src/components/ChapterList.orbit
chapters, err := locals.Catalog.Chapters(ctx.Request.Context(), props.BookID)
if err != nil {
	return err // wrapped by ErrorAt with this position; errors.As still finds *catalog.AccessRefused
}
---
```

#### Rules
- **API**: `func WithErrorHandler(h func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, err error) bool) Option` ((Q1)). `true` means the handler wrote the response; `false` means Orbit answers as today (the built-in 500 with its log line; later `500.orbit`). Several `WithErrorHandler` options: the last wins (decided by rule: Go functional-options convention, like `WithKey`).
- **Which errors reach it** ((Q2)): every render failure that would otherwise become a 500, in pages (the 404 page included), deferred island requests and the `orbit dev` app; never `ErrNotFound` or a `*RedirectError` (found with `errors.As`/`errors.Is`, as today), which stay control flow. A recovered panic reaches it too, as Orbit's panic error (its text names the `.orbit` line; `Unwrap` returns the panic value when that is an `error`, a small addition so `errors.As` sees `panic(err)`). The island endpoint's own 400s and 413s (bad token, props that don't decode, body too big) aren't render errors and don't reach it.
- **Error identity**: `ErrorAt`'s position wrapper (§2.2) and component nesting keep `Unwrap`, so `errors.Is`/`errors.As` see the user's error through any depth, and `errors.Join` works. The handler's `err.Error()` starts with the `.orbit` position of the `return`, so a handler's own log line names it (no separate position API; decided by rule: simplest).
- **Response state**: nothing has been written when the handler runs (responses are buffered, §4): the page's buffer is dropped, so the handler can send any status, headers and body. Headers the page set with `ctx.Header()` are dropped as for the 500 (decided by rule: Astro, where a thrown error discards the page's response; a redirect keeps them, unchanged). Headers set on `w` by middleware before `next` stay. For an island request, Orbit sets `Cache-Control: private, no-store` and `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` on `w` before calling the handler, which may replace them (decided by rule: simplest, so an error answer isn't cached either). The loader keeps the fallback for any non-2xx answer (§5), a 303 included (`redirect: "manual"`).
- **`r`** is the request Orbit was serving: the page request, or the island request (its `URL` is the island URL; the page is in `Referer`, §5b). Locals attached by middleware are there (`orbit.LocalsFromRequest`).
- **Logging**: Orbit logs only errors it answers itself (the 500 line, §4). A handled error isn't logged; the handler decides (decided by rule: Go stdlib, like `httputil.ReverseProxy.ErrorHandler`).
- **Dev and prod behave the same**: the handler runs in `orbit dev` too; only unhandled errors show the dev error page (`<pre>` with the error and position, §4).
- **The handler runs inside `site.Middleware`** on the request's goroutine, concurrently across requests, and isn't recovered by Orbit (a panic in it goes to `net/http`'s recovery, decided by rule: Go stdlib). For `HEAD`, `net/http` drops the body as usual.
- **`RenderComponent`** (§4) keeps its contract: on error nothing is written and the error is returned, since it has no `Site`. For custom handlers that want the same answers as pages: (Q3).
- **Future `src/pages/500.orbit`** (§12): the order is handler → `500.orbit` → built-in page, so a handler that returns `false` for unknown errors composes with it, and `500.orbit` needs no hook of its own.

#### (Q) points
- **(Q1) Shape.**
  - (a) **Recommended**: the `Site` option above. One central function maps any error, including ones the app doesn't own (`context.DeadlineExceeded` → 504, a driver's error); it's the Go analogue of `nyth`'s catch around `next()`. Same shape as Echo's `HTTPErrorHandler` and `ReverseProxy.ErrorHandler` (plus the `bool` for fallback). The data package needs no `net/http` code.
  - (b) An interface errors implement, checked with `errors.As`: `interface{ error; ServeHTTPError(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) }` (or just `StatusCode() int`, rendered as a status-only answer). Zero config, but only for errors the app defines, it couples data packages to HTTP, and `StatusCode()` alone can't express `nyth`'s 303 with `next=` or a 503 body.
  - (c) Both: (a) first, then (b). More API for little gain; (b) can be added later without breaking (a).
  - (d) Middleware catches, like Astro: middleware attaches an error slot (`r, e := orbit.CatchErrors(r)`), Orbit writes nothing on failure, and the middleware answers after `next` returns. Closest to Astro's model, but forgetting to answer gives an empty 200, and every wrapping `ResponseWriter` between them must cope with "nothing written".
- **(Q2) What reaches the handler.**
  - (a) **Recommended**: only 500-bound errors, panics included (above). Redirects and not-found keep working however the handler is written.
  - (b) Also `ErrNotFound` and redirects (the handler could log or rewrite them). Easy to break the 404 page and `ctx.Redirect` by returning `true` by accident.
  - (c) Without panics: a panic is a bug, and `net/http` has no panic hook. But then a custom error page can't cover them until `500.orbit`.
- **(Q3) Answering errors from custom handlers.**
  - (a) **Recommended**: `func (s *Site) ServeError(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, err error)`: answers `err` exactly as a page's would be (redirect, `404.orbit`, the handler, the 500 with its log line). A handler then writes `if err := orbit.RenderComponent(w, r, …); err != nil { site.ServeError(w, r, err) }`. A few lines; it reuses `renderFailed`.
  - (b) Nothing new: the custom handler calls its own `answerError` and falls back to `http.Error`.

#### Cost
- Nothing on the success path, and nothing on the failure path without a handler (one nil check). With one, the cost is the handler's.
- No codegen, dev-loop or LSP change.

#### Tests (what must exist)
- A page and a nested component returning a typed error: the handler's 303/403/503 is sent with its headers and body; the page's buffer and `ctx.Header()` headers aren't.
- `errors.As` finds the user's error through `ErrorAt`, two levels of components and `errors.Join`.
- `false` gives the built-in 500 and the log line; `true` logs nothing.
- `ErrNotFound` and `ctx.Redirect` don't reach the handler; the 404 page's own failure does.
- A panic reaches it; `errors.As` finds a panicked `error`.
- Island request: the handler runs with the island request, after Orbit's default island headers; the island's 400/413 don't reach it.
- `HEAD`; dev mode (`ORBIT_DEV=1`) behaves the same; the last of two options wins.
- `ServeError` ((Q3)) gives the same answers as a page.
- `orbittest.Get` through a site built with the option.

#### User-docs changes (with the implementation)
- `rendering.md` (errors section): the handler, the order with the 500, logging, what reaches it.
- `middleware.md` and `coming-from-astro.md`: "catching what the page threw" becomes `WithErrorHandler`.
- `server.md`: `app.New(orbit.WithErrorHandler(…))` in `main.go`; `ServeError` in custom handlers.
- `lazy-loading.md`: islands and the handler (`Referer`, the fallback stays).
- `reference/runtime-api.md`: `WithErrorHandler`, `ServeError`, the panic error's `Unwrap`.
- `DECISIONS.md`: the decided-by-rule items above.

#### Out of scope
- `src/pages/500.orbit` (§12), which this composes with.
- Typed HTTP error helpers in the runtime (`orbit.HTTPError{Code, …}`); users define their own error types.
- `ErrorAt`-style positions as a separate API.

## 4d. Rendering a slot to a string
**Status: Implemented** (2026-10-26; accepted the same day). User answers: (Q1) `orbit.RenderHTML(ctx, r Renderer) (orbit.HTML, error)`; (Q2) a `</head>` inside a capture is a render error; (Q3) decided by the coordinator with the recommendation (rule: simplest): no Orbit helper for the per-user guard, a documented caching pattern.

**Implementation notes** (`render_html.go`): `Ctx` has an unexported `w *Writer`, set by `servePage`, `serveIsland`, `RenderComponent` and the `orbittest` hook (one pointer store per request); `Writer` has a `capture` flag that makes `HeadEnd` set the render error. The capture writer and buffer are pooled; its stack is a copy of the request writer's (pooled capacity, so no allocation after warm-up); `ctx.w` is restored by a `defer`, so after a panic too. A `nil` `ctx` works like a hand-built one (depth 0). Measured (`BenchmarkRenderHTML`, 2 KB of static markup): 166–200 ns, 1 alloc / 2 KB per miss; a hit (`w.HTML` of a cached value) 0 allocs (`TestRenderHTMLAllocs`). `TestRenderAllocs`/`TestServeAllocs` unchanged. Tests: `render_html_test.go` (in place vs captured with `class:list`, a `client:*` marker and a `server:defer` placeholder; depth through captures; nesting; writer restored after success, error and panic; `</head>`; allocations), `orbittest.Render` of a capturing component, and real `.orbit` files in the orbittest integration project (`Cacher`/`Rich`/`Recurse`: captured HTML equals in-place HTML; recursion through captures stops at 1000 with the stack error). No deviations.

**Why**: `nyth`'s `<Cacher key ttl store>` (34 uses) renders its default slot to an HTML string (`Astro.slots.render("default")`), keeps it in an in-memory fragment cache (cards 6 h, lists 1 h) and writes it with `set:html`. Today this works with `var b bytes.Buffer; slots.Default(orbit.NewWriter(&b))`, but a fresh `Writer` has an empty component stack (so the depth guard restarts at 0: runaway recursion through a captured slot overflows the Go stack), and what `</head>` or `server:defer` do inside it is undocumented.

#### End-to-end example
`src/components/Cacher.orbit`:
```orbit
---
import (
	"time"

	"example.com/nyth/internal/htmlcache"
)

type Props struct {
	Key   string        // everything the slot's HTML depends on, e.g. cardKey(book)
	TTL   time.Duration
	Store string        // "cards", "lists", "pages"
}

html, err := htmlcache.Store(props.Store).Get(props.Key, props.TTL, func() (orbit.HTML, error) {
	return orbit.RenderHTML(ctx, slots.Default)
})
if err != nil {
	return err
}
---
<Fragment set:html={html} />
```
A call site: `<Cacher key={ui.CardKey(book)} ttl={6 * time.Hour} store="cards"><NovelCard book={book} /></Cacher>`. On a hit the slot isn't run at all (in Astro, the slot is built hit or miss, a trap `nyth` documents).

#### Rules
- **API** ((Q1)): `func RenderHTML(ctx *Ctx, r Renderer) (HTML, error)` in the runtime. It renders `r` into a pooled buffer with a pooled `Writer` and returns a copy of the output as `orbit.HTML` (it's Orbit's own escaped output, so `set:html` takes it without a conversion). A nil `r` (no slot content) returns `""`. Works with any `Renderer`: `slots.Default`, `slots.Get("x")`, or a Go function.
- **The current writer**: `Ctx` gets an unexported pointer to the `Writer` rendering the request (set where the runtime pairs them: pages, islands, `RenderComponent`, `orbittest`). The capture's `Writer` starts with that writer's component stack, so the depth guard counts through captures (nested captures work, e.g. a list `<Cacher>` around card `<Cacher>`s), and while it runs the pointer names the capture writer, restored afterwards. A `Ctx` without one (built in a test by hand) starts at depth 0.
- **Errors**: the render's error is **returned**, not set as the page's sticky error, so the caller decides (`return err` hands it to the 500 or the error handler, §4c). On error the partial output is dropped. Panics aren't recovered here; the request's recovery handles them (decided by rule: simplest).
- **Head injection**: the page's asset tags are written by the page's own first `</head>` (§4), never inside a capture, so cached HTML never holds one page's tags. A `</head>` marker reached inside a capture is a render error: `orbit: </head> can't be inside orbit.RenderHTML; keep the document head outside the captured content` ((Q2)).
- **Assets need nothing**: slot content is compiled in the calling file, so every component it renders is imported there and its CSS and scripts are in every page's static graph that uses the caller (§5); scripts aren't part of the HTML. So the Astro trap of a cached copy missing its `<script>` (`nyth`'s `hasScriptTag` check) can't happen. `client:*` markers are static HTML and keep working in cached copies.
- **`server:defer` inside**: the placeholder holds a deterministic token (§3.4) for this build and key, so a cached placeholder stays valid for the process's lifetime and loads per user through the middleware: the way to put per-user parts inside cached HTML. An external cache shared across deploys must put the deploy in its key (a new build has a new build ID and, without a stable `ORBIT_KEY`, a new key).
- **Side effects run only on a miss**: `ctx.SetStatus`, `ctx.Header()` and `locals` writes inside captured content happen when it renders, not when a cached copy is served. Documented.
- **Safety: never cache per-user content.** The cache key must hold everything the HTML depends on, and the content must not read cookies, the session or per-user `locals`. Orbit doesn't enforce this ((Q3)); the docs give the pattern below.
- **`compressHTML`** and scoping are applied at build time as usual; the captured HTML is byte-identical to the same content rendered in place.

#### The documented caching pattern (`rendering.md`)
