- An in-memory cache keyed by everything the fragment depends on (data version, locale), with a TTL and a size cap, plus single-flight so concurrent misses render once. The render shares the leader request's `Ctx` only while it runs; nothing retains it.
- Per-user parts go outside the cached component, or inside it as `server:defer`.
- A shared-scope guard, if wanted, is app code: the cache component sets a flag on `locals` around `RenderHTML` (`locals.Shared = true; defer …`), and the app's per-user accessors refuse while it's set (`nyth`'s `assertNotShared`).
- Measure first: a hit costs a map lookup plus a copy into the page (19 ns for 2 KB below), and rendering static markup in place is cheaper still (12 ns); caching pays off when the content needs slow data or heavy work.

#### (Q) points
- **(Q1) API shape.**
  - (a) **Recommended**: `orbit.RenderHTML(ctx, r)`: one function for any `Renderer`, reachable from the frontmatter (which has `ctx` but no `Writer`). The name says it returns `orbit.HTML`.
  - (b) A method on `Slots`, Astro-style: `slots.Render(ctx, "default") (orbit.HTML, error)`. Closer to `Astro.slots.render`, but only for slots; Go renderers would still need (a). Could be added on top of (a) later.
  - (c) `func (w *Writer) Capture(r Renderer) (HTML, error)`: natural for Go renderers, but the frontmatter can't reach the `Writer` (`orbit__w` is internal, §2.2).
  - (d) Named `RenderString` returning `string`: the caller converts to `orbit.HTML` for `set:html`, a conversion that hides nothing.
- **(Q2) `</head>` inside a capture.**
  - (a) **Recommended**: a render error (above): a cached head would pin one page's state, and the error is cheap to check (one flag in `HeadEnd`).
  - (b) Ignored: the page's tags go after the doctype (§4's no-marker rule), which browsers accept but which differs from the normal placement.
- **(Q3) Help for the "no per-user data" rule.**
  - (a) **Recommended**: none in Orbit; the docs give the `locals`-flag pattern above. Per-user accessors live in the app's `locals`, which Orbit can't see into.
  - (b) `func (c *Ctx) Capturing() bool`, true inside `RenderHTML`, so an app can check it. But the app's data accessors usually don't receive `ctx`, so it helps little.

#### Cost (prototype `scratchpad/specs-attrs/proto/capture_test.go`, a model with pooled buffer and `Writer`; same machine and caveats as §2.5)
- A miss: **1 allocation**, the returned string (2 KB of HTML: 138 ns, 2048 B); the buffer and `Writer` are pooled. The copy is unavoidable, since the caller keeps the string.
- A hit: 0 allocations in Orbit (19 ns to look up and write 2 KB with `set:html`).
- The slot closure itself costs what slots cost (§4b: 1 allocation when it captures and goes through a hook, 0 with direct calls). A cache API that takes a callback (`func() (orbit.HTML, error)`) costs the user's closure, as in the example; a `Get`/`Set` API without a callback avoids it.
- No codegen, dev-loop or LSP change.

#### Tests (what must exist)
- Output equals the same content rendered in place (goldens with nested components, `class:list`, `client:*` markers, `server:defer` placeholders).
- The depth guard counts through captures (a recursive component through `RenderHTML` stops at 1000 with the stack error); nested captures; the current-writer pointer is restored after success, error and panic.
- An error is returned and the page's writer stays clean; partial output is dropped.
- `</head>` inside a capture is the render error; the page's head injection is unaffected.
- `AllocsPerRun`: 1 per miss (the string), 0 per hit.
- `orbittest.Render` of a component using it.

#### User-docs changes (with the implementation)
- `components.md` (slots): `orbit.RenderHTML`.
- `rendering.md`: "Caching fragments" (the pattern and the safety rules above).
- `reference/runtime-api.md`: `RenderHTML`.
- `coming-from-astro.md`: `Astro.slots.render("default")` → `orbit.RenderHTML(ctx, slots.Default)`; slot functions with arguments (`render("default", [item])`) → call the component's `Render` from Go.
- `lazy-loading.md`: `server:defer` inside cached fragments.
- `DECISIONS.md`: the decided-by-rule items above.

#### Out of scope
- A fragment cache in Orbit (SSR caching stays deferred, §12); this is the primitive an app's cache uses.
- Slots as functions with arguments (Astro's `slots.render(name, args)`).
- Streaming or partial flushes (responses stay buffered).

## 4e. Trailing slashes
**Status: Implemented** (2026-10-26). Implementation notes:
- Runtime: `type TrailingSlash uint8`, `TrailingSlashNever`/`TrailingSlashAlways`, `WithTrailingSlash` (`site.go`); the router keeps an `always` flag and an `ext` flag per compiled route; `find` cuts the slash as a substring in `always`, `lookupPath` is the mode-free trie lookup that `redirectTarget` uses in both modes; `hasFileExtension` is rule 4's byte loop (`router.go`). Tests: `router_slash_test.go` (table tests in both modes, the predicate, redirect targets, `TestMatchAllocsAlways`, `FuzzMatchAlways` against the `never` router), `site_slash_test.go` (308 with an empty body for GET/HEAD/POST in both modes, files/`public/`/islands never redirected, Orbit-first mux routes, `TestServeAllocsAlways`: 1 alloc).
- `internal/config`: `Config.TrailingSlashAlways` (a bool; `"never"` is the zero value). `internal/project` `appGo` adds `orbit.WithTrailingSlash(orbit.TrailingSlashAlways)` to `defaults` (after `WithSite`, before `embedded` and the caller's options) only for `"always"`; goldens `testdata/extras/{always,sitealways}.app_app.go.golden`.
- `internal/markdown`: `CanonicalURL(route, always)` (with its own copy of the extension predicate; the runtime's is unexported) for `const URL` (`GenInput.TrailingSlashAlways`) and the project's `Resolver`.
- `never` keeps today's behavior exactly, including `//` → 308 `/` (its `Location` isn't protocol-relative); the `//`-prefix rule matters only in `always`, where `//evil.example` would otherwise become `//evil.example/`.
- End to end: `internal/project` `TestTrailingSlashAlwaysProject` (generated app served through `app.New()`: `Post.URL`, `.md` links with fragments, 308s, `/rss.xml`) and `internal/build` `TestBuildTrailingSlashAlways` (`orbit build`, then `dist/server` over HTTP).
- `orbit dev` needed nothing: an `orbit.json` change already regenerates everything, and dev serves through the generated `app.New()`.

User answers: (Q1) **308 for every method**, today's status kept (user decision, not the recommended 301 for GET/HEAD; differs from Astro's 301 for GET); (Q2) an exported `orbit.WithTrailingSlash(orbit.TrailingSlashNever|TrailingSlashAlways)` option, passed by `app.New()` before the caller's options (recommended). The user decided the option (§12 "`trailingSlash` option", prompted by porting `nyth`, an Astro site with `trailingSlash: "always"`); this section specifies it.

**Goal**: `orbit.json` `"trailingSlash": "never"` (default, today's behavior, §4) or `"always"`. Each page has exactly one canonical URL, and the other form gets a permanent redirect to it. There's no `"ignore"` mode (two URLs per page, user decision), so Astro's own default has no equivalent: an Astro site on `"ignore"` picks one of the two when it moves.

### Astro 7's behavior (verified in `astro@7.3.0`'s `dist/`)
- **Production / on-demand** (`core/routing/trailing-slash-handler.js`, `handleTrailingSlash`, called first in `core/routing/handler.js` `handleRequest`, before the route check):
  - `"never"`: a path ending in `/` is redirected without it; `"always"`: a path not ending in `/` and **without a file extension** is redirected with one. `/` is never redirected.
  - **Unconditional**: it fires before route matching, so `/nope/` (`never`) redirects to `/nope`, which then 404s.
  - **Status**: `request.method === "GET" ? 301 : 308`, so **HEAD gets 308** too. The body is an HTML redirect page (`core/routing/3xx.js`).
  - **Query**: `Location` is the new path plus `url.search` (an empty `?` is dropped by the URL parser).
  - Paths are checked on `new URL(request.url).pathname`, still percent-encoded, so `/a%2F` doesn't end in `/`.
  - **Internal paths** are never redirected: `isInternalPath` (`@astrojs/internal-helpers/path`): prefixes `/_`, `/@`, `/.`, `//` (so `/_server-islands/…` and `/_astro/…` aren't). Several trailing slashes (`/x//`) are first collapsed (to `/x/` in `always`, `/x` in `never`) with the same 301/308.
  - **File extension**: `hasFileExtension` = `/\/[^/]+\.\w+$/`: the last segment has a dot with at least one character before it, and only ASCII word characters (`[A-Za-z0-9_]`, one or more) after its last dot, at the end of the path.
- **Matching** (`core/routing/pattern.js` `getPattern`; `core/routing/create-manifest.js` `trailingSlashForPath`): in `always`, page patterns end with `\/$`, so `/blog/hello` matches nothing and `/blog/hello/` renders; the root pattern is `^\/$`. **Endpoints whose route has a file extension** (`rss.xml.ts`) use `never` (`/rss.xml` only; `/rss.xml/` is a 404). A *page* named `rss.xml.astro` in `always` gets the `\/$` pattern but is never redirected (it has an extension): a quirk.
- **Dev** (`vite-plugin-astro-server/trailing-slash.js`): a mismatch isn't redirected; it gets a **404 with a hint page** ("Your site is configured with `trailingSlash` set to `always`. Do you want to go to /x/ instead?", `template/4xx.js`). Duplicate trailing slashes get a 301. So Astro's dev and prod differ.
- **404 page**: in `always`, the custom 404 is looked up as `/404/` (`core/routing/dev.js`, `getErrorRoutePath`); it renders at the requested path either way.

### Rules
1. **Config**: `"trailingSlash": "never" | "always"`; unset or `null` means `"never"`. Anything else is an error when `orbit.json` loads (§8): `orbit.json: trailingSlash must be "never" or "always", not "ignore"`, quoting the raw JSON value (`not true`). For `"ignore"` the message adds `; Orbit has no "ignore" mode: each page has one URL` (decided by rule: simplest; Astro's default is the likeliest wrong value). `orbit doctor` needs no new check: its config check already reports load errors.
2. **Canonical URL** of a page route:
   - `never`: the pattern itself (`/blog/hello`; `blog/index.orbit` → `/blog`).
   - `always`: the pattern plus `/` (`/blog/hello/`, `/blog/`), except `/`, and except an **extension route**: one whose last segment is static and has a file extension (rule 4). `src/pages/rss.xml.orbit` (route `/rss.xml`, which routing allows today) is `/rss.xml` in both modes (decided by rule: Astro's endpoint rule; an Orbit page that sets its own `Content-Type` plays the role of an Astro endpoint, so it gets that rule rather than Astro's page quirk).
   - `Route.Pattern` stays slash-less in both modes; the mode is site-wide (decided by rule: simplest; patterns, collision checks and the route-param rules don't change).
3. **Matching** (the router's `find`, prod and dev):
   - `never`: unchanged. A path with an empty segment, a trailing `/` included, never matches.
   - `always`: a path ending in `/` (other than `/`) is matched with that one slash cut off (a substring, no allocation), and the match counts only if the route isn't an extension route. A path not ending in `/` matches only extension routes (and `/` matches the root). So `/blog/hello/` renders, `/blog/hello` doesn't match, and `/rss.xml/` doesn't match (decided by rule: Astro, where it's a 404). Params come from the cut path: `[slug]` at `/blog/hello/` is `"hello"`; `[...path]` at `/docs/a/b/` is `"a/b"`, at `/docs/` it's `""`.
   - A dynamic segment with a dotted value follows the same rule: `/blog/v1.2/` renders `[slug]` with `"v1.2"`; `/blog/v1.2` doesn't match and isn't redirected (it has an extension, rule 5), so it reaches `next` (decided by rule: Astro, where it's a 404).
4. **File extension** (decided by rule: Astro's `hasFileExtension`): the escaped path's last segment (after its last `/`) contains a `.` that isn't its first byte, and the bytes after its last `.` are one or more of `[A-Za-z0-9_]`. So `/rss.xml`, `/a/b.min.js` and `/v1.2` have one; `/x.`, `/.env`, `/a.b/` and `/a.%41` don't. A byte loop, no regexp. Routes use the same predicate on the pattern's last segment, computed once in `NewSite`.
5. **Redirect** (`redirectTarget`, now per mode), tried only when nothing matched:
   - `never`: a path ending in `/` whose slash-less form matches a page route (today).
   - `always`: a path other than `/` that doesn't end in `/`, has no file extension (rule 4), and whose form with `/` appended matches a non-extension page route. The trie is slash-less, so the check is a lookup of the path itself that ignores the extension filter; no string is built for it.
   - **Only when the other form matches a page route**; otherwise the request goes to `next` untouched (§4, decided by rule). This differs from Astro's unconditional redirect so that Orbit-first setups keep user routes like `/api/items` and `/api/items/`. To document: in `always` with Orbit first, a mux route `/api/items` is redirected only if `/api/items/` is a page (e.g. under a root `[...rest]` page), as `/api/items/` is in `never` today.
   - **Never redirected**: `/`; paths under `/_orbit/` (assets and island URLs, which §5b's steps 1–2 handle before the router anyway); `public/` files (step 2, GET/HEAD); paths starting with `//` (never a protocol-relative `Location`). These are Orbit's counterparts of Astro's internal paths: Orbit has no Vite `/@…` paths, and `_` files aren't routes (§4), so Astro's `/_`, `/@` and `/.` prefixes would only change today's behavior for param routes (decided by rule: Astro, mapped to Orbit's internal prefix).
   - `Location` is the new escaped path, plus `?` + `RawQuery` when there is a query (`/search?q=go` → `/search/?q=go`); the fragment never reaches the server. Empty body, as today.
   - **Status: 308 for every method** ((Q1), user decision: today's status, unchanged in `never`).
   - Same in dev and prod (the §4 user decision). Astro's dev 404 hint page isn't copied: one behavior, and the redirect is what production does.
   - Duplicate trailing slashes (`/x//`) aren't collapsed: no form of them matches, so they reach `next` (a 404), as today (decided by rule: simplest; Astro collapses them only within its unconditional redirect, which Orbit doesn't have).
6. **Where the mode reaches**:
   - **Runtime**: `NewSite` takes the mode as an option (**(Q2)**); the router keeps it plus an `ext` flag per compiled route.
   - **Generated `app.New()`**: passes the mode from `orbit.json`, only for `"always"` (so today's generated code is unchanged), before the caller's options, like `WithSite`.
   - **Markdown** (§2.4): `Post.URL` and rewritten relative `.md` links use the canonical URL (rule 2): `/blog/hello/` in `always`, with query and fragment after it (`/blog/hello/#setup`). The `Resolver` returns the canonical URL instead of the pattern. Changing the mode regenerates them (`orbit.json` is watched, §7).
   - **`ctx.URL`**: the request URL as received (`/blog/hello/` in `always`); nothing is rewritten. `ctx.Param` per rule 3.
   - **`orbittest.Get`**: it doesn't follow redirects (§4), so in `always`, `Get(t, h, "/blog/hello")` returns the redirect; tests use `/blog/hello/`. Documented; no API change.
   - **`site.NotFound()` / `404.orbit`**: unchanged; it renders at whatever path didn't match (its reserved pattern `404` isn't a URL).
   - **Islands**: `/_orbit/island/<buildID>/<id>` is never redirected (rule 5). A component reading the page URL from `Referer` sees the canonical form.
   - **User links**: Orbit doesn't rewrite `href`s in templates; in `always`, `<a href="/blog/{slug}">` costs a redirect per click. Documented ("write `/blog/{slug}/`"), as in Astro.
   - **`orbit new`**: doesn't write the option (default `never`).

### (Q) points
- **(Q1) Redirect status.** Orbit sends 308 for every method today; Astro sends 301 for GET and 308 for everything else, HEAD included; `net/http`'s `ServeMux` sends 301 for its own slash redirects.
  - **(A, recommended; not chosen) 301 for GET and HEAD, 308 for other methods.** Matches Astro for every browser navigation, so a ported site's CDN logs, crawlers and redirect checks see the same status. HEAD mirrors GET (`curl -I` shows what a browser gets; Go's convention), deviating from Astro's HEAD → 308 quirk. Other methods keep their method and body. Applies to both modes, so `never`'s GET redirect changes from 308 to 301 (docs, DECISIONS and the §13 curl checks change with it).
  - (B) Astro exactly: 301 for GET, 308 for the rest, HEAD included. Exact parity; `curl -I` disagrees with the browser.
  - **(C, chosen by the user) 308 for every method (today).** One code, method-safe; search engines treat 308 like 301; differs from Astro on every GET.
- **(Q2) How the mode reaches the runtime.** It must agree with the generated `.md` URLs, which `orbit.json` decides.
  - **(A, recommended) An exported option**: `type TrailingSlash uint8` with `TrailingSlashNever` (the zero value) and `TrailingSlashAlways`, and `func WithTrailingSlash(TrailingSlash) Option`, passed by `app.New()` before the caller's options (like `WithSite`). Usable from runtime tests and hand-built `NewSite` calls. Its doc comment says to set it in `orbit.json`: passing another value to `app.New()` makes `Post.URL` and rewritten `.md` links redirect once.
  - (B) The same option, but `app.New()` appends it after the caller's options, so `orbit.json` always wins. URLs always agree, but it's the one option a caller can't override (surprising).
  - (C) No option: `NewSite` grows a settings argument filled from the generated route table. No public knob, but `NewSite`'s signature changes for one bit.

### Cost
- Per request in `never`: unchanged. In `always`: a check of the path's last byte, a substring for the match and one `bool` on the matched route; no allocation. `AllocsPerRun` asserts 0 allocations in Orbit's render path and +1 for serving (`Content-Length`), as today (§4b), in **both modes**: `/blog/hello` (`never`) and `/blog/hello/` (`always`).
- Unmatched paths in `always`: the redirect check is one more trie lookup (rule 5), no allocation; the redirect allocates its `Location`, as today.
- Build and dev loop: none (one config field; `.md` URLs are computed as before).

### Tests (what must exist)
- `internal/config`: table cases for `"never"`, `"always"`, `null` and missing; errors for `"ignore"` (with the hint), `"Always"`, `true` and `1`.
- `router_test.go` (table-driven, both modes): matching of `/`, `/blog/`, `/blog/hello/`, `/blog/hello`, `/docs/` and `/docs/a/b/` (rest route), `/rss.xml`, `/rss.xml/`, `/blog/v1.2`, `/blog/v1.2/`, `//`, `/blog//`; params from the cut path; redirect targets with and without a query (`/search?q=go` → `/search/?q=go`); no redirect for `/`, `/_orbit/x`, `//evil.example`, extension paths, `/x//` and paths whose other form isn't a page; the file-extension predicate on rule 4's examples.
- `site_test.go`: status per method (Q1) for GET, HEAD and POST in both modes; `Location`; empty body; static files, `public/` files and island URLs never redirected in `always`; Orbit first with a mux route `/api/items` (`always`) or `/api/items/` (`never`) reaching `next`.
- `site_alloc_test.go`: the `AllocsPerRun` cases above for `always`.
- `internal/markdown` and `internal/project` goldens: `Post.URL` and rewritten links in `always` (`/blog/hello/#setup`, `/`, an extension page unchanged); the generated `app.New()` with and without the option.
- `orbittest`: `Get` of the slash-less path in `always` returns the redirect.
- Integration (a small testdata project with `"trailingSlash": "always"`, built like `examples/blog`): `/blog/hello` → redirect to `/blog/hello/`, `/blog/hello/` → 200, `/` → 200, an extension page → 200 at `/rss.xml`. Added to §13 milestone 5's checks (`internal/build` `TestBuildTrailingSlashAlways`).

### User-docs changes (with the implementation)
- `configuration.md`: the `trailingSlash` option (values, default, the error, no `"ignore"`).
- `routing.md`: the trailing-slash section per mode: canonical URLs, extension routes (`rss.xml.orbit`), dotted param values, what's never redirected, status codes (Q1), the Orbit-first note (rule 5).
- `markdown.md`: `Post.URL` and rewritten links follow the mode.
- `testing.md`: `orbittest.Get` with the canonical path in `always`.
- `middleware.md`, `server.md`: Orbit-first setups and the redirect (rule 5).
- `coming-from-astro.md`: the `trailingSlash` mapping (`"ignore"` has no equivalent; Orbit redirects only paths whose other form is a page; same behavior in dev and prod, where Astro's dev shows a 404 hint page; extension pages; status codes).
- `deploy.md`, `troubleshooting.md`, `dev-server.md`, `rendering.md`, `project-structure.md`: wherever they state "`/x/` gets a 308 to `/x`", point at `routing.md` and follow Q1.
- `DECISIONS.md`: the decided-by-rule items above (error wording, the extension predicate, extension routes, dotted params, the internal prefixes, no collapsing, `Route.Pattern` unchanged).

### Out of scope
- `"ignore"` (user decision) and `base` (a path prefix, §12).
- Collapsing duplicate slashes (`/x//`), and Astro's unconditional redirect of paths that match nothing.
- Rewriting or checking `href`s in templates. A later `orbit check` warning for literal internal links that don't follow the mode is a possible addition.
- A per-route override.

## 4f. Loading data concurrently
**Status: Implemented** (2026-10-26; runtime `load.go`, docs `docs/data-loading.md`; notes, deviations and measurements under "Implementation" at the end of this section). Accepted 2026-10-26. User answers, all as recommended: (Q1) `orbit.All(ctx, loads...)`; (Q3) cancellation: each load gets a context cancelled on the first error; (Q5) a panic in a load is recovered there, the others cancelled, then re-panicked on the page's goroutine; (Q6) the name `orbit.All`. (Q2) first error in time, (Q4) the failing load's `.orbit` position, (Q7) `*orbit.Ctx` as the first argument and (Q8) no memoizing helper in the runtime: decided by the coordinator with the recommendations (rule: simplest / Go stdlib errgroup semantics). User direction (2026-10-26): "ideally we should be able to load all the data at the page level". So **page-level loading is the documented main way to fetch data**; `server:concurrent` (§3.6) stays the porting escape hatch for components that fetch their own data, Astro style; and the runtime gets **a small helper that runs several loads concurrently in a frontmatter and returns the first error**.

**Why**: a frontmatter is plain Go, so concurrent loads already work today, but the pattern is 13 lines of ceremony per page, and each page gets three details slightly wrong in its own way (which error wins, whether a slow sibling is waited for after a failure, what a panic in a load does):
```go
rc := ctx.Request.Context()
var (
	book     catalog.Book
	chapters []catalog.Chapter
	errs     [2]error
	wg       sync.WaitGroup
)
wg.Go(func() { book, errs[0] = locals.Catalog.Book(rc, params.Slug) })
wg.Go(func() { chapters, errs[1] = locals.Catalog.Chapters(rc, params.Slug) })
wg.Wait()
if err := errors.Join(errs[:]...); err != nil { return err }
```
- `errors.Join` of a redirect and a real failure makes `renderFailed`'s `errors.As` (§4) pick the redirect; with the §4c handler, whichever typed error comes first in the join wins.
- **A panic in a `wg.Go` goroutine crashes the whole process**: the request's recovery (`callRender`, §4) only covers the request goroutine.
- Nothing is cancelled, so a page whose catalog call failed in 5 ms still waits for the 100 ms reader call next to it.
- The error's position is the `return`'s line, not the load that failed.

In Astro, the same page is `const [book, chapters] = await Promise.all([getBook(slug), getChapters(slug)])` in the frontmatter. `nyth` (the motivating port: ~35 catalog/reader call sites, typed errors `AccessRefused`/`CatalogUnavailable`/`ReaderBlocked` answered by the §4c hook) fetches mostly in components; this section gives it the page-level form to move to, and the locals loader below for data that several components need.

#### End-to-end example (recommended shape, (Q1) b)
`src/pages/fiction/[bookSlug].orbit`:
```orbit
---
import (
	"context"

	"example.com/nyth/internal/catalog"
	Base "../../layouts/Base.orbit"
	ChapterList "../../components/ChapterList.orbit"
)

var (
	book     catalog.Book
	author   catalog.Author
	chapters []catalog.Chapter
)
err := orbit.All(ctx,
	func(rc context.Context) (err error) {
		if book, err = locals.Catalog.Book(rc, params.BookSlug); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		author, err = locals.Catalog.Author(rc, book.AuthorID) // needs the book: chained in this load
		return err
	},
	func(rc context.Context) (err error) {
		chapters, err = locals.Catalog.Chapters(rc, params.BookSlug)
		return err
	},
)
if err != nil {
	return err // e.g. "src/pages/fiction/[bookSlug].orbit:23: catalog: unavailable"; errors.As finds *catalog.AccessRefused
}
---
<Base title={book.Title}>
  <h1>{book.Title}</h1>
  <p>by {author.Name}</p>
  <ChapterList chapters={chapters} />
</Base>
```
Both loads start at once; the page waits about `max(book + author, chapters)`. If `Chapters` fails first, the book load's `rc` is cancelled, `orbit.All` returns once both loads have returned, and the page's `return err` hands the `Chapters` error (positioned at line 23, its load) to the 500 or the §4c handler.

#### API
```go
// All runs each load concurrently and waits for all of them. Each load gets a
// context derived from c.Request.Context(), cancelled when a load fails (and
// when All returns). All returns the first error in time, or nil.
func All(c *Ctx, loads ...func(context.Context) error) error
```
Rules:
- **Starts**: every load but the first in its own goroutine, in argument order, then the first on the calling goroutine (one goroutine fewer; measured below). Zero loads return `nil`; one load runs inline, without a derived context (nothing to cancel). A `nil` `*Ctx` (a hand-built test) uses `context.Background()`. *Decided by rule* (simplest).
- **Waits for every load**, even after an error, before returning: loads assign variables of the frontmatter, so returning early would race with the template reading them (Go stdlib: `errgroup.Wait`).
- **No concurrency limit**: the loads are written out explicitly, unlike a marked component in a `{for}` (§3.6's cap). A dynamic slice (`orbit.All(ctx, loads...)`) is allowed; the docs say "one query beats N", and a user who needs a cap uses `errgroup.SetLimit` or batches. *Decided by rule* (simplest).
- **Not generic, no codegen, no new `Ctx` field**: one plain function in a new runtime file (`load.go`), so pages that don't call it are unchanged and no generated package carries new type weight (§4b).

#### Errors ((Q2), (Q4))
- **Which error**: the **first in time** ((Q2) a). That's `Promise.all`'s rejection (rule 1: Astro) and `errgroup`'s. With cancellation it's also the cause: once it's recorded, the other loads see a cancelled `rc` and usually fail with `context.Canceled`, which must not win. Two genuine failures racing each other are reported in whichever order they happen (documented; the page fails either way).
- **Position** ((Q4) a): on the error path only, the recorded error is wrapped with the **failing load's `.orbit` line** (the line of its `func` literal), found from the func value's entry PC (`runtime.FuncForPC(…).FileLine`, which follows the generated code's line directives; `relativeFile` as for panics, §4). The wrapper is the existing position error (`posError`), so the frontmatter's `return err` (wrapped at codegen by `ErrorAt`, §2.2) passes it through unchanged and the 500 log line, the dev error page and a §4c handler's `err.Error()` name the load, e.g. `src/pages/fiction/[bookSlug].orbit:23: catalog: unavailable`. `file:line` without a column, like panic positions. Measured: verified with `//line` directives in the prototype; the success path doesn't pay anything.
- **Error identity**: `posError` keeps `Unwrap`, so `errors.Is`/`errors.As` (the §4c hook's `*catalog.AccessRefused`, `catalog.ErrUnavailable`) see through it. A load that returns an error already carrying a position is recorded as is.
- **Control flow**: `orbit.ErrNotFound` and a `*RedirectError` from a load (`return orbit.ErrNotFound` when the book doesn't exist, `return ctx.Redirect(…)` inside a load) are recorded **unwrapped**, like `ErrorAt` does, and win by the same first-in-time rule: the page returns them and gets its 404 or redirect. A later `context.Canceled` from a sibling doesn't hide them.
- **The returned error is just a value**: the frontmatter may handle it (`if errors.Is(err, reader.ErrBlocked) { blocked = true } else if err != nil { return err }`) and render a partial page. Variables of loads that failed or were cancelled hold whatever their load assigned (usually zero values). Documented.

#### Cancellation ((Q3))
Each `All` derives `rc, cancel := context.WithCancel(c.Request.Context())`, passes `rc` to every load, cancels it at the first error and always before returning (as `errgroup.WithContext` does). Recommendation: **yes, cancel** ((Q3) a).
- **Benefit**: measured, a load failing after 5 ms next to a 100 ms load that honors its context returns in **5.2 ms** instead of **100.3 ms**. `database/sql`, `net/http` clients and gRPC all honor contexts, so a 503 for `CatalogUnavailable` isn't held back by the slowest sibling.
- **Cost**: 2 allocations and about 190 ns per `All` call (measured below), only on pages that call it; nothing per load.
- **Why it's fine here when `server:concurrent` declined it** (§3.6 Q4): there, cancellation had to be bolted on by deriving a new request context mid-render, and it made the reported error (in output order) nondeterministic. Here every load receives its context as a parameter (the natural Go place for it), the reported error is the first in time by definition, and cancellation can only follow it. Loads that ignore `rc` (using `ctx.Request.Context()` instead) just aren't cancelled.
- **`rc` is cancelled when `All` returns**: a load must not start background work with it that outlives the call. Documented.
- **Naming**: the parameter is `rc` in the docs. Naming it `ctx` is allowed (shadowing in a nested block, §2.2) but hides Orbit's `ctx` inside the load, so `ctx.Param` there is a compile error; the docs mention it.

#### Panics ((Q5))
A panic in a load is recovered **in that load's goroutine** (otherwise it would crash the process), with the innermost `.orbit` frame of that goroutine (`orbitFrame`, §4), which is the load's own line. It cancels the others like an error, and once every load has returned, **`All` re-panics it on the calling goroutine** ((Q5) a) with the same `*panicError` value. The request's existing recovery then handles it as any frontmatter panic: the 500 and its log line, the §4c handler, the dev overlay; under `server:concurrent`, that component goroutine's recovery. Small runtime change: `callRender` (and §3.6's goroutine recovery) keeps a recovered `*panicError` as is, instead of wrapping it with the `All` call's frame. `http.ErrAbortHandler` is re-panicked as is, as `net/http` expects. Measured: the prototype's panic in a load re-panics on the caller as `…/src/pages/page.orbit:13: panic: assignment to entry in nil map` (the load's line; the prototype skips `relativeFile`).

#### Dependent loads
A load that needs another's result, for each shape:

| Need | (b) `orbit.All` (recommended) | (a) group | (c) typed futures |
|---|---|---|---|
| A chain next to independent loads (`book` → `author`, with `chapters` alongside) | **chain inside one load** (the example above): `max(book + author, chapters)` | same | the dependent load calls `book.Get(rc)` |
| A fan-out after one load (`book` → `author` and `reviews`) | a nested `orbit.All(ctx, …)` inside the load, after `book` | a nested group | two loads `Get` the book |
| Stages (everything in stage 2 needs everything in stage 1) | two `orbit.All` calls in a row; costs the overlap between stages | `Wait`, then a second group | not needed |
| A shared dependency used by several loads, or by the page **and** components | the **locals loader** below: each caller asks for `Book(slug)`, the first call runs it, the others wait for it (deduplicated by key, also across components) | same | `Get` from each load; components still need the locals loader |
| An arbitrary DAG, every load started at once | chains + the locals loader (each shared node is a memoized key) | — | yes, directly: every load starts at once and `Get`s its inputs |

- **Which error the page sees**: chained in one load, the dependency's error is that load's error. With futures, a dependent load that returns its dependency's error returns it *after* the dependency recorded it (the prototype records the group's first error before marking the future done), so the page sees the dependency's own error and position, deterministically (tested 500 times in the prototype). With the locals loader, every waiting caller gets the same error value from the memoized call; the first one recorded wins, so the position is the first load that returned it (any of them; same underlying error).
- **`Get` inside another load (futures)**: allowed and safe (a channel close; values are written before it). **Deadlock**: a load waiting on itself or a cycle. With `:=` it can't be written (`f := orbit.Load(…, func(…) { f.Get(rc) })` is "undefined: f"); it needs futures declared with `var` first, so it's deliberate and rare. Orbit can't detect it cheaply (no goroutine identity; a wait-for graph would need every `Get` to carry its caller's load and take a lock). `Get(rc)` selects on `rc.Done()`, so a cycle is unblocked when any load fails or the client goes away (verified in the prototype: the cycle returns `context deadline exceeded` when the request context ends), but a pure cycle on a client that waits holds the request until then. Documented.
- **The locals loader has the same self-wait risk**: a memoized load for key K that (indirectly) asks for K deadlocks in `sync.OnceValues`. Same answer: documented, not detected.

**Criterion for (Q1)**: (b) covers chains, fan-outs and stages with plain Go inside the loads, and the locals loader covers the shared-dependency case that matters most in `nyth` (the book a page and its components all need). Typed futures are the only shape that expresses an arbitrary DAG with every load started at once, at the cost below; nothing in `nyth` needs that, and it can be added later on the same internals without breaking (b).

#### Ctx and locals safety
Loads run on other goroutines while the frontmatter waits in `All`, so they share the render's state:

| Inside a load | Safe? |
|---|---|
| `rc`, `ctx.Request` (fields, headers, cookies, `Context()`), `ctx.URL`, `ctx.Site`, `ctx.Param`, `params`, `props` | yes: read-only during the render (props by reference: as safe as the reader's other goroutines) |
| `locals` | the pointer yes (resolved at the top of the render, §5b, before any load starts); its **fields** are user data: read-only fields set by middleware are fine, fields written by loads need a `sync` type or a mutex |
| the frontmatter's own variables | assign each from one load only, read them after `All` returns (the docs' pattern) |
| `ctx.SetStatus`, `ctx.Header()`, `ctx.Redirect` as a call | `SetStatus`/`Header()` no: set them after `All` returns; returning `ctx.Redirect(…)` from a load is fine (it only builds the error) |
| `orbit.RenderHTML(ctx, …)`, rendering a component | no: it swaps the `Ctx`'s current `Writer` (§4d); render after `All` |
| `ctx.Request.Body` | read it once, before `All` |

Enforcement: **docs only**, plus `go test -race` advice (Orbit's own state in `All` is synchronized and tested under `-race`). Guarding `SetStatus`/`Header()`/`RenderHTML` would add a check to every call on the unmarked path for a misuse `-race` already reports. *Decided by rule* (simplest).

#### The locals loader (shared data without props drilling)
The documented pattern for data several components need (`nyth`'s book, current reader, shelves): a per-request memoizing loader on `locals`, set by middleware. Components call it instead of receiving the data as props; the page can warm it concurrently with `orbit.All`, so components then hit the cache.
```go
// internal/catalog/loader.go: user code, ~20 lines
type Loader struct {
	ctx   context.Context // the request's, from the middleware
	api   *Client
	mu    sync.Mutex
	books map[string]func() (Book, error)
}

func NewLoader(ctx context.Context, api *Client) *Loader {
	return &Loader{ctx: ctx, api: api, books: map[string]func() (Book, error){}}
}

// Book returns the book, loading it once per request: concurrent callers wait for the first one.
func (l *Loader) Book(slug string) (Book, error) {
	l.mu.Lock()
	f, ok := l.books[slug]
	if !ok {
		f = sync.OnceValues(func() (Book, error) { return l.api.Book(l.ctx, slug) })
		l.books[slug] = f
	}
	l.mu.Unlock()
	return f()
}
```
```go
// middleware
r, l := orbit.EnsureLocals[locals.Locals](r)
l.Catalog = catalog.NewLoader(r.Context(), api)
```
```orbit
---
// page: warm it
err := orbit.All(ctx,
	func(context.Context) error { _, err := locals.Catalog.Book(params.BookSlug); return err },
	func(context.Context) error { _, err := locals.Catalog.Shelves(); return err },
)
if err != nil { return err }
---
```
- **The loader uses the request's context, not the caller's**: a memoized call is shared, so one caller's cancellation (an `All` whose sibling failed) must not cache `context.Canceled` for every later caller, e.g. a page that handles `All`'s error and renders a partial page. So shared loads aren't cancelled by `All` (they still stop when the client leaves). Documented as the one rule of the pattern.
- **Dedupe by key** across the page and all components, including `server:concurrent` ones (the mutex makes it safe) and island requests (their own request, their own loader).
- **Errors are memoized too** (one failing backend call per request, not one per component).
- Orbit ships no helper for it ((Q8)).

#### Which tool
| Tool | Use it when | The first byte waits? | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Page-level loads with `orbit.All`** (recommended) | the page knows what it needs; most pages | for the slowest load | ~0.2 µs and 2–3 allocs per load (the closure, the goroutine start, the variable it assigns), 3 per call (state, cancellation) |
| **The locals loader** | several components (or the page and components) need the same data; no props drilling; dedupe | for the slowest load | a mutex and a map entry per key |
| **`server:concurrent`** (§3.6) | porting components that fetch their own data (Astro style), or a component that is reused on many pages with its own data | for the slowest marked component | ~0.4 µs and 1 alloc per marked instance |
| **`server:defer`** (§3.4) | the part can arrive after the page (below the fold, per user, slow third parties) | no | a second request, a fallback, a seal |

They combine: a page can `orbit.All` its main data, warm the locals loader, and still have a `server:concurrent` sidebar and a `server:defer` recommendations block.

#### (Q) points
1. **(Q1) API shape** (each with the example's two loads; `book` etc. declared with `var` as above):
   - (a) A group, errgroup-like:
     ```go
     g, rc := orbit.NewGroup(ctx)
     g.Go(func() (err error) { book, err = locals.Catalog.Book(rc, params.BookSlug); return })
     g.Go(func() (err error) { chapters, err = locals.Catalog.Chapters(rc, params.BookSlug); return })
     if err := g.Wait(); err != nil { return err }
     ```
     Familiar to Go developers, natural for conditional loads (`if user != nil { g.Go(…) }`) and loops. But **forgetting `g.Wait()`** compiles and renders the template while loads still write its variables (a data race `go vet` doesn't see), and it's one more allocated object. Same cost as (b) otherwise.
   - (b) **`orbit.All(ctx, loads...)`** (recommended): one statement that can't be left unwaited, reads like `Promise.all` (rule 1: feels like Astro), same errors, cancellation and panics as (a). Conditional loads go inside the load (`if user == nil { return nil }`), a dynamic list is a slice. Dependencies: see the table above (chains, nested `All`, stages, the locals loader).
   - (c) Typed futures with generics:
     ```go
     g := orbit.NewGroup(ctx)
     book := orbit.Load(g, func(rc context.Context) (catalog.Book, error) { return locals.Catalog.Book(rc, params.BookSlug) })
     chapters := orbit.Load(g, func(rc context.Context) ([]catalog.Chapter, error) { return locals.Catalog.Chapters(rc, params.BookSlug) })
     if err := g.Wait(); err != nil { return err }
     // template: book.Value().Title, chapters.Value()
     ```
     No outer variables, and the only shape for an arbitrary DAG (a load calls `book.Get(rc)`). But each func literal repeats its result type, the template reads `.Value()`, a forgotten `Wait` reads zero values, cycles are possible (above), and each load allocates a future and a channel (+0.4 KB for 3 loads). **Compile cost** (§4b): `Load[T]` and `Future[T]` are instantiated per distinct `T` in each generated package: measured, a package with 3 loads went from **35.6 KB to 134.9 KB** of export/object data (~33 KB per instantiated type; `string`, a struct and a slice), with the package's `go build` time within noise (20–24 ms vs 23 ms, dominated by the `go` command). At 200 pages that's megabytes more build-cache and link input for `orbit build`, and per-edit work in `orbit dev` stays per package. Arity variants taking method values (`orbit.Load1(g, locals.Catalog.Book, params.BookSlug)`) would be shorter but multiply the API.
   - (d) Docs only: the WaitGroup pattern, or `golang.org/x/sync/errgroup` in the user's own module (fine for users: the stdlib-only rule is about Orbit's runtime; `errgroup` would be a new runtime dependency, which §10 doesn't allow, so Orbit can't ship it). Zero API, but the panic-crashes-the-process trap, no load positions, and the usual `g, ctx := errgroup.WithContext(…)` shadows Orbit's `ctx`.
2. **(Q2) Which error**: (a) **first in time** (recommended: `Promise.all`, errgroup; with cancellation, the cause); (b) first in argument order (deterministic when loads ignore `rc`, but with cancellation an earlier load can fail with `context.Canceled` *because* of a later one, so it needs filtering of Orbit-caused cancellations, as §3.6 Q4 b; and it waits for every load before choosing even without cancellation); (c) `errors.Join` (rejected above: a redirect hides a failure in `renderFailed`, and the §4c hook's `errors.As` matches whichever typed error comes first).
3. **(Q3) Cancellation**: (a) **a derived, cancellable context per `All`, passed to each load** (recommended, above: 5 ms vs 100 ms on failure, 2 allocs and ~190 ns per call); (b) none, loads use `ctx.Request.Context()` (the funcs would be `func() error`, 2 allocs fewer; a failing page waits for its slowest load, like §3.6).
4. **(Q4) Error position**: (a) **the failing load's line** (recommended: free on success, ~0.6 µs on the error path, names the call that failed); (b) the `return err`'s line only, as for any frontmatter error (simplest; the error text usually names the operation anyway).
5. **(Q5) Panics**: (a) **recovered in the load, re-panicked on the calling goroutine after every load returned, keeping the load's position** (recommended: a panic stays a panic, so a frontmatter that handles `All`'s error to render a partial page can't swallow a nil-pointer bug; the outcome at the site level is the same 500/handler/overlay); (b) returned as `All`'s error (simpler; but it can be handled like a data error, and `errors.Is` checks in the frontmatter see it).
6. **(Q6) Name**: (a) **`orbit.All`** (recommended: `Promise.all`; short; reads well as `err := orbit.All(ctx, …)`); (b) `orbit.LoadAll` (says what it's for, longer); (c) `orbit.Concurrently`. Not `orbit.Parallel`: it's §3.6's generated-code function.
7. **(Q7) First argument**: (a) **`*orbit.Ctx`** (recommended: what a frontmatter has; the request context is derived inside); (b) `context.Context` (usable outside frontmatters, but every page writes `ctx.Request.Context()`, and custom handlers have errgroup).
8. **(Q8) A memoizing helper in the runtime** (`orbit.Memo[K, V]` for the locals loader): (a) **no, a documented pattern** (recommended: ~20 lines of user code; the context rule and the key type are app decisions; a generic runtime type would be re-instantiated per package that mentions it, §4b); (b) yes, a small generic type.

*Decided by rule* (to log in `docs/DECISIONS.md`): first load inline, zero/one load cases and the nil `Ctx` (simplest); wait for every load after an error (Go stdlib: errgroup); no concurrency limit (simplest); control-flow errors unwrapped (consistent with `ErrorAt`); `http.ErrAbortHandler` re-panicked as is (net/http); safety by docs only (simplest).

#### Cost (prototype `scratchpad/load-proto/`: `All` with and without a derived context, typed futures, a page file with `//line` directives for positions and panics; 3 trivial loads with no I/O, `b.Loop()`, 5 runs, medians; Ryzen 7 9700X, 16 threads, Go 1.27.3, load average 3–33 on a shared machine; all prototype tests pass under `-race`)

| Benchmark | `-cpu 1` | 16 threads | allocs | B |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 sequential calls (no concurrency) | 7.5 ns | 8.0 ns | 0 | 0 |
| hand-written `WaitGroup` + `errors.Join` (above) | 621 ns | 572 ns | 11 | 416 |
| `errgroup.WithContext` (x/sync v0.24.0) | 762 ns | 747 ns | 13 | 456 |
| **(b) `orbit.All`, cancellable** | **675 ns** | **685 ns** | **12** | **464** |
| (b) `orbit.All`, no derived context ((Q3) b) | 484 ns | 470 ns | 10 | 368 |
| (b) `orbit.All`, every load in a goroutine (no inline first) | 722 ns | 759 ns | 13 | 512 |
| (b) 2 loads, `book` → `author` chained in one | 500 ns | 490 ns | 9 | 384 |
| (c) typed futures, 3 loads | 792 ns | 831 ns | 12 | 864 |
| (c) typed futures, `author` `Get`s `book` | 1.07 µs | 1.13 µs | 15 | 1000 |
| (b) error path: 2 loads, one fails (position lookup included) | 582 ns | 591 ns | 6 | 384 |

- So the helper costs about what the hand-written pattern costs (+55 ns, +1 alloc for 3 loads, with cancellation; −140 ns and −1 alloc without it), a few hundred nanoseconds against the milliseconds it hides. Of the 12 allocations, 3 are the frontmatter variables the closures assign (they escape, in any closure pattern), 3 the closures, 2 the goroutine starts, 1 the shared state, 2 the derived context.
- **Latency** (loads sleeping 10 ms, best of 10): 1/2/3 loads take 10.1/20.3/30.5 ms sequentially and **10.2/10.2/10.2 ms** with `All`; a `book` → `author` chain (10 + 10 ms) next to `chapters` (10 ms) takes 20.4 ms.
- **Unused, it costs nothing**: no codegen, no `Ctx` or `Writer` field, no change to the SSR path; `TestRenderAllocs`/`TestServeAllocs` unchanged. Not generic, so no per-package instantiation (the futures' 35.6 → 134.9 KB above doesn't apply). No dev-loop, build or LSP change.

#### Tests (what must exist)
- **Runtime** (`load_test.go`, also under `-race`): every load runs and `All` waits for all, after an error too (a slow load still finishes before `All` returns); first error in time (a later-in-order load failing first wins); cancellation reaches the other loads' `rc` (the fail-fast latency bound) and `rc` is cancelled after `All` returns; `ErrNotFound` and `*RedirectError` returned unwrapped and winning over a later `context.Canceled`; positions on the error path (a page file with line directives: the failing load's line, `errors.As`/`errors.Is` through the wrapper, an already-positioned error kept); a panic in a goroutine load and in the inline first load re-panicked on the caller with the load's position, the others waited for; `http.ErrAbortHandler`; zero and one load; a nil `*Ctx`; the latency test (3 × 10 ms loads well under 2 × 10 ms).
- **Through a site**: a page whose load returns a typed error reaches the §4c handler with the load's position in `err.Error()`; a load returning `ErrNotFound` gives the 404 page, `ctx.Redirect` the redirect; a load panic gives the 500 with the load's line in the log line (not the `All` line); the same inside a `server:concurrent` component.
- **Allocations**: `TestRenderAllocs`/`TestServeAllocs` unchanged; an `AllocsPerRun` bound for `All` with 3 trivial loads (so a regression shows).
- **Integration**: an `examples/blog` (or orbittest fixture) page using `orbit.All`, rendered with `orbittest.Get`.
- `go build ./... && go vet ./... && go test ./...` and `go test -race ./...`.

#### User-docs changes (with the implementation)
- **New `data-loading.md`** ("Loading data"), linked from the docs nav right after `rendering.md`, making page-level loading the primary pattern: fetch in the page's frontmatter, pass data down as props; `orbit.All` with the example above; dependent loads (chains, nested `All`, stages); errors (first in time, the load's position, typed errors and the §4c hook, `ErrNotFound`/redirect from a load, handling the error for a partial page); cancellation and `rc`; panics; the safety table; the locals loader with its context rule; the "which tool" table; "one query beats N loads"; `go test -race`.
- `rendering.md`: the "Slow components" section (§3.6's docs) points at `data-loading.md` first, then `server:concurrent`/`server:defer`; its "fetching in the page with Go's own concurrency (errgroup-style)" line becomes `orbit.All`.
- `components.md`: data fetching in components (§3.6's note) leads with "prefer loading in the page or through the locals loader".
- `middleware.md`: setting up the locals loader (`catalog.NewLoader(r.Context(), api)`).
- `coming-from-astro.md`: `const [a, b] = await Promise.all([…])` in the frontmatter → `orbit.All(ctx, …)`; `await` in a component → load in the page, the locals loader, or `server:concurrent` when porting as is.
- `lazy-loading.md` and `performance.md`: the "which tool" table (or a link to it) and the cost row.
- `reference/runtime-api.md`: `All`.
- `troubleshooting.md`: a data race reported by `-race` in a load; a request that hangs on a self-waiting memoized load.
- `DECISIONS.md`: the decided-by-rule items above.

#### Out of scope
- Typed futures and a group type ((Q1) a, c), until a real page needs an arbitrary DAG; both can sit on the same internals later without breaking `All`.
- A concurrency limit, a timeout option (`context.WithTimeout` inside the load does it), and a memoizing helper in the runtime ((Q8)).
- Enforcing the safety rules at runtime.
- Making unmarked components concurrent, or hoisting component fetches into the page automatically (§3.6 rejected automatic concurrency).
- Streaming or early flushes (responses stay buffered, §4).

#### Implementation
**Files**: runtime `load.go` (`All`, its `loadGroup` state, `loadError`); `recoveredPanic` in `site.go`, now used by `callRender`, `callComponent` (`render_component.go`) and `runConcurrent` (`concurrent.go`), which keep a recovered `*panicError` as is. Tests: `load_test.go` and `load_orbit_test.go` (two loads behind `//line` directives), every item of the Tests list above, also under `-race`; the `orbittest/testdata/project` page `/loads` (two 30 ms loads, `?fail=error|panic|notfound`) through `orbittest.Get`, which checks real generated code: the 500 log line names the failing load's `func` line and the panicking line. Docs: `data-loading.md` (new, in the README nav after Rendering), `rendering.md`, `components.md`, `middleware.md`, `coming-from-astro.md`, `lazy-loading.md`, `performance.md`, `reference/runtime-api.md`, `troubleshooting.md`, `DECISIONS.md` ("Concurrent loads").

**How it works**: zero loads return `nil`; one load is a plain call with the request's context (a panic propagates as is, and the page's own recovery positions it at the load's line). Otherwise `All` derives `context.WithCancel`, starts loads 2..n with `go g.goRun(rc, load)`, runs the first inline, waits on a `sync.WaitGroup`, cancels, re-panics the first recorded panic if any, and returns the first recorded error. Each load runs under a deferred `recover`; a recorded error or panic cancels `rc`. A load's error goes through `loadError`, which, like `ErrorAt`, passes `ErrNotFound`, `*RedirectError` and `*posError` through, and otherwise wraps the error in a `*posError` at the func value's entry line (`reflect.ValueOf(load).Pointer()`, `runtime.FuncForPC(…).FileLine(Entry())`, `relativeFile`), only when that file ends in `.orbit`.

**Deviations and choices** (logged in `docs/DECISIONS.md`, "Concurrent loads"):
- **A panic beats an error**: when a load panics, `All` re-panics even if another load failed first (a panic stays a panic, the reason for (Q5) a). The first panic in time wins.
- **Loads outside `.orbit` files** (a method value, a helper returning a closure from Go code) get no position: the error is returned as is, and the frontmatter's `return` adds its own position through `ErrorAt`.
- **Nested `All`**: the inner call derives from `c.Request.Context()`, not from the outer load's `rc` (the API takes a `*Ctx`), so an outer sibling's failure doesn't cancel the inner loads; the outer `All` waits for them. Documented. Threading the outer `rc` would need a second entry point or a context-carrying `Ctx`; see the open question in the report.
- The single-load case doesn't recover: a panic reaches the page's recovery directly, with the same position (the panicking line is the innermost `.orbit` frame either way).

**Measured** (`BenchmarkAll`, 3 trivial loads, `b.Loop()`, Ryzen 7 9700X, 16 threads, Go 1.27.3, medians of 5, load average under 2):
- `All` with 3 loads: **460 ns** at `-cpu 1`, **470 ns** on 16 threads, 8 allocations / 304 B, of which 5 are `All`'s own (`AllocsPerRun`, asserted ≤ 5 by `TestAllAllocs`): its state, the derived context (2), 2 goroutine starts; the other 3 are the benchmark's closures. One load: 0 allocations in `All`. 3 plain sequential calls: 4.8 ns.
- Error path (2 loads, one failing, with the position lookup): 1.5 µs (`-cpu 1`) / 1.8 µs (16 threads), 11 allocations: above the prototype's 0.6 µs because `relativeFile` calls `os.Getwd` and `filepath.Rel` (as panic positions do); error path only.
- Latency: 3 loads sleeping 10 ms take ~10 ms (`TestAllConcurrent` asserts < 20 ms); a failing load next to one honoring a 1 s context returns in ~5 ms (`TestAllCancels`); `/loads` (two 30 ms loads) renders under 60 ms through `orbittest.Get`.
- Unused, it costs nothing: `TestRenderAllocs` (0) and `TestServeAllocs` (1) unchanged; no codegen or `Ctx`/`Writer` field. The runtime package's build archive grew by 32 KB (1.89 → 1.93 MB, 1.7%: object code of `All`); `All` is neither generic nor inlinable, so generated packages' export data doesn't change.

## 4g. The matched route, for middleware and logging
**Status: Proposed** (2026-10-26, from the `nyth` assessment; (Q) points open).

**Why**: `nyth`'s page log (`src/lib/server/request.ts`, `pageLog.ts`) records one line per page after the response: time, request ID, method, **`context.routePattern`** (`"/fiction/[bookSlug]"`) and **`context.params`**, status, user agent, and on purpose "never the URL or its query: the route pattern and its params only". In Orbit, the router knows the matched `Route` (`Pattern`, `File`) and the params, but only inside `site.Middleware`; the user's middleware sees the request before and the response after, never the route. Logging `r.URL.Path` instead breaks `nyth`'s privacy rule, and re-matching the path in user code would duplicate Orbit's router. The assessment's sketch, `orbit.RouteOf(r) (pattern string, ok bool)`, can't work on its own: the `r` an outer middleware holds is never the one Orbit could write to unless something was attached to it before `next` was called. So the slot has to be attached first, `EnsureLocals`-style (§5b).

#### End-to-end example (recommended shape, (Q1) a)
```go
// cmd/server/pagelog.go
func pageLog(next http.Handler) http.Handler {
	return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		start := time.Now()
		r, route := orbit.EnsureRouteInfo(r)
		rec := &statusRecorder{ResponseWriter: w, status: http.StatusOK}
		next.ServeHTTP(rec, r)
		if route.Kind != orbit.RoutePage {
			return // static files, slash redirects, islands, 404s, the app's own routes
		}
		params := map[string]string{}
		for name, value := range route.Params() {
			params[name] = value
		}
		log.Record(Line{Time: start, Method: r.Method, Route: route.Pattern, Params: params, Status: rec.status})
	})
}
```
`GET /fiction/the-long-way` (page `src/pages/fiction/[bookSlug].orbit`) records `{"method":"GET","route":"/fiction/[bookSlug]","params":{"bookSlug":"the-long-way"},"status":200}`. It wraps `site.Middleware` (or the mux leading to `site.Handler()`) like any middleware that needs locals.

#### Rules
- **API** (runtime, stdlib only):
  ```go
  type RouteKind uint8
  const (
  	RouteNone     RouteKind = iota // Orbit didn't render this request (static file, slash redirect, next, never reached Orbit)
  	RoutePage                      // a page route matched (whatever the outcome: 200, redirect, error, ErrNotFound)
  	RouteNotFound                  // site.NotFound() answered: 404.orbit, or the empty 404
  	RouteIsland                    // a /_orbit/island/ request
  )
  type RouteInfo struct {
  	Kind    RouteKind
  	Pattern string // "/fiction/[bookSlug]"; "/404"; "/_orbit/island/[buildID]/[id]"
  	File    string // "src/pages/fiction/[bookSlug].orbit"; the deferred component's file; "" if none
  	// unexported: the route's param names (its own static slice) and values ([8]string inline, then a slice)
  }
  func EnsureRouteInfo(r *http.Request) (*http.Request, *RouteInfo) // the attached *RouteInfo, or attaches a new one
  func (ri *RouteInfo) Param(name string) string                    // like Ctx.Param: "" when absent
  func (ri *RouteInfo) Params() iter.Seq2[string, string]           // name, value, in the route's order
  ```
  `EnsureRouteInfo` works like `EnsureLocals` (§4): every middleware can call it and they share one value; it returns `r` unchanged when one is attached. Several middlewares (a logger, metrics) read the same struct.
- **Filled by Orbit** in `site.Middleware`, `site.Handler()` and `site.NotFound()` when a `*RouteInfo` is attached, as soon as the request is matched and **before rendering**, so an error, a panic or `ctx.Redirect` still leaves it set (`nyth` logs 500s with their route). What's recorded ((Q2)):
  | Request | `Kind` | `Pattern` | `File` | params |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | a page route | `RoutePage` | `Route.Pattern` | the page | the route's |
  | `site.NotFound()` (404.orbit or the empty 404), also through `Handler()` | `RouteNotFound` | `"/404"` | `src/pages/404.orbit`, or `""` | none |
  | `/_orbit/island/<buildID>/<id>` (every answer: 200, 400, 404, 405, 413, errors) | `RouteIsland` | `"/_orbit/island/[buildID]/[id]"` | the component's `.orbit` file when the ID is known | `buildID`, `id` |
  | static files (`/_orbit/*`, `public/`), trailing-slash redirects (§4e), requests passed to `next` | unchanged (`RouteNone` if nothing else set it) | | | |
  Decided by rule (Astro): Astro's middleware gets `context.routePattern` for pages, `"/404"` for the 404 route and `"/_server-islands/[name]"` (with its `name` param) for server islands, and doesn't run for static assets or before its trailing-slash redirect. A request that goes through Orbit twice (`site.Middleware` passes it to the app's mux, whose catch-all is `site.NotFound()`) gets the last value.
- **Params**: the names are the compiled route's own strings and the values are the router's (substrings of the escaped path, or their decoded copies, §4), so copying them into the `RouteInfo`'s inline array allocates nothing; a route with more than 8 params (the size of `Ctx`'s own storage) allocates once. The `Ctx`'s pooled storage is released after the request, but the copy belongs to the middleware, so the strings stay valid after `next` returns and may be retained.
- **Zero cost when unused**: a package-level flag, set by the first `EnsureRouteInfo` call (an atomic store once), gates Orbit's context lookup; a process that never calls it pays one atomic load per matched request. Once used, Orbit does one `r.Context().Value` lookup per request it serves.
- **Concurrency**: written on the request's goroutine before rendering and read after `next` returns, so no synchronization is needed; reading it from another goroutine during the request isn't supported (documented, like `Ctx`).
- **Elsewhere**: `WithErrorHandler`'s handler (§4c) gets the request carrying the slot, so `EnsureRouteInfo(r)` there returns the filled value (to log the pattern of a failed page); `orbittest.Get` fills it like a real request; `RenderComponent` doesn't (no route; decided by rule: simplest); dev and prod behave the same.

#### (Q) points
- **(Q1) Shape.**
  - (a) **Recommended**: the `EnsureRouteInfo` slot above. The idiom users already know from `EnsureLocals`, works without `src/locals`, plain net/http, testable. Costs 3 allocations per request in the middleware that attaches it (the `RouteInfo`, the context value, the request copy; measured below), as `EnsureLocals` does; 6 when a middleware attaches both. Name alternatives: `orbit.TrackRoute(r)`, `orbit.WithRouteInfo(r)`.
  - (b) A field on the user's `Locals`, filled by convention: `type Locals struct { Route orbit.RouteInfo; … }`; the generator already parses `src/locals` (§5b) and would make `app.New()` pass an accessor (`orbit.WithLocalsRoute(func(l *locals.Locals) *orbit.RouteInfo { return &l.Route })`). 0 allocations beyond `EnsureLocals` (measured: 3, the same as `EnsureLocals` alone), and pages could read `locals.Route` too. But it's a convention to learn, needs locals, and puts routing data in a user type.
  - (c) An interface on the `http.ResponseWriter`: Orbit walks `Unwrap()` (the `http.ResponseController` convention) looking for `interface{ SetOrbitRoute(RouteInfo) }`, which the app's status recorder implements. 0 allocations (loggers wrap `w` anyway), but any wrapper without `Unwrap` in between hides it silently, and an interface to implement is less discoverable than a function.
  - (d) Docs only: not workable. The app can't know the matched pattern without re-implementing Orbit's router, and logging the path breaks `nyth`'s rule.
- **(Q2) What's recorded beyond pages.**
  - (a) **Recommended**: the table above (Astro's set: pages, `/404`, islands).
  - (b) Pages only: `RouteNotFound` and `RouteIsland` dropped, and `Kind` becomes a `bool`. Simpler, but a request log can't tell an island or a 404 from a request Orbit never saw.
- **(Q3) Also set `r.Pattern`?** `ServeMux` writes the matched pattern into the request it was given, in place (`http.Request.Pattern`, Go 1.23), and some observability middleware reads `r.Pattern` after the handler. Orbit could do the same, with the pattern in ServeMux syntax precomputed per route (`/fiction/{bookSlug}`, `[...path]` → `{path...}`), at no cost.
  - (a) **Recommended**: no, not in the first release: writing into the caller's request is surprising outside a mux, it's invisible to any middleware that copied the request in between (`EnsureLocals` included), and Orbit's patterns aren't ServeMux's. `RouteInfo` covers the need; revisit if users ask.
  - (b) Yes, in addition to `RouteInfo`.

#### Cost (prototype `scratchpad/small-specs3/`, the real `orbit.EnsureLocals`, a model of `EnsureRouteInfo` and Orbit's fill, a request context as deep as `net/http`'s; Go 1.27.3, `testing.AllocsPerRun`; no timings, the machine was reserved for another agent's profiling)
| Case | allocs |
|---|---|
| unused: Orbit's lookup with nothing attached (before the flag) | 0 |
| `EnsureRouteInfo` + Orbit's fill + reading it | 3 |
| `EnsureLocals` alone (today) | 3 |
| `EnsureLocals` + `EnsureRouteInfo` | 6 |
| (Q1) b: the route in `Locals`, filled through `LocalsFromRequest` | 3 (0 extra) |
| `for name, v := range route.Params()` | 0 |

- Render path untouched: `TestRenderAllocs` (0) and `TestServeAllocs` (1) must stay as they are without a slot; a new `TestServeRouteInfoAllocs` asserts 0 allocations in Orbit's handler with a slot attached (the 3 are the middleware's).
- No codegen, dev-loop or LSP change.

#### Tests (what must exist)
- Each row of the table: page (200, `ctx.Redirect`, returned error, panic, `ErrNotFound` from a page, which stays `RoutePage`), `NotFound()` with and without `404.orbit`, an island (200, bad token, unknown ID, 405), a static file, a slash redirect in both modes, a request passed to `next`; params, percent-decoded values, a rest param, a route with more than 8 params.
- Two middlewares share one value; a request through `Middleware` then `NotFound` gets `RouteNotFound`; the value read in a `WithErrorHandler` handler; retained strings valid after the `Ctx` is reused.
- `AllocsPerRun`: the rows above; unused costs nothing.
- `orbittest.Get` fills it.

#### User-docs changes (with the implementation)
- `middleware.md`: "Logging the matched route" (the example above, the table, when it's filled).
- `reference/runtime-api.md`: `RouteInfo`, `RouteKind`, `EnsureRouteInfo`, `Param`, `Params`.
- `coming-from-astro.md`: `context.routePattern`/`context.params` in middleware → `EnsureRouteInfo`.
- `server.md` (the order of middleware around `site.Middleware`); `DECISIONS.md`: the decided-by-rule items above.

#### Out of scope
- The route inside pages and components (Astro's `Astro.routePattern`): a cheap `ctx.RoutePattern()` later, since `Ctx` knows its route.
- Setting `r.Pattern` ((Q3) b), route metrics helpers, and routes of the app's own mux (use its `r.Pattern`).

## 5. Assets (scripts and styles)
- **Scope ID**: `o-` + the first 8 hex chars of sha256 of the file's project-relative path with `/` separators (`src/components/Card.orbit`), so IDs are the same on every OS. It's added as an attribute to every HTML element in the template, but only when the component has a scoped `<style>`.
- **Scoped CSS**: parse with `tdewolff/parse/v2/css`. Add the attribute selector `[data-o-xxxx]` to the last compound of each selector (Astro's default `scopedStyleStrategy: "attribute"`, user decision at the dogfood checkpoint): +1 attribute specificity, so scoped rules beat global rules with the same selector whatever the bundle order. Within the compound it goes before the first pseudo-class or pseudo-element (`a:hover` → `a[data-o-…]:hover`), as in Astro.
  - Recurse into `@media`, `@supports` and `@layer`.
  - Skip the bodies of `@keyframes` and `@font-face`.
  - `:global(sel)` opts out locally; the scope then goes on the nearest compound outside the `:global(…)` (`.prose :global(p)` → `.prose[data-o-…] p`), and a fully `:global` selector isn't scoped. Pseudo-classes and pseudo-elements come after the scope (`p::first-line` → `p[data-o-…]::first-line`), as in Astro.
  - **Order in the page stylesheet**: the import graph in module-evaluation order: a component's imports first, in import order, then its own styles; each component once.
- **`is:inline`** is stripped from the emitted tag; every other attribute stays as written (Astro behavior).
- **Which scripts are processed** (Astro's rule): a `<script>` with no attributes, or only a `src` pointing at a local relative file, is processed and bundled. Any other attribute means it's left as-is, like `is:inline`: `type="application/ld+json"`, `type="text/template"`, `async`, `src="https://…"`, and so on.
- **Per-page graph**: a page's assets are its own scripts and styles plus those of every transitively imported component. This is static, from imports, like Astro: a component's script is included once per page, even if it's rendered conditionally or many times.
- **esbuild**: one build call with a plugin that serves virtual modules (`orbit:script/<id>.ts`, `orbit:style/<id>.css`), each with `resolveDir` set to its `.orbit` file's directory, so relative imports and the nearest `tsconfig.json` resolve from there.
  - One entry per page: `format=esm`, `bundle`, `minify`, hashed names under `/_orbit/`, built together with the lazy entries with esbuild's code splitting (§5e, implemented): a page's own blocks stay in its entry (page-qualified block modules), imported files shared by several entries and `import()` targets are chunks, `/_orbit/<name>-<hash>.js`.
  - A page with no scripts gets no JS entry and no `<script>` tag.
  - Bare imports resolve from `node_modules` if one exists.
  - **Implemented (milestone 6)**: block modules resolve to the `.orbit` file itself (file namespace, `?orbit-script=i` suffix) with content padded to the block's line and column, so esbuild's error positions and source maps are `.orbit` positions and `resolveDir`/tsconfig lookup come for free. `orbit generate` also bundles, in memory, so `generate` and `build` write the same `orbit_gen/` (about 8 ms on 200 components). Fonts and images imported from scripts/CSS are hashed into `/_orbit/`. An import of a `.orbit` file that isn't a block (`import { data } from "./X.orbit"` from a block script or a `.ts` file) loads one constant module, `export { data, json } from "orbit:client"`; `orbit:client` is the embedded runtime (`internal/assets/client/client.ts`, 368 B minified) and `orbit:types` is empty (§5c). The CLI binary grows by about 8 MB with esbuild; user servers don't link it.
  - **CSS imported from a script** (`import "flatpickr/dist/flatpickr.css"`) joins the page's stylesheet, unscoped, as esbuild bundles CSS imports by default (decided by coordinator: esbuild default). A lazy component's imported CSS stays in the page stylesheet too.
- **Lazy entries (`client:*`)**: each component that has a processed `<script>` and at least one lazy call site (`client:idle`, `client:visible` or `client:media`) anywhere gets its own esbuild entry, `orbit:lazy/<id>.ts`, which imports that component's script modules. It's built with the same settings, in the same build, into `/_orbit/<Name>-<hash>.js`; the imported files it shares with page bundles or other lazy entries are chunks it imports (§5e).
  - **Whole-subtree scope and eager wins, per page** (user decision: a directive covers the component's whole subtree):
    - A component is **eager on a page** if it's reachable from the page root (deferred islands included) by a path of call sites that crosses **no** lazy directive. Its scripts go in the page bundle.
    - Every other component with scripts that's reachable only through lazy call sites is **lazy on that page**: it gets no page-bundle entry, and the page lists its entry as a `lazy` asset `{ID, URL}`.
    - Entries stay **per component**, deduplicated across call sites. Each lazy *call site* gets a static list (in the page's lazy map) of the entries to `import()` when its strategy fires: the tagged component plus every lazy-on-this-page component in its static subtree.
    - A directive-less call inside a lazy subtree is part of that subtree, so it's lazy unless the same component is also reached eagerly elsewhere.
    - A nested lazy directive inside a lazy subtree takes its own components out of the outer list. Its own strategy is observed only after the outer one has fired.
  - Scripts stay once-per-page modules: the loader `import()`s each entry at most once, when the first instance meets its strategy. With several strategies on one page, whichever fires first loads it.
  - A module that queries the DOM when it loads sees only the instances present at that moment, not those that arrive later in a deferred island. So `styles-and-scripts.md` and `lazy-loading.md` recommend custom elements (`customElements.define` with `connectedCallback`) for the scripts of lazy and deferred components.
  - Code shared between a lazy entry and the page bundle (e.g. `src/scripts/util.ts`) is one chunk that both import, evaluated once per page (§5e; it was duplicated, with a module state per copy, before code splitting).
  - Styles are unaffected: a lazy component's CSS stays in the page's stylesheet.
- **Deferred components' assets**: a `server:defer` component and everything it imports stay in the page's static graph, so their CSS and scripts load with the page, and the island fragment needs no assets of its own. Those scripts run before the fragment arrives, which is another reason for custom elements.
- **The loader**: one small inline `<script type="module">` (TypeScript source in the framework's `loader/`, minified (islands about 1.1 KB, lazy about 1.3 KB; about 0.75 KB each gzipped) and embedded in the runtime, §4), plus `<style>orbit-island,orbit-lazy{display:contents}</style>`. Head injection adds it only to pages that need it: the islands part when `Route.Islands` is set, and the lazy part, with the page's `{id: url}` map, when the page has `lazy` assets. Both are decided statically from the page's graph, so islands nested in fragments are covered (decided by rule: consistent with the static asset graph above). The loader is inline, as Astro inlines its island scripts, which saves a request before the island fetches can start (decided by rule: Astro behavior). A strict CSP needs `'unsafe-inline'` or the script's hash; document this (CSP hashes are deferred, §12).
  - **Islands**: once the page is parsed, the loader fetches every `<orbit-island>` in parallel, with `GET <src>?p=<token>`, where `src` is `/_orbit/island/<buildID>/<id>` as written by `Defer` with the build ID of the instance that rendered it. When that URL would exceed 8000 characters (about 8 KB of props), it uses `POST` to the same path instead, with the token as a `text/plain` body (decided by rule: simplest limit that fits nginx's default 8 KB request-line buffer; Astro switches at 2048). Fetches use `redirect: "manual"`.
    - On a 2xx response, the placeholder is replaced by the fragment, parsed with `Range.createContextualFragment` so that inline scripts in it run (as in Astro, no wrapper remains). The new nodes are then scanned for `<orbit-island>` and `<orbit-lazy>` elements, so nested islands and lazy components inside islands work.
    - On any other status, or a network error, the fallback stays. In dev, the loader logs the status and the response body with `console.error`; in prod it stays silent (decided by rule: simplest).
  - **Lazy**: observes each `<orbit-lazy>` whose ID is in the map, according to its strategy, and `import()`s the entry when the first instance fires.
  - **Without JS**, island fallbacks stay, and lazy components are still rendered on the server; only their scripts never load.
- **TypeScript** (client side):
  - `<script>` content is **TypeScript by default**, like Astro: the virtual modules use `loader=ts`. Plain JS is valid TS, so nothing breaks.
  - Scripts can import `.ts`/`.tsx`/`.js` files from anywhere in the project (e.g. `src/scripts/`) and npm packages.
  - esbuild picks up `tsconfig.json` if present (`paths`, `target`, `jsx` settings).
  - esbuild **only strips types and doesn't type-check**. Type checking is done by `orbit check` through **Bun** (§6). `orbit new` scaffolds a `tsconfig.json` modeled on Astro's `strict` preset (`target`/`module` `ESNext`, `moduleResolution: Bundler`, `strict`, `noEmit`, `isolatedModules`, `rootDirs: [".", "orbit_gen/types"]`, `include: ["src/**/*.ts", "orbit_gen/types/*.d.ts"]`, §5c) and an empty `src/env.d.ts` for the user's global declarations (no `package.json` or `typescript` package is needed, since `bun check` bundles the checker).
  - `<script is:inline>` is emitted untouched, so it must be plain JS.
- **Manifest**:
  - Prod: the result is compiled into `orbit_gen/manifest`, mapping page → `[]Asset` (including its `lazy` entries and its `modulepreload` chunks, §5e).
  - Dev: esbuild uses an incremental `api.Context` that outputs to `.orbit/dev/_orbit/` and, after every rebuild, writes `.orbit/dev/manifest.json` atomically (temp file plus rename). With `ORBIT_DEV=1`, the app reads asset URLs from `$ORBIT_STATIC/manifest.json` (re-read when its mtime changes) instead of the compiled manifest, so a CSS/JS-only rebuild with new hashes needs no Go rebuild.

## 5b. Orbit as middleware, the server entry, and typed locals

### Orbit is an `http.Handler` middleware, and the user chooses its position
`site.Middleware(next)` works like this:
- If the request matches something Orbit owns, Orbit serves it:
  - `/_orbit/*` and `public/` files, for **GET and HEAD only**; other methods fall through to `next`.
  - page routes, for **all methods** (the page reads `ctx.Request.Method`). So `POST /blog/hello/like` reaches the user's mux only if no page matches it, and a `[...rest]` page claims every method under its prefix; document this for Orbit-first setups.
- Otherwise it calls `next.ServeHTTP` **untouched**. Nothing is buffered or written.

So the user's own `main.go` decides precedence just by composing handlers:

```go
// cmd/server/main.go  (scaffolded by `orbit new`, owned by the user)
func main() {
    site := app.New()                       // generated: example.com/blog/orbit_gen/app

    api := http.NewServeMux()
    api.HandleFunc("GET /api/posts", listPosts)
    api.HandleFunc("POST /blog/{slug}/like", like)   // can call orbit.RenderComponent(...)

    // A) Custom routes take precedence over pages: the mux's "/" is the least specific
    //    pattern, so any more specific route wins and everything else goes to Orbit.
    api.Handle("/", site.Handler())
    h := logging(theme(api))

    // B) Pages take precedence: Orbit first, falling back to the mux. Give the mux a
    //    catch-all so unmatched paths render 404.orbit instead of the mux's plain 404.
    // api.Handle("/", site.NotFound())
    // h := logging(theme(site.Middleware(api)))

    if err := orbit.ListenAndServe(h); err != nil {
        log.Fatal(err)
    }
}
```

- Because it's a plain `func(http.Handler) http.Handler`, Orbit can also sit inside chi (`r.Use(site.Middleware)` or `r.NotFound(site.Handler().ServeHTTP)`), Gin (via `gin.WrapH`), and so on. These embeddings aren't tested in the MVP (§12).
- **Middleware order**:
  - Middleware wrapped *outside* Orbit (logging, auth, CORS, gzip) applies to pages and custom routes alike.
  - Middleware placed *after* Orbit only sees requests Orbit didn't handle.
  - There is no Orbit-specific middleware type (no `OnRequest`; user decision). All middleware is plain `func(http.Handler) http.Handler` in the user's `main.go`; request-scoped data reaches pages through typed locals (below).
- **Inside `site.Middleware`** (prod and dev):
  1. Island endpoint `/_orbit/island/<buildID>/<id>` (GET, HEAD, POST; other methods get a 405): render a deferred component as a fragment (see "Deferred island requests" below). This prefix is reserved and is never a static file.
  2. Static file lookup (`/_orbit/*`, `public/`; GET/HEAD only).
  3. Page router (trie match; the trailing-slash 308 per mode, §4, §4e).
  4. Take the `*Locals` attached by `EnsureLocals`/`WithLocals` from the request context, if any (otherwise `LocalsOf` allocates lazily).
  5. Page `Render` into the pooled buffer, then head injection at the `HeadEnd` offset.
  6. Write the response once, with `Content-Length`.

  If none of steps 1–3 matches, it calls `next`. `site.Handler()` uses `NotFound()` (which renders `404.orbit`) as `next`.
- **Short-circuits** come from the page frontmatter: `return ctx.Redirect("/login", 302)`, `return orbit.ErrNotFound` (renders `404.orbit`), or `return err` (500). Middleware can also short-circuit the usual net/http way, by writing a response and not calling `next`.
