- **Response rewriting** (headers, status, body) uses a standard wrapping `http.ResponseWriter` in the user's middleware. Because page responses are buffered and written once (§4), the wrapper sees the complete HTML with assets injected.
- **Server entry**: `orbit.json` `"server": "./cmd/server"` (the default when the directory exists).
  - Without an entry, Orbit generates `orbit_gen/cmd/server`, which runs `orbit.ListenAndServe(app.New().Handler())`.
  - `orbit new` always scaffolds `cmd/server/main.go`, so the composition is visible from day one.
- **Dev and prod**: the same user `main` runs in both.
  - The dev server builds the entry and runs it with `ORBIT_DEV=1`, `ORBIT_ADDR=127.0.0.1:<port>`, `ORBIT_STATIC=.orbit/dev` (holding `_orbit/` and `manifest.json`) and `ORBIT_PUBLIC=public`. It also passes an `ORBIT_KEY` generated once per `orbit dev` session, so open pages' islands survive app restarts; an `ORBIT_KEY` the user has set wins.
  - `app.New()` reads these, so in dev the app itself serves `/_orbit/*` and `public/` (§7). In prod, `public/` is copied into `dist/client` and `ORBIT_PUBLIC` is unset.
  - `orbit.ListenAndServe` listens on `orbit.Addr()`. Users who start their own `http.Server` should use `orbit.Addr()` to stay dev-compatible; document this.
- **Rendering from custom handlers**:
  - `orbit.RenderComponent(w, r, LikeButton.Render, LikeButton.Props{…})` renders any component as a fragment, with no head injection.
  - Rendering a full page from a custom handler (`site.RenderPage`) is deferred (§12).
  - The user's Go code imports components through the generated declaration packages, e.g. `example.com/blog/orbit_gen/decl/components/likebutton`.

### Deferred island requests
A `server:defer` placeholder (§3.4) makes the browser request `/_orbit/island/<buildID>/<id>?p=<token>` (or POST it, §5). That request is handled in step 1 above:
- **Through the user's middleware**: it's an ordinary request from the browser, so it passes through every middleware wrapped around `site.Middleware` (logging, auth, cookies, `EnsureLocals`), exactly like a page request. In setup A it reaches Orbit through the mux's `/` catch-all. Its `locals` come from its own pass through the middleware; nothing from the page request carries over.
- **Opening the token**:
  - An unknown component ID (one never used with `server:defer`) gets a 404.
  - The token comes from `?p=` (GET, HEAD) or the request body (POST, read through `http.MaxBytesReader` with a 1 MiB limit; larger bodies get a 413, as in Astro).
  - It's opened (§3.4) with the component ID and build ID taken from the URL path. A missing, malformed or tampered token, or one sealed with another key, gets a 400 with an empty body (decided by rule: Astro answers 400). So does JSON that doesn't decode into `Props`.
- **Rendering**: the component renders into a pooled buffer with a fresh pooled `Ctx`. `ctx.Request` and `ctx.URL` are the island request, as in Astro, and the route param storage is empty (`ctx.Param` returns `""` for every name). The page's URL is in the `Referer` header, the pattern Astro documents: `page, err := url.Parse(ctx.Request.Referer())`. Browsers send the full page URL on same-origin fetches under the default `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` policy; a `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` removes it. Document this.
- **Response**: buffered and written once with `Content-Length`, `Content-Type: text/html; charset=utf-8`, no head injection. Headers:
  - `Cache-Control: private, no-store` by default. A component can replace it through `ctx.Header()`; guidance for public caching is deferred with island caching (§12).
  - `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`, so search engines don't index fragments (decided by rule: Astro sets the same header).
- **Errors**: `return err` or a panic gives a 500, and `orbit.ErrNotFound` gives a 404 with an empty body, not `404.orbit`, which is a full page (decided by rule: simplest). `ctx.Redirect` sends its 3xx, which the loader doesn't follow. In dev, a 500 body holds the error text with its `.orbit` position instead of the overlay page, and the loader logs it. Any non-2xx response keeps the fallback.
- **CSRF**: a CSRF middleware that requires a token on POST blocks islands whose props need POST. Exempt `/_orbit/island/`, or use Go's `http.CrossOriginProtection`, which lets same-origin fetches through. Document this.
- **Limitation**: placeholders inside fragments from `RenderComponent` (e.g. htmx partials) load only if the page they land in already has the islands loader. The MVP doesn't handle this (§12).

### Typed locals
Request-scoped data (the current user, a request ID, …) is a **user-declared struct** in the optional Go package `src/locals` (package `locals`):

```go
// src/locals/locals.go
package locals

type Locals struct {
    RequestID string
    Theme     string
}
```

- **Where it lives**: `src/locals/`, package `locals`, holding `type Locals struct`. *Decided by rule* (simplest clear convention, now that `src/middleware` has no other purpose; log it in `docs/DECISIONS.md`).
- **Detection**: the generator looks for `type Locals struct` in `src/locals` with `go/parser`. If there isn't one, it uses `orbit.NoLocals` (`struct{}`).
- **In every page and component that uses `locals`** (and always in the editor's tolerant mode), the generated `render` starts with `locals := orbit__rt.LocalsOf[orbit__locals.Locals](orbit__ctx)` (the runtime and `src/locals` are imported under internal aliases, §3.2), so `locals` has type `*locals.Locals`. The frontmatter and template then use `locals.User.Name` with full type checking, and gopls completes `locals.` in the editor. `LocalsOf` allocates a zero `Locals` lazily, once per request; components share the page's instance. Inside the body the variable `locals` shadows the package name; hoisted helpers that need the type import the package as usual.
- **Setting locals from net/http middleware** in `main.go`:

  ```go
  func requestInfo(next http.Handler) http.Handler {
      return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
          r, l := orbit.EnsureLocals[locals.Locals](r)
          l.RequestID = newID()
          l.Theme = "light"
          if c, err := r.Cookie("theme"); err == nil {
              l.Theme = c.Value
          }
          next.ServeHTTP(w, r)
      })
  }
  ```

  It must wrap `site.Middleware` (or the mux that leads to `site.Handler()`), so it runs before Orbit. `site.Middleware` picks the pointer up from the request context. Every middleware uses the same `r, l := orbit.EnsureLocals[locals.Locals](r)` line: the first one attaches the value, and later ones get the same pointer back, with `r` unchanged. `WithLocals` remains for attaching a value built elsewhere. Custom handlers read locals with `orbit.LocalsFromRequest[locals.Locals](r)`, which returns nil if no middleware attached any (§4). This makes locals the typed bridge between the user's HTTP stack and pages. Island requests (above) run through the same middleware, so deferred components see locals too.
- **Import rule**: `src/locals` (and anything it imports) must not import `orbit_gen/app`, because `app` → impl packages → `src/locals` would be an import cycle. Keep it a plain types package. The generator checks this and reports it clearly.

## 5c. Typed server→client bridge
**Status: Implemented** (2026-10-26; implementation notes at the end of this section). Accepted 2026-10-26: the user chose the recommended answer to every **(Q)** point: (Q1) per-component modules imported by relative path (`import { data } from "./LikeButton.orbit"`); (Q2) `orbit.JSON` on any attribute, several per element; (Q3) `set:json` typed too (`json(id)`); (Q4) single-quoted values; (Q5) nil slices and maps typed `| null`; (Q6) 64-bit ints are `number` with a docs note; (Q7) types in `orbit_gen/types/`; (Q8) TypeScript editor support inside `<script>` is a separate item, next after this one; (Q9) no `bun check` in `orbit dev`. Written before implementation, like §6.1. Replaces the §12 sketch; deviations from it are listed at the end.

**Goal**: data that a component hands to its own `<script>` keeps its Go type on the TypeScript side, so `orbit check` reports a script that reads a field the server doesn't send, or treats a nullable value as present, at the `.orbit` line:col. Nothing new at runtime beyond `encoding/json` and a ~180-byte accessor; no new dependency.

### End-to-end example
```go
// internal/posts/posts.go
type Post struct {
	ID        int64     `json:"id"`
	Slug      string    `json:"slug"`
	Title     string    `json:"title"`
	Tags      []string  `json:"tags"`
	Published time.Time `json:"published"`
	Likes     int       `json:"likes"`
	Author    *Author   `json:"author,omitempty"`
	draft     bool
}
type Author struct {
	Name string `json:"name"`
	URL  string `json:"url,omitempty"`
}
```
```orbit file=src/components/LikeButton.orbit
---
import "example.com/blog/internal/posts"

type Props struct {
	Post  posts.Post
	Liked bool
}
---
<like-button data-post={orbit.JSON(props.Post)} data-liked={orbit.JSON(props.Liked)}>
  <button type="button">♥ <span>{props.Post.Likes}</span></button>
</like-button>
<script>
  import { data } from "./LikeButton.orbit";

  class LikeButton extends HTMLElement {
    connectedCallback() {
      const post = data(this, "post"); // posts.Post
      let liked = data(this, "liked"); // boolean
      const count = this.querySelector("span")!;
      this.querySelector("button")!.addEventListener("click", async () => {
        const res = await fetch(`/blog/${post.slug}/like`, { method: "POST" });
        if (res.ok) count.textContent = String(post.likes + (liked ? 0 : 1));
      });
      this.title = post.tags.join(", ");
    }
  }
  customElements.define("like-button", LikeButton);
</script>
```
Rendered (single-quoted value, (Q4)):
```html
<like-button data-post='{"id":42,"slug":"hello","title":"Hello \u003cworld\u003e","tags":["go"],"published":"2026-10-26T12:00:00Z","likes":7}' data-liked='false'>…
```
Generated by the typed pass (§3.3 pass 2), in `orbit_gen/types/` ((Q7)):
```ts
// orbit_gen/types/go.d.ts — every Go named type reached from an orbit.JSON or set:json value
// Code generated by orbit. DO NOT EDIT.
declare module "orbit:types" {
  export namespace posts {
    /** example.com/blog/internal/posts.Post */
    export interface Post {
      id: number;
      slug: string;
      title: string;
      tags: string[] | null;
      published: string;
      likes: number;
      author?: Author;
    }
    /** example.com/blog/internal/posts.Author */
    export interface Author {
      name: string;
      url?: string;
    }
  }
}
```
```ts
// orbit_gen/types/src/components/LikeButton.orbit.d.ts — this component's own data
// Code generated by orbit. DO NOT EDIT.
import type { posts } from "orbit:types";

/** The orbit.JSON data-* attributes written by src/components/LikeButton.orbit, by dataset name. */
export interface Data {
  /** data-post (LikeButton.orbit:9:14) */
  post: posts.Post;
  /** data-liked (LikeButton.orbit:9:49) */
  liked: boolean;
}
export declare function data<K extends "post" | "liked">(el: Element, key: K): Data[K];
```
`orbit check`:
```text
src/components/LikeButton.orbit:24:20: error TS18047: 'post.tags' is possibly 'null'.
```
(A nil `[]string` encodes as `null`, (Q5).) `data(this, "psot")` gives `…:17:31: error TS2345: Argument of type '"psot"' is not assignable to parameter of type '"liked" | "post"'.`; removing `data-liked` from the template, or changing `Likes` to a `string` in Go, turns the script's uses into errors on the next `orbit check`. All three were checked with Bun 1.4.4's `bun check` on a hand-written copy of these files (spike, below).

### Server side: `orbit.JSON`
- **API** (runtime, §4):
  ```go
  type JSONValue struct{ /* v any */ }          // opaque; the zero value encodes as null
  func JSON(v any) JSONValue                      // v encoded exactly as json.Marshal(v) would
  func (w *Writer) JSONAttr(pos string, v JSONValue)  // generated: an attribute value, single-quoted (Q4)
  func (w *Writer) JSON(pos string, v any)        // set:json, now with the position (bug fix, below)
  ```
  `orbit.JSON` is a plain function returning a value type, so gopls completes and type-checks it like any Go call, and pass 1 (no types) still compiles: it goes through the `Attr`/`Expr` fallbacks, which gain a `JSONValue` case. No generics (§4b: a generic runtime type would be re-instantiated in every generated package).
- **Where it's allowed (Q2)**: as the value of **any attribute** of an HTML element: `data-*` (typed, below), but also `hx-vals={orbit.JSON(m)}` (htmx) or `x-data={orbit.JSON(state)}` (Alpine), which take JSON; in a spread (`orbit.Attrs{"data-x": orbit.JSON(v)}`, untyped); and in content, where it's written HTML-escaped like a string (`<pre>{orbit.JSON(cfg)}</pre>`; decided by rule: simplest, it's what `Expr` does with any value, and rejecting it would need a pass-2-only error). On a component tag it's just a Go value of type `orbit.JSONValue` passed to a prop. A `JSONValue` attribute is always written, `false` and `null` included (unlike `Attr`'s bool rule).
- **Several per element**, any `data-*` name; no fixed `data-orbit` (Q2).
- **Typed write** (§3.3): pass 2 classifies an attribute value whose static type is `orbit.JSONValue` as `WriteAttrJSON`: ` name='` and `'` are merged into the neighbouring `Raw` constants and the value is `orbit__w.JSONAttr("src/components/LikeButton.orbit:9:24", <expr>)`, with the user's expression copied verbatim (directives unchanged). The fallback `Attr(name, JSONValue)` gives the same bytes (golden typed-vs-fallback test), without the position.
- **Escaping** (attribute context): `encoding/json` already escapes `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028 and U+2029 inside strings (`<`…), and JSON has no `&` outside strings, so the only character left to escape is the quote: `'` → `&#39;` in a single-quoted value (Q4), or `"` → `&quot;` in a double-quoted one. `JSONAttr` doesn't rely on `encoding/json`'s HTML-escaping default: like `Writer.JSON` today, it escapes `<`, `>`, `&` itself (a no-op scan when they're absent). Result: the attribute can't be broken out of, and the browser's attribute decoding gives back the exact JSON text. A `FuzzJSONAttr` test asserts it (decode(escape(json)) == json, no unescaped quote).
- **Encoding path (cost)**: each `Writer` keeps one `json.Encoder` (created with the pooled `Writer`, so 0 allocations per render) writing into a small escaping `io.Writer` over the pooled buffer; the encoder's trailing newline is truncated. No intermediate `[]byte` (saves `json.Marshal`'s result copy). Errors: the buffer is truncated back to the attribute's start before the sticky error is set. `set:json`'s `Writer.JSON` switches to the same path.
- **Errors**:
  - **Generate time** (pass 2, at the `orbit.JSON` argument; walk shared with §3.4's `jsonWalker`, which accepts interfaces here since nothing is decoded): func, chan, complex and `unsafe.Pointer` types, and map keys that aren't strings, integers or `encoding.TextMarshaler`s, anywhere in the value's type (fields tagged `json:"-"` and unexported fields skipped, as `encoding/json` does): `src/components/LikeButton.orbit:9:36: orbit.JSON: Post.Notify has type func(), which encoding/json can't encode`. These always fail at runtime, so failing early is safe. A type implementing `json.Marshaler` or `encoding.TextMarshaler` is accepted whatever its kind.
  - **Render time**: what only the value can show (NaN or ±Inf floats, a pointer cycle, an `any` field holding a func, a `MarshalJSON` error) is a render error carrying the `.orbit` position passed to `JSONAttr`: `src/components/LikeButton.orbit:9:24: orbit.JSON: json: unsupported value: NaN`. A typed write without a pass 2 (LSP tolerant mode, a package with type errors) uses `Attr`, whose error has no position (that output is never served: the build fails, §3.3).
  - **Bug fix in the same change** (found while writing this): `set:json` encode errors don't carry the `.orbit` position today. `Writer.JSON(v)` sets `orbit: set:json: …` and codegen passes no position (`internal/compiler/testdata/codegen/card/pages_index.render.go.golden`), although §2.3 and `styles-and-scripts.md` ("fails with an error at the `.orbit` position of the `set:json`") say it does. `Writer.JSON(pos, v)` fixes it the same way.

### Client side: per-component data modules (Q1)
- **`import { data } from "./LikeButton.orbit"`**: a block script (or any `.ts` file under `src/`) imports a component's `.orbit` file **by relative path** to get **that component's data contract**: `data(el, key)`, typed from the component's own `orbit.JSON` uses, plus `json(id)` for its `set:json` blocks (Q3), and the types `Data` / `JSONBlocks`. It never imports the component itself (still impossible: no framework islands).
- **Keys**: one per `data-*` attribute of an HTML element in the component's template (slot content included, since it's compiled in the parent; spreads excluded) whose value is a **direct call** `orbit.JSON(arg)` (the runtime's function, recognized through the file's import spec, so an aliased or shadowed `orbit` works; no `types.Info.Uses` map needed). The key is the attribute's **dataset name**, the DOM's rule (`data-author-name` → `authorName`), as users already read it with `el.dataset` (decided by rule: web standard; simplest). A `JSONValue` that isn't a direct call (a variable, a helper returning one) gives the key with type `unknown`. Names with characters other than `[a-z0-9-]` after lowercasing aren't keys.
- **Type of a key**: the TS mapping (below) of `arg`'s static type. The same key written several times (two elements, `{if}` branches) gets the union of their types, deduplicated (`posts.Post | posts.Summary`).
- **`Data[K]` is what the component may write**, not proof that `el` carries it: an element without the attribute throws at runtime (`orbit: <like-button> has no data-post attribute`).
- **Which mismatches are caught**, for the options considered:

  | Mismatch | (A) sketch: `data<Post>(el)` from `orbit:types` | **(B) per-component module** (recommended) | (C) registry keyed by custom-element tag | (D) magic `orbit:self` specifier |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | script uses a field/type the Go type doesn't have | ✓ (for the `T` the user picked) | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
  | script picks the wrong type (`Post` vs `Summary`) | ✗ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
  | key typo, or the attribute removed from the template | ✗ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
  | element read isn't the one carrying the data | ✗ | ✗ (runtime error) | ✓ for custom elements, via `this` | ✗ |
  | plain elements (`<button data-x>`), not custom elements | ✓ | ✓ | ✗ | ✓ |
  | works in `bun check`, the user's TS server for `.ts` files, and a future TS proxy, with standard resolution | ✓ | ✓ (`rootDirs`, below) | ✓ (global declarations) | ✗: TS has no per-importer resolution of a bare specifier; `orbit check` would have to rewrite the import |
  | cost | named types only | + one small `.d.ts` per component with data | + a global tag map, a runtime base class (~300 B), collisions between components using the same tag | as (B), plus extraction rewriting |

  (B) is the only one that's both per-component and standard TypeScript: `./LikeButton.orbit` resolves to `LikeButton.orbit.d.ts`, which `rootDirs` overlays from `orbit_gen/types/src/…` onto `src/…`. Its cost is a self-import by file name (renaming the file makes TS report `Cannot find module './LikeButton.orbit'`, loudly, at the import). It also gives `.ts` files typed access to any component's data (`import { data } from "../components/LikeButton.orbit"` in `src/scripts/like.ts`), which the editor checks today (Q8). (C) could be added later on top of (B) for custom elements.
- **Runtime** (`orbit:client`, embedded in the CLI as TypeScript and served by the esbuild plugin; 180 B minified for `data`, ~350 B with `json`, tree-shaken, deduplicated per page bundle):
  ```ts
  export function data(el: Element, key: string): unknown   // JSON.parse(el.getAttribute("data-" + kebab(key))), throws if absent
  export function json(id: string): unknown                 // JSON.parse(document.getElementById(id).textContent), throws if absent
  ```
  `orbit:client`'s own declarations are generic and untyped (`data<T = unknown>(el, key): T`), for attributes written through spreads or by other code; the docs recommend the component module.
- **esbuild** (§5): an import of any `*.orbit` file from a block script or a `.ts` file resolves to one constant virtual module, `export { data, json } from "orbit:client"` (types are erased; the old error `a script can't import a .orbit file` goes away). esbuild never needs pass 2's results, so its input key (§7.1) doesn't change when only types change. `orbit:types` resolves to an empty module (only `import type` makes sense; esbuild drops type-only imports anyway).

### Type mapping Go → TS (follows `encoding/json`, v1 semantics)
`types.Unalias` first. Named types become **named TS declarations** (needed for recursion, readable in hover); everything else is inlined.

| Go (static type) | TS | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `bool` | `boolean` | |
| ints, uints, floats (`int64`, `uint64`, `uintptr` too) | `number` | precision past 2⁵³: no warning (Q6); use the `,string` option for such IDs |
| `string`, `orbit.HTML`, `orbit.URL` | `string` | |
| `,string` tag option on a bool/int/uint/float/string field (or a pointer to one) | `string` (`string \| null` for a pointer) | as `encoding/json` |
| `[]byte` (slice of a `uint8` kind that isn't a marshaler) | `string \| null` | base64; `[N]byte` arrays stay `number[]` (v1) |
| `[]T` | `T[] \| null` | nil slice → `null` (Q5) |
| `[N]T` | `T[]` | tuples not used (simplest) |
| `map[K]V` (K: string kind, integer kind, or `TextMarshaler`) | `Record<string, V> \| null` | keys are always JSON strings |
| `*T` | `T \| null` | |
| `struct` | interface (named) or `{ … }` (anonymous) | fields below |
| interfaces (`any`, `error`, others) | `unknown` | |
| `time.Time` | `string` | RFC 3339 (`MarshalJSON`) |
| `time.Duration` | `number` | nanoseconds (an `int64`) |
| `json.Number` | `number` | written as a number literal |
| `json.RawMessage` | `unknown` | |
| other types whose static type implements `json.Marshaler` | `unknown` | (later: an override, Out of scope) |
| other types implementing `encoding.TextMarshaler` (value receiver) | `string` | `netip.Addr`, UUID types, … |
| a marshaler only on `*T`, at a `T` position | `unknown` | whether `encoding/json` calls it depends on addressability; `unknown` is never wrong (simplest) |
| func, chan, complex, `unsafe.Pointer`, bad map keys | **generate-time error** | see Errors |
| generic instance `Page[Post]` | `Page<posts.Post>`, with `interface Page<T>` | type parameters map through |

**Struct fields**, exactly `encoding/json`'s `typeFields`:
- Exported fields only (and embedded structs, exported or not); `json:"-"` skips; `json:"-,"` is a field named `-`; the tag name renames; non-identifier names are quoted (`"my-field": string`).
- `omitempty` → optional (`name?: T`), except on a non-pointer struct type, where `omitempty` has no effect; on a pointer, slice or map it also drops `| null` (a nil value is omitted, so a present one isn't null). `omitzero` → optional, and drops `| null` the same way (nil is the zero value; an empty non-nil slice or map is kept).
- **Embedded structs are promoted** with Go's visibility rules and `encoding/json`'s tie-breaks (shallowest depth wins; at equal depth a tagged field wins; otherwise the conflicting fields are all dropped, silently, as `encoding/json` does). Fields promoted through an embedded **pointer** are optional (a nil embedded pointer omits them). An embedded field with a tag name, or of a non-struct type, is a normal field.

**Names**:
- One namespace per Go package, named after the package (`posts.Post`). Two packages with the same name get the shortest distinguishing suffix of their import paths joined with `_` (`internal_posts`, `legacy_posts`). Types declared in an `.orbit` frontmatter (decl or impl package) go in a namespace named after the file's base name (`LikeButton.link`), disambiguated the same way. Names that are TS keywords or predeclared type names (`default`, `string`, …) get a `_` suffix. Each declaration carries its Go path in a doc comment.
- Deterministic output (sorted by namespace, then name), so `orbit_gen/` stays reproducible (§6, committing `orbit_gen/`).

**Unchecked units** (a package with type errors, unreadable export data; §3.3 Fallback): the component's keys are known from pass 1, but not their types, so its module is written with every key `unknown` (deterministic: generate output depends only on sources). `go build` fails on those units anyway.

### Where the types live and how tools see them (Q7)
- **Files**: `orbit_gen/types/go.d.ts` (`declare module "orbit:types"`), `orbit_gen/types/client.d.ts` (`declare module "orbit:client"`, constant), and `orbit_gen/types/<path>.orbit.d.ts` for each `.orbit` file with at least one key (mirroring `src/`). Written by the same `WriteGenerated` as the Go files (only changed files rewritten; stale ones removed), so they're gitignored with `orbit_gen/`, covered by `orbit doctor`'s freshness check, and committed along with it by projects that commit `orbit_gen/`.
- **Starter `tsconfig.json`** (`orbit new`): adds `"rootDirs": [".", "orbit_gen/types"]` and `"include": ["src/**/*.ts", "orbit_gen/types/*.d.ts"]` (decided by rule for the include: Astro ≥ 5 adds its generated `.astro/types.d.ts` to `include` the same way). `src/env.d.ts` is unchanged.
- **`orbit check`** (§6): the generated `.orbit/check/tsconfig.json` adds `"../../orbit_gen/types"` to its `rootDirs` (it already sets `["../..", "."]`, replacing the user's) and `"../../orbit_gen/types/*.d.ts"` to `include`, so the check works on projects whose `tsconfig.json` wasn't migrated. Verified with `bun check` (spike): `./LikeButton.orbit` from `.orbit/check/src/components/LikeButton.orbit.0.ts` resolves through both `rootDirs` entries.
- **Editor**: the user's TS server (VS Code's built-in one) sees the types in `.ts` files under `src/` through the user's `tsconfig.json`; without `orbit_gen/types/` (fresh clone before `orbit generate`) it reports `Cannot find module './X.orbit'`, like Go reports a missing `orbit_gen/` package.
- **Existing projects (migration note)**: add the two `tsconfig.json` entries for editor support; `orbit check` needs nothing. `orbit doctor` gets a ⚠ check: the project uses `orbit.JSON` or `set:json` and `tsconfig.json` lacks the `orbit_gen/types` `rootDirs` entry (fix: the two lines).

### When it's regenerated (dev-loop cost)
- The types are a by-product of pass 2 (§3.3), so they're written by `orbit generate`, `build`, `dev` and `check`, on exactly the edits that already run pass 2: a frontmatter/template edit re-checks its impl package; a `Props` edit its importers; a user `.go`/`go.mod`/`orbit.json` edit everything. Per-unit results (keys, types, the named types reached) are kept in the resident checker, and `go.d.ts` is re-emitted from their union (microseconds; below).
- **No extra Go build and no extra esbuild**: `.d.ts` files aren't Go (cmd/go ignores them), `orbit_gen/` isn't watched (§7), and esbuild's input doesn't depend on types. An edit that only changes types costs a few file writes.
- **`orbit dev` doesn't run `bun check`** (Q9).
- **The LSP neither writes nor serves the types**: it never writes `orbit_gen/` (§9; the types are no exception, since `orbit dev`/`generate` keep them current), and it has no TypeScript features to serve them to (Q8). A future TS proxy could build them in memory from the typed check it already runs (§9 Startup step 2).

### Errors and positions
- TS errors in block scripts map back through `internal/check`'s existing per-script offset table: nothing is injected into the extracted script, so positions need no new mapping (example above).
- Errors inside generated `.d.ts` files would be Orbit bugs; they're printed with their `orbit_gen/types/…` path, prefixed `internal:` (as the LSP does, §9).
- The generate-time and render-time Go errors are at `.orbit` positions (Server side, Errors). They show in `orbit build`, `orbit dev`'s overlay and `orbit check`'s Go half, like `server:defer`'s JSON check; the LSP doesn't show pass-2 errors today, and this doesn't change that.

### (Q) points
1. **(Q1) How a script's types connect to the data**: (B) per-component module imported by relative path, `data(el, key)` keyed by dataset name, typed from that component's `orbit.JSON` uses (recommended; table above), vs. (A) the sketch's hand-picked `data<T>(el)`, (C) a global registry keyed by custom-element tag, (D) a magic `orbit:self` specifier.
2. **(Q2) Server API**: `orbit.JSON(v)` on any attribute (typed for `data-*` on HTML elements), several per element, also allowed in content and spreads (recommended), vs. a fixed `data-orbit` attribute (one per element, the sketch) or a directive (`data-post:json={v}`, which gopls can't see as Go).
3. **(Q3) `set:json` gets types too**: `json(id)` in the same module, keyed by the script's static `id` (a dynamic `id` gives no key) (recommended: same machinery, a few lines), vs. `data-*` only.
4. **(Q4) Quoting**: single-quoted values with `'` → `&#39;` (recommended: measured −43% raw bytes, −16% gzip on a 50-post list; readable in view-source) vs. double quotes with `"` → `&quot;` (every other Orbit attribute, and Astro's `<astro-island props="…">`). Browsers decode both to the same JSON.
5. **(Q5) Nil slices and maps**: honest `T[] | null` / `Record<…> | null`, with `omitempty`/`omitzero` documented as the way to get a non-null type (recommended: that's what `encoding/json` sends; strict TS then catches the `null` case, the point of the bridge), vs. assume non-nil (unsound), vs. Orbit encoding nil as `[]`/`{}` (json v2's default, but then `set:json`, `server:defer` and `json.Marshal` would disagree).
6. **(Q6) 64-bit integers**: plain `number` and a docs note (recommended: `int` is 64-bit, so a warning would fire on almost every struct), vs. a generate-time warning (the sketch), vs. `bigint` (wrong: `JSON.parse` returns numbers).
7. **(Q7) Location**: `orbit_gen/types/` (recommended: one generated directory with one lifecycle; `.orbit/` stays a deletable cache, §1; doctor freshness and the commit option cover it) vs. `.orbit/types/` (the sketch; Astro's `.astro/types.d.ts` analogue).
8. **(Q8) Editor support**: today the benefit lands in **`orbit check`** (block scripts and `.ts` files) and in the **editor for `.ts` files only**: TS inside `.orbit` `<script>` has no language features (§9). Docs show moving logic into `src/…/*.ts` (or `<script src="./like-button.ts">`) for editor checking. Recommended: keep TS proxying a **separate item** with its own spec (it needs `typescript` and a Node runtime, or a TS server that Bun doesn't ship; virtual documents per block script placed like `orbit check`'s extraction), next in line after this one; (B) needs nothing extra from it. Alternative: make it part of this item (much larger; new editor-time requirement).
9. **(Q9) `orbit dev` and `bun check`**: not in this item (recommended: the dev loop stays ~0.2 s; `bun check` is ~15 ms on a small project but grows with scripts, and its errors would need an overlay design) vs. a background `bun check` after each rebuild, printed to the terminal only (non-blocking; measure at 200 files first).

Decided by rule (to log in `docs/DECISIONS.md`): `orbit.JSON` also allowed in content and spreads (simplest); keys are dataset names (web standard); `tsconfig.json` `include` entry (Astro); generate-time errors only for always-failing types (Go stdlib: `json.Marshal` fails on the type); a pointer-receiver-only marshaler at a value position maps to `unknown` (simplest that's never wrong); `[N]T` → `T[]` (simplest).

### Cost (measured in a throwaway spike, `scratchpad/bridge-spike/`; Ryzen 7 9700X, 16 threads, Go 1.27.3, load average 0.2–0.8)
- **Encoding** (`GOMAXPROCS=1`, `b.Loop`, a 7-field `Post` with a `time.Time`, a slice and a pointer, 209 B of JSON):
  - reused per-Writer `json.Encoder` into the buffer with attribute escaping: **740 ns, 2 allocs / 224 B**; `json.Marshal` + escape (today's `Writer.JSON` shape): 795 ns, 3 allocs / 448 B; the same with a pointer argument: 580 ns, **0 allocs**.
  - Of the 2 allocations, 1 is boxing the struct into `any` (at the `orbit.JSON` call) and 1 is internal to `encoding/json`: **Go 1.27's `encoding/json` runs on the json/v2 engine by default** (`GOEXPERIMENT=jsonv2` is on in `internal/buildcfg`), which copies a non-addressable value with `reflect.New` before encoding. Orbit's own code adds 0.
  - a 2-field struct: 185 ns, 2 allocs, vs. 10 ns and 0 allocs for the same two values as plain typed `data-*` attributes (§3.3). The docs keep recommending plain `data-*` for scalars and `orbit.JSON` for structured data.
- **AllocsPerRun rule (§4b)**: `orbit.JSON` is user data, like `Expr` and `server:defer` props, so it's outside the zero-allocation "typical page"; §4b's "what may still allocate" list gains it. A test asserts that `JSONAttr` adds no allocation beyond `encoding/json`'s own (0 for a pointer, equal to `json.NewEncoder(io.Discard).Encode` for a value).
- **Size**: the 209-byte value is 389 bytes double-quoted with `&quot;` (+86%) and stays 209 single-quoted (no `'` in it). 50 such elements: 19,970 vs. 11,470 B raw, 746 vs. 626 B gzipped (the repetition flatters gzip).
- **Generate**: a go/types walk plus TS emission for `Page[Post]` (4 named types, recursion, generics) takes **1.9 µs and 53 allocs per use**, without the method-set checks for marshalers (estimate ×2–3 with them, cached per named type). At 200 uses, well under 1 ms of a ~70 ms `orbit generate` (§12 Dev-loop speed work); writing the `.d.ts` files is a few more file writes. Measured with `bench/devloop` when implemented; no change to the template-edit loop is expected (no Go or esbuild work added).
- **Client**: `data` is 180 B minified (`json` adds ~170 B), only in bundles that use it.
- **`orbit check`**: `bun check` on the spike's two files: 15 ms; one more `.d.ts` per component with data.

### Tests (what must exist)
- **Runtime**: `JSON`/`JSONAttr`/`Writer.JSON(pos, v)` escaping (quotes, `<`, `&`, U+2028/9, invalid UTF-8), the position in render errors (and `set:json`'s), the buffer truncated on error, `Attr`/`Expr`/`Spread` with a `JSONValue`, `FuzzJSONAttr`; `AllocsPerRun` as above; `BenchmarkJSONAttr` (small and typical values, value vs. pointer) with `b.ReportAllocs()`.
- **Compiler**: the typed-vs-fallback golden test extended with `JSONValue` attributes (byte-identical); golden `.d.ts` files in `internal/compiler/testdata/types/` (`-update`), covering every mapping row, embedded-field promotion and conflicts, name collisions between packages and with TS keywords, generics, recursive types, frontmatter types, unions of one key, non-direct `JSONValue` values (`unknown`), aliased/shadowed `orbit` imports, and unchecked units; the **type-mapping table as a table-driven test** (Go type source → TS text); generate-time errors with positions (golden diagnostics).
- **Conformance** (the guard against drifting from `encoding/json`): for each mapping row, sample values (including zero/nil ones) are encoded with `json.Marshal` and written as `const v: <TS type> = <json>;` into a temp project, plus negative samples with `// @ts-expect-error`; one `bun check` over all of it (skipped with a notice without Bun, like the loader tests).
- **`internal/check`**: a project with a mismatching script reports the `.orbit` position; the generated tsconfig's `rootDirs`/`include`; a missing types directory gives `TS2307` at the import.
- **`internal/assets`**: `./X.orbit` from a block script and from a `.ts` file bundles the runtime; `orbit:client`; `orbit:types` is empty; the runtime's minified size is asserted (≤ 400 B).
- **Docs**: `internal/docstest` runs the TS half of `orbit check` (when Bun is present) on doc pages with `orbit.JSON`, so the docs' examples type-check and an `expect-error` block shows the mismatch message.
- **End to end**: `examples/blog`'s `LikeButton` uses `orbit.JSON`; `TestExampleBlog` checks that the rendered attribute parses as JSON and that `orbit check` passes; a dev test checks that an edit changing only an `orbit.JSON` argument's type rewrites the `.d.ts` without an esbuild rebuild.
- **`orbit doctor`**: the tsconfig ⚠ check.

### User-docs changes (with the implementation)
- `components.md` "Passing data to client scripts": a new first subsection, typed data with `orbit.JSON` and the component module (the example above), when to keep plain `data-*`.
- `styles-and-scripts.md`: "Passing data from the server to scripts" (same, linking to components.md), "Importing TypeScript files" (importing `../components/X.orbit` from `.ts` files), "Type checking with `orbit check`" (what's checked, the error format, the `null` tip with `omitempty`).
- New `reference/client-modules.md`: `orbit:client`, `./X.orbit` modules, `orbit:types`, the full mapping table and naming rules.
- `reference/runtime-api.md`: `JSON`, `JSONValue`, the Writer hooks (`JSONAttr`, `JSON(pos, v)`), `Attr`/`Expr` escaping for `JSONValue`.
- `reference/template-syntax.md`: `orbit.JSON` attribute values; `set:json` typed with `json(id)`.
- `project-structure.md`: `orbit_gen/types/`, the starter `tsconfig.json`; `editor-setup.md`: what TypeScript support covers (`.ts` files yes, `<script>` blocks not yet); `reference/cli.md`: generate/check write and use the types, the doctor check.
- `troubleshooting.md`: `Cannot find module './X.orbit'` (run `orbit generate`; tsconfig `rootDirs`), the unsupported-type error, the missing-attribute runtime error; and the old `a script can't import a .orbit file` entry removed (also `docs/DECISIONS.md` line "Importing a `.orbit` file from a script is an error").
- `coming-from-astro.md`: `define:vars` row points to `orbit.JSON`; `performance.md`: the encoding cost; `lazy-loading.md`: works the same in deferred and lazy components.
- `styles-and-scripts.md` and §2.3: the `set:json` position claim becomes true with the fix.

**Plan updates on acceptance**: §2.3 (`orbit.JSON` values, `set:json` typing), §3.3 (`WriteAttrJSON`, the types by-product), §4 (API), §4b (the allocation list), §5 (esbuild modules), §6 (`orbit check` tsconfig, doctor), §6 `orbit new` (starter tsconfig), §10 (files).

### Out of scope
- TypeScript language features inside `.orbit` `<script>` (Q8, separate item).
- A custom-element registry/base class keyed by tag (option C), and typed `HTMLElementTagNameMap` entries.
- Literal unions from Go constants (`type Status string` with consts → `"draft" | "published"`): Go doesn't close the set, so it'd be unsound without an opt-in.
- Overriding the TS type of a `json.Marshaler` type (a method or comment), and stdlib marshalers beyond the table (e.g. `*big.Int`).
- Runtime validation of the parsed data, and the client→server direction (typed form actions, §12).
- `bun check` in `orbit dev` (Q9).

### Implementation notes
- **Code**: runtime `json.go` (`JSON`, `JSONValue`, `JSONAttr`, `Writer.JSON(pos, v)`, a per-Writer `json.Encoder` over an escaping sink); compiler `codegen_json.go` (`Output.JSONSites`, direct-call detection by the file's runtime import names, dataset names), `typed_bridge.go` (keys and types per unit, `TypedUnit.Types`, generate-time errors), `tstypes.go`/`tstypes_emit.go` (mapper, `typeFields` port, naming, `EmitTypes`); `project.GenFiles` writes `orbit_gen/types/`; `internal/assets` `client.go`; `internal/check` tsconfig; `internal/doctor` `tsconfig` check; `orbit dev` keeps its early build when only `.d.ts` files were written.
- **Positions**: `JSONAttr`'s is the attribute value's `{`; `set:json`'s is the `set:json` attribute.
- **Measured** (Ryzen 7 9700X, `GOMAXPROCS=1`): `JSONAttr` typical post 510 ns / 1 alloc by value, 365 ns / 0 allocs by pointer; 2-field struct 130 ns / 1 alloc, 95 ns / 0 by pointer; plain typed attributes 13 ns. `orbit:client` 368 B minified. Mapper: 5.3 µs / 68 allocs cold for `Page[Post]`, 7 ns cached.
- **Deviations and additions** (small, logged in `docs/DECISIONS.md` "Typed bridge"): `JSONValue` implements `json.Marshaler`, and `set:json` unwraps a `JSONValue`; `set:json` values get the same generate-time unsupported-type check as `orbit.JSON` arguments; a component without keys has no module (importing it is `TS2307`); named non-struct types (`time.Duration`) are `type X = …` declarations rather than inlined; tag names follow Go 1.27's json/v2-engine parsing on malformed tags; fallback errors without a position read `orbit: orbit.JSON: …`; the mapper goldens are in `testdata/tstypes/` and the pipeline goldens in `testdata/types/`.

### Deviations from the §12 sketch, and why
- `data<T>(el)` on a fixed `data-orbit` → a per-component `data(el, key)` over any `data-*` attribute: the sketch couldn't catch a wrong `T` or a missing attribute (Q1, Q2).
- `.orbit/types/orbit-types.d.ts` → `orbit_gen/types/` with one module per component plus `go.d.ts` (Q7).
- The int64 warning is dropped (Q6); slices and maps are `| null` (the sketch didn't say; Q5); embedded structs follow `typeFields`' tie-breaks (not a plain flatten); `TextMarshaler` → `string`, and pointer-receiver marshalers → `unknown`.
- `set:json` gets types too (Q3), and its missing error position is fixed.

## 5d. Tailwind CSS
**Status: Implemented** (2026-10-26; implementation notes, deviations and measurements at the end of this section). User answers: (Q1) local CLI, then `bun x --package @tailwindcss/cli@4.3.3`, else an error with install lines; (Q2) Tailwind at-rules in component `<style>` blocks are processed; **(Q3) Orbit inlines the CSS a Tailwind stylesheet imports and rewrites their `url()`s itself, matching `@tailwindcss/vite` (user decision, not the recommendation)**; (Q4) one-shot runs per change with the token check; (Q5) the VS Code extension sets `tailwindCSS.includeLanguages` for `orbit` by default; (Q6) no Tailwind in `orbit new`, a "Using Tailwind" docs page. Spec written User decision the same day, prompted by porting `nyth` (an Astro site on Tailwind v4 through `@tailwindcss/vite`): Orbit supports Tailwind CSS out of the box through Tailwind's **standalone CLI**, a native binary that needs no Node, used as an **external, optional tool** and turned on by `@import "tailwindcss"` in a stylesheet. Nothing changes for projects that don't use Tailwind. The measurements below come from a throwaway spike in `scratchpad/tailwind-spike/` (Ryzen 7 9700X, 16 threads, Go 1.27.3, Tailwind 4.3.3; the machine was shared, so each number gives its load average).

**In short**: a CSS file (or a `<style>` block) that uses Tailwind is a **Tailwind input**. Before esbuild loads it, Orbit pipes it through `tailwindcss --optimize` (run in the project root, so Tailwind's automatic source detection scans the project, `.orbit` files included, and honors `.gitignore`). esbuild then bundles, minifies and hashes the result like any other CSS, and it goes into the manifest the usual way. Scoped blocks are scoped first and run through Tailwind after, as in Astro. In `orbit dev`, the Tailwind run starts as soon as the change batch starts, **in parallel with regenerate and `go build`**, so a template edit that adds a class gets new CSS without waiting longer (measured +8 ms on 16 threads, +15 ms on 4 cores, from CPU contention). A CSS- or script-only edit pays the run on its critical path (~18 → ~95 ms). There's no runtime cost: the output is a static stylesheet, built ahead of time ("pay at build time", CLAUDE.md). There's no new Go dependency and no `orbit.json` key.

### End-to-end example
`src/styles/global.css` (the project's one Tailwind stylesheet, Tailwind v4's CSS-first config):

```css
@import "tailwindcss";

@theme {
  --color-ember: #d1703f;
  --font-ui: "Figtree Variable", system-ui, sans-serif;
}

@utility card-row {
  @apply flex items-baseline gap-2 border-b border-zinc-700 py-4;
}
```

`src/layouts/Base.orbit` includes it, the way an Astro layout imports `../styles/global.css` (an Orbit frontmatter is Go, so the import goes in a style block):

```orbit
---
type Props struct{ Title string }
---
<html lang="en">
  <head><title>{props.Title}</title></head>
  <body class="bg-zinc-900 font-ui text-zinc-100"><slot /></body>
</html>

<style is:global>
  @import "../styles/global.css";
</style>
```

`src/components/Card.orbit` uses utilities in the template, including `class:list` and Go strings, plus a scoped style that uses `@apply` through `@reference`:

```orbit
---
type Props struct {
	Title  string
	Active bool
}
size := "p-8"
if !props.Active {
	size = "p-2"
}
---
<article class="card-row" class:list={"rounded-lg", size, "ring-2 ring-ember": props.Active}>
  <h2 class="title">{props.Title}</h2>
</article>

<style>
  @reference "../styles/global.css";
  .title { @apply text-lg font-bold hover:text-ember; }
</style>
```

What happens:

| Step | What Orbit does | Result (spike) |
|---|---|---|
| `orbit build`, `orbit generate` | esbuild reaches `global.css` through the layout's `@import`. Its first line is `@import "tailwindcss"`, so Orbit runs `tailwindcss --optimize -i <abs>/src/styles/global.css` with the project root as the working directory, then hands the output to esbuild as that file's content. | Tailwind found `card-row`, `rounded-lg`, `p-8`, `p-2`, `ring-2`, `ring-ember`, `bg-zinc-900`, … in the `.orbit` files, **including the Go string literals and the `class:list` elements**. `orbit_gen/`, `.orbit/` and `dist/` were skipped (gitignored). |
| the same, `Card.orbit`'s `<style>` | Orbit scopes the block as today (`.title` → `.title[data-o-…]`; Tailwind's at-rules are left alone, verified with `compiler.ScopeCSS`), then pipes it to `tailwindcss --optimize -i -` with `Card.orbit`'s directory as the working directory, so `@reference "../styles/global.css"` resolves. | `.title[data-o-…] { font-size: var(--text-lg, 1.125rem); …; font-weight: var(--font-weight-bold, 700) }` and `@media (hover: hover) { .title[data-o-…]:hover { color: var(--color-ember, #d1703f) } }`: scoped, as in Astro, plus Tailwind's `@property --tw-font-weight` fallback for the applied utility. 0.06 s per block. (This example was run as written in the spike.) |
| esbuild | bundles, minifies (prod) and hashes both into the page's stylesheet, in import-graph order; `url()`s in `global.css` resolve from `src/styles/`. | nyth's real stylesheet: Tailwind gives 90 KB; esbuild minifies it to 72,465 B (13.7 KB gzipped) in 4 ms with no warnings, the same size as Tailwind's own `--minify` (72,431 B). |
| `orbit dev`, edit Card's template to add `underline` | the batch starts the Tailwind run at once, next to regenerate and `go build`; Tailwind's output changed, so the incremental esbuild rebuild runs after it and rewrites the dev manifest; the reload waits for both. | 200-file project: template edit 177 → 185 ms on 16 threads (load 0.9–3.0); Tailwind took 75–85 ms of that, in parallel. |
| `orbit dev`, edit `@theme` in `global.css` | Tailwind, then esbuild; no Go work. | ~18 → ~95 ms (Tailwind 70–75 ms, then esbuild 18 ms). |
| no Tailwind CLI installed | an error at the root: `src/styles/global.css:1:1: @import "tailwindcss" needs the Tailwind CSS CLI (v4): …install line…; see orbit doctor`. | Today the same project fails with esbuild's `src/layouts/Base.orbit:10:26: Could not resolve "tailwindcss"`. |

### The engine and how Orbit finds it (Q1)
Research on 2026-10-26 (GitHub releases and the npm registry):
- **Tailwind CSS v4.3.3**, released 2026-07-16 (npm `latest`; 4.3.2 2026-06-29, 4.3.1 2026-06-12, 4.3.0 2026-05-08). Pin: **4.3.3** (over three months old, ≥ two weeks per CLAUDE.md).
- **Standalone CLI**: one binary per platform on the GitHub release (`tailwindcss-linux-x64` 112 MB, `-arm64`, `-musl` variants, `macos-arm64`/`x64`, `windows-x64.exe`), with a `sha256sums.txt`. It's a Bun-compiled executable with Tailwind, Lightning CSS and the Oxide scanner inside. Homebrew's `tailwindcss` formula (4.3.3) is the same CLI. Spike: start-up ~45 ms; 84–124 MB RSS per run.
- **`@tailwindcss/cli`** 4.3.3 on npm: the same CLI as a **JavaScript** package (`bin` `tailwindcss` → `dist/index.mjs`, `#!/usr/bin/env node`) over the native `@tailwindcss/oxide` addon. It needs Node or Bun to run. Measured on nyth's stylesheet: under Bun 0.08 s, under Node 25 0.11 s, standalone 0.09–0.11 s (load 7.8).
- `tailwindcss` itself (npm) has no CLI in v4. `@tailwindcss/standalone` on npm is a stale 4.0 alpha; not used.

**Version requirement: v4 only** (decided by rule, Astro: Astro's Tailwind integration is `@tailwindcss/vite`, and the v3 one, `@astrojs/tailwind`, is deprecated). A v3 CLI is refused with a message (its CSS directives and flags differ). A v3-style JavaScript config still works through v4's `@config "./tailwind.config.js"` (verified in the spike). The version is read once per process from `tailwindcss --help`'s first line (`≈ tailwindcss v4.3.3`, ~60 ms; there's no `--version`).

**Lookup order** (like §9b's, minus the editor setting; it runs only when the project has a Tailwind input):
1. The project's **`node_modules/.bin/tailwindcss`** from `@tailwindcss/cli` at v4: run directly when `node` is on PATH, else as `bun <path>`, else this step is skipped. A project that pins Tailwind in `package.json` gets that version (and it's the case where `node_modules` exists anyway, for JavaScript plugins, below).
2. **`tailwindcss` on PATH**: the standalone binary (GitHub release, Homebrew, or a `bin/` directory the user adds to PATH), or a global `@tailwindcss/cli`.
3. **Bun present**: `bun x --package @tailwindcss/cli@4.3.3 tailwindcss …`, pinned to Orbit's version (the §9b pattern; the first run downloads into Bun's cache).
4. Not found: the error above, only when a stylesheet is a Tailwind input. The fix line names the release download for the current `GOOS`/`GOARCH` (`curl -sLo ~/.local/bin/tailwindcss https://github.com/tailwindlabs/tailwindcss/releases/download/v4.3.3/tailwindcss-linux-x64 && chmod +x ~/.local/bin/tailwindcss`), `brew install tailwindcss`, and `bun add -d @tailwindcss/cli@4.3.3`.

The **(Q1)** choice is how a user with nothing installed gets the CLI, including in Docker and CI (the scaffolded Dockerfile's `golang:` build stage has none):
- (a) **Steps 1–4** (recommended): no download code in Orbit; "Using Tailwind" shows the install lines and a Dockerfile line, `ADD --checksum=sha256:dc61b3ac… https://github.com/tailwindlabs/tailwindcss/releases/download/v4.3.3/tailwindcss-linux-x64 /usr/local/bin/tailwindcss` plus `RUN chmod +x …` (Docker verifies the hash).
- (b) (a) without the `bun x` step: simpler, but a Bun user still has to install something.
- (c) (a) plus **Orbit downloading the pinned standalone binary itself** into `os.UserCacheDir()/orbit/tailwindcss/4.3.3/`, verified against sha256 values from the release's `sha256sums.txt` pinned in Orbit's source, with stdlib `net/http` only. Zero-install everywhere, Docker included, but `orbit build` would do network I/O, the download is **112 MB**, and Orbit would own a pinned hash per platform (7 binaries) and cache cleanup.

There's no `orbit.json` key and no environment variable for the path (decided by rule, simplest: PATH already covers a project-local binary).

### What gets processed (Q2)
A **Tailwind input** is one of:
- a **`.css` file** that esbuild loads (from a `<style>`'s `@import`, a script's `import "./x.css"`, or another CSS file's `@import`) whose text has an `@import` of `tailwindcss` or `tailwindcss/…` (`@import "tailwindcss" source("…")`, `@import "tailwindcss/theme.css" layer(theme)`, …) or a `@tailwind` directive. That's a **root**: it emits utilities, so it depends on the project's sources.
- a **`<style>` block** (scoped or `is:global`) whose content has such an `@import`, or uses a Tailwind at-rule: `@apply`, `@reference`, `@theme`, `@utility`, `@variant`, `@custom-variant`, `@source`, `@plugin`, `@config`. Without `@import "tailwindcss"` it's a **block input** (typically `@reference` + `@apply`); its output doesn't depend on the scanned sources.

Detection runs Orbit's CSS tokenizer over the text (comments and strings don't count); it's a few µs per file and runs for every project, so non-Tailwind projects pay nothing measurable. CSS files that a root `@import`s are inlined by Tailwind and never reach esbuild on their own. A plain CSS file is untouched.

Rules:
- **Where it runs**: inside esbuild's load step (the `file`-namespace `OnLoad` for `.css`, and the existing `.orbit` block loader), before esbuild parses the CSS, so esbuild bundles, minifies, hashes, resolves `url()` and writes the manifest as for any CSS (§5). The Tailwind output is cached per input in the bundler (key: the input text; for roots also a generation counter bumped by the dev loop, below).
- **Command**: `tailwindcss --optimize -i <input> -o -` with a cleaned environment (`NO_COLOR`), stdout read in full:
  - (As implemented, every input runs as `-i -` with the text on stdin, after Orbit's inlining (Q3), working directory = the input's directory, plus the `source(…)` below; see Deviations.)
  - **Root files**: `-i <absolute path>`, working directory = **project root**. Tailwind's automatic source detection uses the working directory as its base (verified: run from `/tmp`, it scanned all of `/tmp`), so it scans the whole project, as `@tailwindcss/vite` scans Vite's root in Astro (decided by rule, Astro). The input's own `@import`s resolve from its directory.
  - **Blocks**: the scoped text on stdin (`-i -`), working directory = **the `.orbit` file's directory**, so relative `@reference`/`@import` resolve as written. When the block has `@import "tailwindcss"` with no `source(…)`, Orbit adds `source("<path to the project root>")` to that import, because a stdin input's detection base is its working directory (verified: `source("../..")` from `src/components` scanned the project and honored `.gitignore`).
  - **`--optimize` always**, in dev too: Tailwind's raw output uses CSS nesting (`&:hover { @media (hover: hover) { … } }`), which Tailwind's own browser targets (Safari 16.4, Chrome 111) don't support; `--optimize` runs Lightning CSS, which flattens it. `@tailwindcss/vite` optimizes in builds; doing it in dev too keeps dev and prod CSS the same (decided by rule, simplest; measured at no extra cost, 0.09 s with or without). Tailwind's `--minify` isn't used: esbuild minifies prod bundles (§5), to the same size.
  - Several inputs run in parallel, at most `GOMAXPROCS` processes at once.
- **Scoping order**: a scoped block is scoped first (today's `compiler.ScopeCSS`, unchanged) and run through Tailwind after, which is Astro's order (its compiler scopes the `<style>` before Vite's CSS plugins run). Consequences, all as in Astro:
  - `@apply` inside a scoped rule applies to the scoped selector, variants included (`.title[data-o-…]:hover`).
  - `@theme`, `@utility`, `@custom-variant`, `@plugin`, `@source` bodies aren't scoped: the scoper already leaves unknown at-rules alone (verified).
  - **Utilities are never scoped**: they're generated by Tailwind after scoping. A scoped block that `@import`s `tailwindcss` therefore emits global utilities next to its scoped rules. Docs recommend one global stylesheet (Astro's guidance) and `@reference` for `@apply` in components.
  - Tailwind puts utilities in `@layer utilities`, while component styles are unlayered, so a scoped rule beats a utility on the same property whatever the specificity (CSS cascade layers; the same in Astro with Tailwind v4). Docs say so.
- **Order in the page stylesheet**: unchanged (§5: import-graph order). The root's output sits where its `@import` is, typically the layout's `<style is:global>`, before component styles.
- **Several roots**: allowed, each run separately (Tailwind's behavior); docs recommend one.
- **Block inputs repeat Tailwind's fallbacks**: each block that `@apply`s utilities gets its own `@layer properties` / `@property --tw-*` declarations for them (seen in the spike), so they repeat across blocks, as under Astro; harmless, and another reason the docs prefer `@utility` in the global stylesheet over many `@apply` blocks.
- **`url()` in files a root `@import`s (Q3, user decision: Orbit rewrites them)**: Tailwind's CLI inlines local and npm `@import`s itself and, unlike `@tailwindcss/vite` and the PostCSS plugin (`@tailwindcss/node`'s `shouldRewriteUrls`, which the CLI doesn't set), **doesn't rebase their `url()`s** (verified: `url("./x.woff2")` in `fonts/fonts.css` stays `./x.woff2` in the output). So **Orbit inlines a Tailwind input's CSS imports itself before running Tailwind** (`internal/tailwind/inline.go`, over tdewolff's CSS tokenizer), matching `@tailwindcss/vite`:
  - each top-level `@import` that esbuild's resolver (`PluginBuild.Resolve`, kind `ResolveCSSImportRule`, from the importing file's directory) resolves to a CSS file is replaced by that file's text, recursively: relative paths and bare npm packages (`@fontsource-variable/figtree` → its `package.json` `main`/`exports`/`style`, `pkg/dist/x.css`), as esbuild bundles CSS imports;
  - its conditions are kept as blocks, in import order: `@media <media> { @supports (<cond>) { @layer <name> { … } } }` (`layer` alone is an anonymous layer); a nested `@charset` is dropped; an import cycle is dropped (as browsers and esbuild do);
  - in an inlined file whose directory isn't the input's, every relative reference is rewritten to be relative to the input's directory: `url(…)` (quoted or not, with `?query`/`#hash` suffixes kept), `@source "…"` (also after `not`), and `./`/`../` paths of `@plugin`, `@config`, `@reference` and of the `@import`s left in place (a bare `@plugin "daisyui"` is a package and stays). esbuild then resolves the rewritten `url()`s from the input's directory and hashes the files into `/_orbit/` (a Fontsource `.woff2` becomes `/_orbit/figtree-latin-wght-normal-HASH.woff2`, tested end to end in `internal/build` and `internal/assets`). Relative paths, not absolute ones, so the output stays the same in a copy of the project;
  - left as written: `url(data:…)`, URLs with a scheme, protocol-relative and root-relative ones (`/x.png`, from `public/`), `#fragment`s; and left to Tailwind: imports of `tailwindcss`/`tailwindcss/…`, remote imports, imports esbuild can't resolve (Tailwind reports them), and imports with conditions only Tailwind knows (`theme(…)`, `source(…)`, `prefix(…)`).
  - Errors map back through the inlining: the inliner records, for each piece of its output, the file and offset it came from, so Tailwind's `stdin.css:L:C` lands on the right file and line.
- **Errors**: Tailwind exits 1 with a boxed, always-colored message on stderr. Orbit strips the ANSI codes and box characters and reports it at a `.orbit`/`.css` position (Go convention, `file:line:col`): Tailwind's own `file:line:col` when it gives one (CSS syntax errors), otherwise for "Cannot apply unknown utility class `x`" the first `@apply` that names `x` in that input, otherwise the input's start (the block's line:col). `orbit build`, `orbit generate` and `orbit check` fail; `orbit dev` shows the overlay, as for any CSS error.
- **Legal comment**: Tailwind's `/*! tailwindcss v4.3.3 | MIT License … */` banner stays, where esbuild's default puts legal comments (decided by rule, esbuild default).

### Source detection
Tailwind v4 scans the project without configuration. Verified with the 4.3.3 CLI on a small project and on `bench/devloop`'s 200-file one:
- **`.orbit` files are scanned by default**, and classes are found in plain attributes, in `class:list={"rounded-lg", cls, "ring-2": props.Active, "bg-red-500": !props.Active}` (every literal element), and in Go string literals in the frontmatter or in `{…}` (`cls := "text-ember"`, `map[bool]string{true: "p-8", false: "p-2"}`, `class={"font-bold " + big}`). `.go` files elsewhere in the module (`internal/ui/c.go`) and `.md` files are scanned too. Classes built at runtime (`fmt.Sprintf("w-%d", n)`) aren't, as Tailwind documents for every framework: write complete class names.
- **`.gitignore` is honored even without a Git repository** (verified after removing `.git`), so the starter's `.gitignore` (`orbit_gen/`, `.orbit/`, `dist/`, `node_modules/`, §6 `orbit new`) keeps generated code and build output out of the scan. Without those lines the 200-file project's run went from 0.07 to 0.12 s (load 6.9): generated code repeats every class, so the CSS is the same, only slower. `orbit doctor` warns (below); Orbit doesn't add `@source not` lines itself (decided by rule, simplest).
- `node_modules`, binary files, CSS files and lock files are skipped by Tailwind itself.
- Anything else goes through Tailwind's own directives in the stylesheet: `@source "../../packages/ui"` for classes outside the project, `@source not "../internal/api"` to skip a large Go tree, `@import "tailwindcss" source(none)` for an explicit list, `@source inline("…")` for safelisting.

### Dev loop (Q4)
Today a template edit **skips esbuild** (§7.1). With Tailwind, an edit that adds a class needs new CSS. Options, measured on `bench/devloop`'s 200-file project with 12 Tailwind utilities added to every `.orbit` file and a `global.css` root (scans 203 files; the CSS has 1,336 distinct whitespace tokens to choose from), with `-dev` (`internal/dev`'s pipeline) and a Tailwind run started next to `Pipeline.Run`:
- (a) **A long-running `tailwindcss --watch` child per root**, Orbit reading its output file. Rebuilds are fast once warm: **5.6–10 ms** from the file write to the new output for a new class; for an edit with no new class Tailwind doesn't rewrite the file and prints `Done in 300–500µs` (load 1.5). But Orbit has no reliable way to know when the CSS for a batch is ready: Tailwind watches files on its own, debounces on its own, writes the output in place (a reader can see a half-written file), and its only signal is the human-readable `Done in …` line on stderr, which `--silent` turns off and which can change between versions. A reload sent too early shows stale CSS. One process per root also stays resident (84–124 MB each), and block inputs would each need their own.
- (b) **Orbit runs the CLI once per relevant batch** (recommended, with the gate below). A run costs **0.06 s** on a small project, **0.07–0.10 s** on the 200-file project and **0.10–0.11 s** on nyth's 181 `.astro` files with its 1,230-line `global.css` (loads 1.6–2.2); ~45 ms of each is the binary's start-up. Started at the beginning of the batch, it runs **in parallel** with regenerate, the type check and `go build`, which take ~170 ms for a template edit, so it's off the critical path:

  | Edit (200 files, `-dev`, 7 runs, median) | today | with Tailwind in parallel |
  |---|---:|---:|
  | template body, 16 threads (load 1.0 → 0.9–3.0) | 177 ms | 185 ms (Tailwind 75–85 ms, hidden) |
  | frontmatter, 16 threads | 168 ms | 173 ms |
  | template body, 4 cores (`GOMAXPROCS=4 taskset -c 0-3`, load 3.3–3.4) | 169 ms | 184 ms (Tailwind 69–85 ms) |
  | `.go` body edit (every `Props` depends on it), 16 threads | 561 ms | 573 ms |
  | scoped `<style>` / `<script>` content, 16 threads | 18 ms | ~95 ms: Tailwind 70–75 ms, then esbuild ~20 ms (the spike measured Tailwind's 70–75 ms next to the pipeline's 20–23 ms; the real order is sequential) |

  The extra 8–15 ms on template edits is CPU contention between Tailwind and `go build`, not waiting.
- (c) **Orbit reruns only when the "class candidate set" changes**: keep the set of whitespace-separated tokens of every file Tailwind could scan, and run Tailwind only when a batch brings a token that wasn't in the set at the last run. Tailwind candidates never contain whitespace (arbitrary values use `_`), so a new candidate implies a new token. Cost: **1.1 ms** for all 203 files of the 200-file project (1,336 tokens), **4.5 ms** for nyth's 181 files (10,972 tokens), 17–27 µs per changed file. On its own it doesn't help much: most template edits add some new word (text, an identifier), so they'd still run Tailwind; but they run it in parallel anyway. It does skip the run for edits that only remove, move or repeat tokens.

**Recommended: (b) with (c) as a gate.** In detail:
- At the start of each batch, Orbit decides which Tailwind inputs to rerun, then starts them at once, in parallel with the rest of the pipeline:
  - **any `.css` file changed**, or `tsconfig.json`/`package.json` (plugins): every input (roots and blocks; Orbit doesn't track Tailwind's import graph, and a `@theme` change affects `@reference` blocks);
  - **a block input's text changed**: that block;
  - **any other changed file** in the batch (the §7 watched set, plus `orbit.json` `"watch"` files, which is how a `@source` directory outside the module gets watched): the roots, if the gate sees a new token.
- When an output differs from the cached one, the incremental esbuild rebuild runs after it (today's asset step, which a template edit otherwise skips) and rewrites `.orbit/dev/manifest.json`. The reload waits for both the Go side and the asset side. When no output changed, esbuild stays skipped.
- Like Tailwind's own watch mode, a dev stylesheet only grows between full runs: removing the last use of a class doesn't rerun Tailwind (no new token), so the class stays until the next run. Production builds always run from scratch.
- A failed run keeps the last good CSS and shows the overlay; the next batch reruns.
- The CLI lookup (Q1) and its version check happen once per `orbit dev` process (~60 ms, during the first build, in parallel with it).

### Production build
- `orbit build` and `orbit generate` run every Tailwind input once, from scratch, before esbuild bundles (both bundle; §5, §6 step 3). The bundle step already runs while `go list -export` preloads, so part of Tailwind's ~0.1 s overlaps. Estimate for the 200-file project: **≤ +0.1 s** on a ~0.31–0.33 s incremental `orbit build` (load 11, noisy), less where the preload hides it; nothing on a project without Tailwind.
- **Determinism**: Tailwind's output was byte-identical across 5 runs of nyth's stylesheet, across 3 runs on the 200-file project, and in a copy of the project at another path (no absolute paths in the output). esbuild's hashing is already deterministic, so the same sources give the same hashed URLs and manifest, and `orbit_gen/` stays byte-identical between `generate` and `build`.
- **`orbit check`** runs generate, so it reports Tailwind errors too (an unknown `@apply` class is a check failure), and needs the CLI when the project uses Tailwind.
- Source maps: prod's optional external maps (`"sourcemap": true`) and dev's linked maps cover Tailwind inputs as esbuild sees them, i.e. Tailwind's output, not the `.orbit`/`.css` lines (out of scope, below).

### Configuration and plugins
- **No `orbit.json` key.** Tailwind v4 is configured in CSS (`@theme`, `@utility`, `@custom-variant`, `@plugin`, `@source`), and Orbit only needs to find the CLI.
- **First-party plugins are built into the standalone CLI** (verified with 4.3.3): `@plugin "@tailwindcss/typography"`, `@plugin "@tailwindcss/forms"` and `@plugin "@tailwindcss/aspect-ratio"` work with no `node_modules`. Container queries are in Tailwind v4's core (`@tailwindcss/container-queries` doesn't resolve and isn't needed).
- **Local JavaScript plugins and configs work** with the standalone CLI (Bun inside runs them): `@plugin "./plug.mjs"`, `@config "./tailwind.config.js"` (verified).
- **Third-party plugins from npm** (`@plugin "daisyui"`) need the package in `node_modules`, installed by any package manager; the **standalone CLI then loads it**, with no Node at build time (verified with `daisyui@5.7.47` installed by `bun add`). Without `node_modules`, Tailwind's error (`Can't resolve 'daisyui'`) is shown at the root. Docs: `bun add -d daisyui` (or npm), and the Dockerfile copies `package.json` and runs the install in the build stage.

### Editors (Q5)
- **Tailwind CSS IntelliSense** (`bradlc.vscode-tailwindcss` 0.16.0, 2026-07-20, on the Marketplace and Open VSX) works on `.orbit` files with `"tailwindCSS.includeLanguages": { "orbit": "html" }`. Its default `tailwindCSS.classAttributes` already has `class` and `class:list`; completion inside `class:list={…}`'s Go string literals is to be checked when implemented (they look like JS strings to its matcher). It finds the v4 stylesheet by itself; that it works with no `tailwindcss` package in `node_modules` (its server ships a bundled Tailwind for that case) is to be confirmed when implemented. For `.css` files, Tailwind's docs recommend `"files.associations": { "*.css": "tailwindcss" }` so VS Code's CSS validator stops flagging `@theme`/`@apply`; Orbit's docs repeat it.
- **Orbit's extension contributes the mapping by default** (`contributes.configurationDefaults`, which VS Code allows for another extension's setting) (recommended): zero config for Orbit users, no effect without the Tailwind extension. A user's own `tailwindCSS.includeLanguages` replaces the default object, so docs say to include `"orbit": "html"` when setting it. Alternative: docs only.
- **No conflict with Orbit's LSP**: two servers on one document is normal in VS Code; Orbit offers nothing inside `class` attribute values, the Tailwind server offers nothing in Go regions, and hovers from both are shown together. Neovim: `tailwindcss` language server with `filetypes` including `orbit` and `settings.tailwindCSS.includeLanguages = { orbit = "html" }`.
- `orbit fmt` is unaffected: `bun fmt` formats Tailwind's at-rules (nyth's `global.css` formatted, then compiled, gave byte-identical Tailwind output).
- Orbit's own LSP doesn't learn Tailwind (no class completion or validation of its own): out of scope.

### `orbit new`, docs and Astro migration (Q6)
- **`orbit new` doesn't scaffold Tailwind** (recommended): the starter stays dependency-free, and adding Tailwind is two files (above). A new docs page, **"Using Tailwind"**, gives the install lines, `global.css`, the layout's `<style is:global>@import …`, `@reference` for `@apply`, plugins, the editor settings, the Dockerfile line, and the dev-loop behavior. Alternative: `orbit new --tailwind` (writes `src/styles/global.css` and the layout's import; the Dockerfile line too), easy to add later.
- **Migrating from Astro** (`coming-from-astro.md`): drop `@tailwindcss/vite` from `astro.config` and `package.json` (Orbit needs nothing in their place but the CLI); keep `global.css` as is; move `import "../styles/global.css"` from the layout's frontmatter to `<style is:global>@import "../styles/global.css";</style>`; Fontsource and other `url()`-bearing CSS can stay imported from `global.css`, since Orbit rewrites their `url()`s (Q3); `class:list` works the same; `@reference` in component styles works the same.

### `orbit doctor`
A new **Tailwind** check, after TypeScript:
- inside a project with a Tailwind input: ✓ `tailwindcss 4.3.3 (path)` (or "through `bun x`"); **✗** when no v4 CLI is found ("needed by `src/styles/global.css`", with the fix line), since `orbit build` fails without it; ⚠ when `.gitignore` doesn't ignore `orbit_gen/`, `.orbit/` and `dist/` (slower scans).
- inside a project without Tailwind, and outside a project: ✓ with the version when found, else ✓ "not installed (needed only by projects that use Tailwind)".

### (Q) points
1. **(Q1) Install story**: (a) found locally (`node_modules/.bin/tailwindcss` run with Node or Bun, `tailwindcss` on PATH), then `bun x --package @tailwindcss/cli@4.3.3`, else an error with install lines and a Dockerfile `ADD --checksum` line in the docs (recommended), vs. (b) (a) without `bun x`, vs. (c) (a) plus Orbit downloading the pinned 112 MB standalone binary into its cache, hash-checked (zero-install, Docker included, but network I/O in `orbit build` and a pinned hash per platform).
2. **(Q2) `<style>` blocks**: blocks with Tailwind at-rules are Tailwind inputs, scoped first and then run through Tailwind, one process each (0.06 s, parallel, cached by text) (recommended: Astro supports `@apply` + `@reference` in component styles, and the scoper already handles Tailwind's at-rules), vs. only `.css` files are processed and a block using a Tailwind at-rule is an error pointing to a `.css` file (simpler; no per-block processes).
3. **(Q3) `url()` in files a Tailwind root `@import`s**: Tailwind CLI behavior (not rebased) with a hint in esbuild's resolve error, and docs saying to import such CSS from a `<style is:global>` or a `<script>` (recommended for the first release), vs. Orbit inlining the root's relative local `@import`s itself before Tailwind and rebasing their `url()`s, as `@tailwindcss/vite` does (~150 lines over the existing tokenizer; npm imports still not rebased), vs. mapping each `url()` back through Tailwind's `--map` source map (exact, more code).
4. **(Q4) Dev loop**: (b) one run per relevant batch, started in parallel with regenerate and `go build`, gated by the token set (c) (recommended: template edit +8 ms on 16 threads, +15 ms on 4 cores; CSS/script-only edits ~18 → ~95 ms), vs. (a) `--watch` children (5.6–10 ms per rebuild, but readiness depends on parsing stderr text, and a resident process per input), vs. (b) without the gate (slightly simpler, a run on every batch).
5. **(Q5) VS Code**: Orbit's extension contributes `tailwindCSS.includeLanguages: {"orbit": "html"}` through `configurationDefaults` (recommended) vs. docs only.
6. **(Q6) Starter**: no Tailwind in `orbit new`, a "Using Tailwind" page (recommended) vs. an `orbit new --tailwind` flag now.

Decided by rule (to log in `docs/DECISIONS.md`):
- v4 only; v3 CLIs refused; `@config` works through v4 (Astro: `@tailwindcss/vite`).
- Detection base = project root for root files (Astro: Vite's root).
- Scope first, then Tailwind (Astro's order).
- `--optimize` in dev and prod (simplest: same CSS in both; nesting lowered for Tailwind's targets).
- esbuild minifies, not Tailwind (simplest: one minifier, same size).
- No `orbit.json` key and no path variable (simplest).
- `.gitignore` relied on for exclusions, doctor warns (simplest).
- Errors at `file:line:col`, ANSI stripped (Go convention).
- Tailwind's license banner kept where esbuild puts legal comments (esbuild default).
- Several roots allowed (Tailwind behavior).
- A `"watch"` file change also reruns Tailwind (simplest way to cover `@source` outside the module).
- `orbit check` reports Tailwind errors (it runs generate).

### Cost
- **Runtime**: none. The output is a static, hashed stylesheet like any other; the SSR hot path is untouched.
- **Projects without Tailwind**: a token scan of each CSS input for the markers, µs per file; no process is started and no lookup happens.
- **`orbit build`/`generate`/`check`**: one Tailwind run per input, in parallel: ~0.1 s for a root (0.07–0.10 s at 200 files, 0.10–0.11 s for nyth), 0.06 s per block input; partly overlapped by the `go list -export` preload. Estimated ≤ +0.1 s on a ~0.3 s incremental build at 200 files.
- **`orbit dev`**: template and frontmatter edits +5–8 ms on 16 threads, +15 ms on 4 cores (contention; Tailwind runs in parallel); CSS and script edits ~18 → ~95 ms; edits that bring no new token: nothing. The first build adds the version probe (~60 ms) and one run, in parallel with the first `go build`.
- **Memory**: 84–124 MB RSS per Tailwind run, for its ~0.1 s; nothing resident.
- **Install**: optional; the standalone binary is 112 MB.
- **Code**: about 600–900 lines: `internal/tailwind` (lookup, version, run, error cleanup, the token gate), hooks in `internal/assets` (the CSS `OnLoad`, block inputs), `internal/dev` (rerun decision, parallel start, the esbuild-after-Tailwind step), `internal/doctor`; the VS Code `configurationDefaults` entry; docs. No new Go dependency (`os/exec`).

### Tests (what must exist)
- **Detection** (table-driven): `@import "tailwindcss"`, `'tailwindcss'`, `url(tailwindcss)`, `tailwindcss/theme.css` with `layer(…)`, `source(…)`; `@tailwind utilities`; each Tailwind at-rule in a block; not inside comments or strings; a plain CSS file stays plain.
- **Lookup**: a fake `tailwindcss` script on a temp PATH (prints a v4 banner; v3 banner refused; missing → the error with the install lines); `node_modules/.bin` preferred over PATH; the `bun x` command line; the not-found error only when an input exists.
- **Pipeline with a fake CLI** (always runs, no real Tailwind): the arguments, working directory and stdin per input kind; the `source(…)` injection for blocks; output fed to esbuild (bundled, minified, hashed, in the manifest, in import-graph order); scoped-then-Tailwind order; error cleanup and positions (ANSI, box characters, Tailwind's own `file:line:col`, the `@apply` search, the block start); the `url()` hint.
- **Real CLI** (skipped with a notice when no v4 CLI is found, like the loader's `bun test`): a small project like the example, asserting the classes found (`.orbit` attributes, `class:list`, Go strings, `.go`, `.md`), `orbit_gen/`/`.orbit/`/`dist/` excluded, `@reference` + `@apply` scoped, a first-party plugin, determinism (two builds, identical `dist/`).
- **Dev** (`internal/dev`, fake CLI): a template edit with a new token reruns the root in parallel and rebuilds esbuild once its output changed; an edit with no new token doesn't run it; a `.css` change reruns every input; a block edit reruns only that block; the reload waits for the asset side; a failed run keeps the last CSS and shows the overlay.
- **`bench/devloop`**: a `-tailwind` flag adding the classes and `global.css` (as in the spike) and reporting the template- and CSS-edit costs, run when implemented; results in `docs/performance.md`.
- **`orbit doctor`**: the Tailwind check's ✓/✗/⚠ cases.
- **VS Code**: the `configurationDefaults` entry in `package.json` (a manifest test).

### User-docs changes (with the implementation)
- New **`docs/tailwind.md`** ("Using Tailwind"), linked from `styles-and-scripts.md`, `getting-started.md`'s next steps and the docs index.
- `styles-and-scripts.md`: Tailwind inputs, scoped blocks with `@reference`/`@apply`, unscoped utilities, layers vs. scoped rules, "Styles and scripts in `orbit dev`" (Tailwind reruns).
- `dev-server.md`: what a change reruns with Tailwind, and the costs.
- `configuration.md`: no Tailwind key; `"watch"` for `@source` directories outside the module.
- `reference/cli.md`: `orbit build`/`generate`/`check` run Tailwind; the doctor check.
- `editor-setup.md`: Tailwind IntelliSense (the default Orbit contributes, `files.associations`), Neovim.
- `coming-from-astro.md`: the migration notes above; `deployment.md`: the Dockerfile `ADD --checksum` line and npm plugins in the build stage; `troubleshooting.md`: CLI not found, v3 found, a class missing (dynamic names, `@source`), `url()` in imported files; `performance.md`: dev and build costs.

### Out of scope
- Tailwind v3 (`tailwind.config.js` as the entry, `@tailwind base`), PostCSS and other CSS processors (Sass, Less, PostCSS plugins).
- Source maps through Tailwind (dev and `"sourcemap": true` maps show Tailwind's output for Tailwind inputs).
- Orbit downloading the CLI (Q1 c) and `orbit new --tailwind` (Q6), unless chosen.
- Class completion, validation or sorting in Orbit's own LSP or `orbit fmt` (Tailwind's IntelliSense and Prettier plugin cover these where the user wants them).
- Removing classes from the dev stylesheet before the next full run.

### Implementation notes
