- **`internal/tailwind`** (no new dependency; `os/exec` and tdewolff's CSS lexer): `detect.go` (`Detect`: a quick text check, then the tokenizer; `Info{Input, Root, Pos}`), `inline.go` (Q3, above; `Inlined.Position` maps output offsets back to files), `find.go` (`Find` with an injectable `Env`, `Install` lines per `GOOS`/`GOARCH`, `ParseVersion` of `--help`'s banner), `run.go` (`Processor`: the output cache keyed by working directory + text, two generations (`cssGen`: every input stale; `srcGen`: root inputs stale), in-flight runs shared, at most `GOMAXPROCS` processes, errors cleaned up and placed), `gate.go` (the token gate), `tailwindtest/` (a fake shell-script CLI for plumbing tests, and the real-CLI lookup: `ORBIT_TEST_TAILWIND` or `tailwindcss` on `PATH`, skip with a notice).
- **`internal/assets`**: a file-namespace `OnLoad` for `\.css$` that returns nothing (esbuild's own loader) unless `Detect` says Tailwind input; style blocks are detected in `plan.style` after scoping and run in the `.orbit` loader; `Options.Tailwind` lets `orbit dev` share its processor; `Bundle` calls `Processor.Begin`/`Sweep` around each rebuild so the cache holds only current inputs. esbuild calls the load step again on every incremental rebuild, so cached outputs reach it without a context change. Errors in a scoped block are moved from the scoped text back to the text as written (`blockPosition`).
- **`internal/dev`** (`tailwind.go`): at the start of `pipeline.run`, a batch with a `.css`/`tsconfig.json`/`package.json` change bumps `cssGen` (the CSS rebuild reruns everything); otherwise the gate reads the batch's other files, and on a new token `srcGen` is bumped and `Processor.Rerun` starts the root inputs the last bundle used, in goroutines. After the asset step, if esbuild didn't run in the batch, a goroutine waits for the reruns and runs the incremental bundle when an output changed; the run joins it before returning, so the reload waits for both sides. A run that ends before its asset step (a `watch`-only batch, a front error) bundles from the last input, or clears the asset key so the next run bundles. The gate is created after the first successful bundle with a Tailwind input (seeded in the background from the files as Tailwind just saw them, with the watcher's `skipDir`).
- **`internal/doctor`**: the `tailwind` check, after `typescript`; inputs are found by walking `src/` (`.css` files, and `<style>` blocks parsed with `compiler.Styles`).
- **The standalone CLI's temp file race**: the Bun-compiled binary extracts its native addon to `$TMPDIR/.<hash>-00000000.node`, a fixed name, so concurrent runs fail now and then with `cannot open shared object file` (1 of 12 concurrent runs in a test). Each run gets its own `TMPDIR` (a temporary directory, removed after), which fixed it (32 of 32).
- **Tests**: detection, inlining and positions, lookup, cleanup, the processor's cache and generations, the gate (`internal/tailwind`); the fake-CLI plumbing, error positions and the not-found error, plus a real-CLI run of the end-to-end example with a Fontsource package (font hashed and emitted), determinism and a real `@apply` error (`internal/assets`); the dev reruns with the fake CLI and a real `orbit dev` session that adds a class (`internal/dev`); `orbit build` + serving + determinism + `orbit check`'s error with the real CLI (`internal/build`); the doctor cases; the VS Code manifest (`editors/vscode`); `docs/tailwind.md`'s examples built with the real CLI (`internal/docstest`, skipped with a notice without it). `class:list` isn't in the end-to-end examples: it isn't implemented on this branch (another change adds it); the spike verified that Tailwind finds its literals.

### Deviations from the spec, and why
- **Every input runs on stdin** (`-i -`), working directory = the input's directory (the stylesheet's, or the `.orbit` file's), with `source("<project root, relative>")` added to an `@import "tailwindcss"` (or `tailwindcss/utilities.css`, or a `@tailwind utilities`) that has none, instead of `-i <abs>` from the project root for root files. After the Q3 inlining, the text Tailwind must see isn't a file on disk; stdin plus the input's directory keeps the input's own relative `@plugin`/`@config`/`@source`/`@reference` paths resolving as written, and `source(…)` keeps detection at the project root (decided by rule, simplest; logged in `docs/DECISIONS.md`).
- **A `.css` file with Tailwind's at-rules but no `tailwindcss` import is an input too** (§5d's Q2 text counted only imports and `@tailwind` for `.css` files), like a block input (decided by rule, Astro: `@tailwindcss/vite` runs on every stylesheet).
- **No `url()` hint**: the hint planned for Q3's recommended option isn't needed, since Orbit rewrites the `url()`s.
- **The not-found message** reads `<marker> needs the Tailwind CSS CLI v4, which wasn't found (<v3 found, problems>); install it: <lines>; see orbit doctor`.
- **The first `orbit dev` build** runs the lookup and the first Tailwind run inside the first bundle, before the first `go build`, not in parallel with it (one-time, ~0.15 s).

### Measurements (Ryzen 7 9700X, 16 threads, Go 1.27.3, Tailwind 4.3.3 standalone; shared machine)
- **Without Tailwind, unchanged**: `bench/devloop -dev`, template edit 173 → 173 ms, CSS edit 17 → 17 ms (main vs this branch, load 0.6–0.8); `orbit build` at 200 files 0.31 → 0.30 s (load 5.8).
- **With Tailwind** (`bench/devloop -dev -tailwind`, every edit brings a new token; load 13–21): template 183 → 188 ms (16 threads) and 183 → 205 ms (4 cores); frontmatter 173 → 180 / 178 → 200 ms; `.go` body 594 → 642 ms / 1.10 → 1.04 s (noise); `<style>`/`<script>`-only 18 → 90 ms / 23–27 → 91 ms. `orbit build` 0.30 → 0.40 s (load 8.5). Details in `docs/performance.md`.

## 5e. Code splitting
**Status: Implemented** (2026-10-26; accepted the same day). **Dev cost accepted by the user** (2026-10-26): a `<style>`/`<script>` edit in `orbit dev` goes from ~16 to ~26–31 ms at 200 files (the Cost section's 18 → 13 ms came from a prototype binary that measured (A)), `orbit generate` +~11 ms, prod bundling +~15 ms; template, frontmatter and `.go` edits unchanged. Implementation notes, deviations and measurements at the end of this section. User answers, all as recommended: (Q1) (B) a page's own script and style blocks stay in its entry, only imported files become shared chunks; (Q2) `<link rel="modulepreload">` for the entry's static chunks; (Q3) CSS of `import()`ed modules stays in the page stylesheet; (Q5) deploy skew is docs only. (Q4) no preloading of lazy/dynamic chunks' dependencies and (Q6) no opt-out: decided by the coordinator with the recommendations (rule: simplest). No Vite (user decision). Moved up from §12 by the user, prompted by porting `nyth` (an Astro site that lazy-loads Lexical, a quick-search engine and a read-aloud engine with dynamic `import()`). Written before implementation, like §5c; the prototype is in `scratchpad/codesplit/` (a patched copy of `internal/assets`, not committed).

**Goal**: a dynamic `import()` in a script loads a separate file when it runs, instead of being bundled into every page that reaches it; a module shared by a page bundle and a `client:*` lazy entry exists once, with one module state; code shared between pages is a cacheable file of its own. No opt-out, no new dependency, no runtime cost on the SSR path, and the dev loop stays as fast as today or faster.

**Today (verified with the prototype)**: each page is one self-contained esbuild entry. esbuild without `Splitting` turns `await import("../scripts/heavy")` into `Promise.resolve().then(() => (init_heavy(), heavy_exports))`: the "lazy" module, and the CSS it imports, are in every page bundle that can reach it. A module imported by both the page bundle and a lazy entry (`src/scripts/shared.ts` with a counter) is copied into both, and the two copies count separately.

### End-to-end example
```orbit file=src/components/QuickSearch.orbit
<quick-search><button type="button">Search</button></quick-search>
<script>
  import { track } from "../scripts/analytics";
  class QuickSearch extends HTMLElement {
    connectedCallback(): void {
      track("search-ready");
      this.querySelector("button")!.addEventListener("click", async () => {
        // A separate chunk, fetched on the first click only.
        const { createQuickSearch } = await import("../lib/quickSearch");
        createQuickSearch(this).open();
      });
    }
  }
  customElements.define("quick-search", QuickSearch);
</script>
```
```orbit file=src/pages/index.orbit
---
import (
	Base "../layouts/Base.orbit"
	QuickSearch "../components/QuickSearch.orbit"
	Comments "../components/Comments.orbit"
)
---
<Base title="Home">
  <QuickSearch />
  <Comments client:visible />   <!-- its script also imports ../scripts/analytics -->
</Base>
```
`orbit build --verbose` (sizes illustrative):
```
src/pages/index.orbit
  /_orbit/index-L5AGVGNY.css                      1.30 kB  gzip    0.57 kB
  /_orbit/index-BAFLXIK7.js                       0.42 kB  gzip    0.30 kB
    /_orbit/chunk-X65WUZF2.js                     0.31 kB  gzip    0.22 kB  (preload)
  /_orbit/Comments-3QCXUABQ.js                    0.30 kB  gzip    0.24 kB  (lazy)
src/pages/about.orbit
  /_orbit/about-ZYDYI6W6.css                      0.90 kB  gzip    0.45 kB
  /_orbit/about-4GKIGORU.js                       0.42 kB  gzip    0.30 kB
    /_orbit/chunk-X65WUZF2.js                     0.31 kB  gzip    0.22 kB  (preload)
loaded with import()
  /_orbit/quickSearch-PDBSW4EO.js                48.10 kB  gzip   16.02 kB
total CSS                                         2.20 kB  gzip    1.02 kB
total JS                                         49.55 kB  gzip   17.08 kB
```
The page's `<head>` gets:
```html
<link rel="stylesheet" href="/_orbit/index-L5AGVGNY.css">
<script type="module" src="/_orbit/index-BAFLXIK7.js"></script>
<link rel="modulepreload" href="/_orbit/chunk-X65WUZF2.js">
```
`index-BAFLXIK7.js` holds `QuickSearch`'s block (and every other eager block of the page, in graph order) and starts with `import{a as t}from"/_orbit/chunk-X65WUZF2.js"` (`analytics.ts`, shared with the lazy `Comments` entry and with `about`); its click handler calls `import("/_orbit/quickSearch-PDBSW4EO.js")`. `analytics.ts` runs once per page, whether the page bundle or `Comments` loads first.

### Rules
1. **One esbuild build, `Splitting: true`**, with every page entry and every lazy entry together (the build is already one call, §5; ESM is already the format). esbuild puts each module in a chunk per set of entries that reach it; a dynamic `import()` target is an entry of its own, so it becomes its own chunk with its static imports shared the same way.
2. **Block scripts stay in their page's entry (per-page block modules) (Q1).** Each page imports its blocks under a page-qualified specifier (`orbit:script/<ComponentID>-<i>@<page>.ts`, resolved to the `.orbit` file with the suffix `?orbit-script=<i>&page=<page entry name>`), so esbuild sees one module per page and inlines it into that page's entry; only **imported files** (`.ts`/`.js` files, npm packages, `orbit:client`, CSS files imported from scripts) are shared across entries. This is what keeps rule 6's evaluation order and keeps a page at one request plus its shared imports (measured below). Blocks have no exports anyone can import (a `./X.orbit` import is the §5c client module, not the block), so a block copied into several pages, or into a page and a lazy entry, can't split any state: per page it still runs once, from the page bundle or from its lazy entry (eager wins, §5). Style blocks get the same page qualifier, so their CSS stubs never form empty shared JS chunks; their scoped CSS is computed once per block and reused by every copy. Lazy entries keep the unqualified specifier: they're per component already.
3. **Dynamic `import()`** of a relative file or package becomes `import("/_orbit/<name>-<hash>.js")`, loaded when it runs. Its CSS: rule 7.
4. **Shared modules**: a module reached from several entries (pages, lazy entries, dynamic chunks) is in exactly one chunk, evaluated once per page load. This replaces §5's "shared code is duplicated" caveat, for lazy entries and for `server:defer` components alike.
5. **Names and URLs**: entries keep `[name]-[hash]` (`index-…`, `Comments-…`); chunks are `ChunkNames: "[name]-[hash]"`, in the same flat `/_orbit/` directory, as Astro puts entries and chunks in `/_astro/[name].[hash].js`: `/_orbit/chunk-<hash>.js` for shared code and `/_orbit/<module>-<hash>.js` for a dynamic-import target (esbuild's `[name]`, from the imported file's base name; a name equal to a page's or component's differs by its hash). Hashes are esbuild's content hashes, which include the hashes of the chunks a file imports, so a change to `analytics.ts` renames its chunk and every file importing it, and nothing else. Deterministic: two clean builds of the same input produce byte-identical files (prototype: identical names; a test asserts contents). The page qualifier is the page's entry name, not its index, so adding a page renames nothing else (it only appears in dev source comments).
6. **Evaluation order**: a page's block scripts run in graph order, as today (rule 2). Modules they import run before the page's blocks, each after its own imports (ESM). Two shared chunks that don't import each other run in esbuild's chunk order, not in import order (esbuild's known splitting limitation, evanw/esbuild#399): with `import "./polyfill"; import "lib"` in one block, `lib` may run first if they end up in different chunks. Documented rule: **a module that needs another module's side effects imports it itself.** Without rule 2, the same reordering hits components: the prototype's page rendering `<First />` then `<Second />` ran `second` before `first` when `Second` was also used on another page.
7. **CSS**: unchanged. The page stylesheet is still one file in §5's order; esbuild computes each entry's CSS from its whole import graph independently of JS chunking (prototype: every page stylesheet of `examples/blog` and the 200-file `bench/devloop` project is byte-identical with and without splitting). CSS imported by a module that is only reached through `import()` is in the page stylesheet of every page that can reach it, as a lazy component's CSS is today (Q3). esbuild also emits that dynamic chunk's own `<module>-<hash>.css`, which nothing links: it isn't written.
8. **Empty chunks are removed.** A CSS file or a types-only module imported from scripts on several pages gets an empty JS chunk (esbuild has no option against it; `examples/blog` without rule 2 had one empty chunk imported by 3 pages, and a script-level `import "./lib.css"` on two pages gives one even with rule 2). After the build, `collect` drops non-entry JS outputs with no code (`isEmptyJS`) and deletes the `import"/_orbit/…";` statements that reference them, repeating until nothing changes; a page entry left empty gets no `<script>` (§5). Where a source map exists (dev, `"sourcemap": true`), the statement is replaced by the same number of spaces, so mappings stay valid; otherwise it's removed. The file names keep esbuild's hash (still a deterministic function of the input).
9. **Head injection (Q2)**: the entry's `<script type="module">` as today, plus one `<link rel="modulepreload" href>` per chunk in the entry's static import closure (from the metafile's `imports`, `import-statement` kind, transitively; dynamic imports excluded), after the script tag. Precomputed per route in `NewSite` like the other tags: no per-request cost, no allocation change. A lazy entry's chunks and dynamic chunks aren't preloaded (they'd defeat the laziness; Q4).
10. **Lazy entries and the loader**: unchanged. The lazy map still maps a component ID to entry URLs; an entry is now usually a few imports of chunks plus the component's blocks, and the browser fetches the chunks it doesn't have yet when the loader imports it. `<orbit-island>` fragments carry no assets (§5) and are unaffected; `loader/` doesn't change.
11. **Cache headers**: chunks are under `/_orbit/` and hashed, so they get `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` like every bundle (`static.go`, no change), and `no-cache` in dev.
12. **Deploys (Q5)**: `orbit build` replaces `dist/client` (and `--embed` embeds only the new files). A page loaded before a deploy that later runs an `import()`, or meets a lazy strategy, asks for a chunk of the old build, which is a 404 unless something kept it. That was already true for lazy entries, but `import()` on a click makes it common. The docs (`deploy.md`) recommend keeping the previous deploy's `dist/client/_orbit/*` next to the new files (hashed names never collide; copy them into `dist/client/_orbit/` before building the image, or serve `/_orbit/` from object storage or a CDN that only adds files), and show a `window.addEventListener("unhandledrejection", …)` reload pattern for scripts that `import()`. Astro has no built-in answer either (its Vercel adapter has skew protection; Vite fires `vite:preloadError` for the same case).
13. **No opt-out** (Q6): nothing measured costs enough to justify a switch.

### Manifest and asset kinds
- **New asset kind `"modulepreload"`** (the `rel` value): `orbit.Asset{Kind: "modulepreload", URL: "/_orbit/chunk-X65WUZF2.js"}`, one per chunk of rule 9, in the entry's closure order. `Route.Assets` order becomes css, js, lazy, modulepreload (the existing stable sort by kind); `headTags` writes the new kind; older runtimes ignore unknown kinds already.
- **Prod**: the compiled `orbit_gen/manifest` gains those rows, nothing else: `{Kind: "modulepreload", URL: "/_orbit/chunk-X65WUZF2.js"},` after the page's `js` row. Dynamic chunks aren't in the manifest (only the bundles reference them).
- **Dev**: `.orbit/dev/manifest.json` gains `{"kind": "modulepreload", "url": "/_orbit/…"}` entries (same shape as today, `devAsset`). `WriteDev` writes and prunes chunks like any other file.
- **`orbit build --verbose`**: under each page, its `modulepreload` chunks indented below its `js` row with `(preload)`, its lazy entries with `(lazy)`; then a `loaded with import()` section listing every chunk that only dynamic imports reach; totals count each file once, chunks included (today's totals count `css` and `js` kinds only).
- `assets.Result.Files` gains the chunk files (and their `.map`s); `WriteFiles`, `WriteDev`'s pruning, `newFSFiles` and `--embed` need no change.

### (Q) points
1. **(Q1) What gets shared**: (B) block scripts and styles stay per page, only imported files are split (recommended: keeps block order, measured 2.0 requests per page on the 200-file project, no empty style chunks; costs ~20 ms more `orbit generate` bundling at 200 files (18 → 37 ms median), none in the dev rebuild (18 → 13 ms either way), see Cost), vs. (A) esbuild's default with shared block modules (fewest bytes and the best cross-page caching, and the fastest build, but 21 requests per page on the same project, components' scripts run in esbuild's chunk order rather than graph order, and every style block shared by several pages adds an empty chunk that rule 8 must strip), vs. (B') only script blocks per page, style blocks shared (about 5 ms less than (B), but 38 empty style chunks per build to strip on that project). Astro/Vite (Rollup) shares at module granularity like (A), with Rollup's correct evaluation order, and since Astro 5 each component's script is rendered as its own `<script type="module">` where it's declared (deduplicated per page), so scripts run in document order; (B) is the closest esbuild gets to that order.
2. **(Q2) `<link rel="modulepreload">` for the entry's static chunks**: emit them (recommended: removes one round trip, entry → chunks, before scripts run; the closure is flat, since esbuild's entry imports every chunk it needs directly; ~60 B of HTML per chunk, about 1 per page on the 200-file project, ~15 B gzipped), vs. none (Astro's behavior: Astro 5.8.1 writes only the script's `<script type="module">`; no `modulepreload` appears in its own code, and Vite's preload helper adds them only at `import()` time, for a dynamic chunk's dependencies; Vite's HTML mode emits them for entries). Not a minor detail by rule: it trades HTML bytes against latency on every page.
3. **(Q3) CSS of a dynamically imported module**: in the page stylesheet of every page that can reach it (recommended: esbuild's behavior, today's behavior, the same rule as a lazy component's CSS; no flash of unstyled content and no runtime helper; nyth imports no CSS from its lazy chunks), vs. loaded with the chunk (Vite's `__vitePreload` adds the chunk's `<link rel="stylesheet">` before resolving `import()`): Orbit would have to rewrite every `import()` into a helper call with a generated CSS map (an esbuild plugin transform over every script), a larger change for a later item if a site needs it.
4. **(Q4) Preloading a lazy entry's or dynamic chunk's dependencies when it loads**: no (recommended: one extra round trip only for chunks the page hasn't loaded yet; most shared chunks already came with the page bundle), vs. the loader adding `modulepreload` links for an entry's chunks when its strategy fires (+~100 B of loader, the lazy map lists chunks), vs. Vite-style `import()` rewriting (same cost as Q3's alternative).
5. **(Q5) Deploy skew**: docs only (recommended, rule 12), vs. an `orbit build --keep-assets <old dist/client>` flag that copies the previous `_orbit/` files into the new output (small, but it makes `dist/` grow without bound unless it also prunes by age), vs. a runtime fallback (the server can't produce an old chunk).
6. **(Q6) Opt-out**: none (recommended: the dev rebuild is faster with splitting, `orbit build` time is within noise (bundling +~20 ms with Q1 (B)), request count stays low with Q1 (B)), vs. `orbit.json` `"splitting": false` (a second code path to test).

Decided by rule (to log in `docs/DECISIONS.md`): chunks flat in `/_orbit/` with esbuild's default `[name]` (Astro: `/_astro/[name].[hash].js` for entries and chunks alike; a `chunks/` subdirectory was the alternative); the asset kind is named `modulepreload` (simplest: the `rel` value); the unreferenced chunk CSS of rule 7 isn't written (simplest: nothing links it); empty-chunk imports blanked where a map exists, removed otherwise (simplest that keeps maps valid); `immutable` caching for chunks (simplest: the existing `/_orbit/` rule); diagnostics of a block copied into several pages are reported once (deduplicated by file, line, column and message; simplest).

### Cost (prototype: `Splitting` + `ChunkNames` in `buildOptions`, page-qualified blocks in `plan.go`, the empty-chunk pass in `collect`; Ryzen 7 9700X, 16 threads, Go 1.27.3, esbuild v0.28.2. The machine was shared and heavily loaded during part of the runs; load averages per row)
- **Dev loop** (`bench/devloop -kinds css,ts -runs 15`, 200 files, 16 threads, median (min) of the esbuild stage, the whole save-to-ready step for these edits; modes in sequence, load average 2.2): today **18 (17) ms** for both `css` and `ts`; (B) (recommended Q1) **13 (12–13) ms**; (A) 13 (12) ms. Splitting makes the incremental rebuild faster: less output to link and write (no copy of `util.ts` and `orbit:client` per page). Sessions at load 5.8–7.8 and 17.9–19.0 gave the same picture (today 18–19, (B) 13–17, (A) 13–15). `orbit dev`'s first build: 834 / 843 / 865 ms (noise).
- **`orbit generate`/`build` bundling** (the prod `Bundle` call, in-process, timed around `esbuildBundler.Bundle`; min and median of 15 alternated runs; load average 2.0–2.3, and the same within 1 ms at 11.7): today **15.6 / 17.8 ms**; (A) 11.3 / 12.5 ms; (B') 30.4 / 31.9 ms; (B) **35.7 / 36.8 ms**. So (B) adds ~20 ms to `orbit generate` (~70 → ~90 ms) and `orbit build` (~320 ms, where it's within run-to-run noise: 313–388 ms both ways). The cause is that a block used on N pages is parsed and minified N times (about 800 block modules instead of 40 on this project); the incremental dev rebuild doesn't pay it, since esbuild re-parses only the changed file. Implementation should first try caching the scoped CSS and padded contents per block (the prototype recomputed them) before accepting the number.
- **Bytes and requests** (`orbit build`, the 200-file `bench/devloop` project, 40 pages, every 4th component with a script importing `util.ts`): per page, today 1 request, 1,924 B raw / 323 B gzip; (A) 21.1 requests, 3,798 B / 3,333 B gzip (the sum of per-file gzip: small files compress badly); (B) 2.0 requests, 1,993 B / 410 B gzip. All JS files of the build: today 77.0 kB, (A) 38.8 kB, (B) 78.6 kB. `examples/blog`: no shared imports between its pages and its lazy entry, so (B) produces the same files as today, and (A) adds one empty chunk.
- **SSR**: none. The `modulepreload` tags are part of the per-route tag string computed once in `NewSite`; `AllocsPerRun` tests unchanged.
- **CLI binary**: none (esbuild is already linked).

### Considered: Vite (rejected by the user, 2026-10-26)
The user asked whether Orbit should use Vite (Rolldown) for splitting, then decided to keep esbuild: Vite needs Node or Bun at build time, while esbuild runs inside the `orbit` binary behind the one internal `Bundler` interface (a settled decision). Partial numbers from the prototype, kept for the record (Vite 8.3.4 with Rolldown 1.2.15, installed in `scratchpad/codesplit/vite/` only; the same 200-file graph dumped as real files, 40 page entries; load average 2.6–4.5):
- **Output**: like (A): 38 shared chunks, 21.1 JS requests per page, plus **20.2 CSS files per page** (Vite's per-chunk CSS code splitting; Astro adds its own per-page CSS handling on top), 3,650 B raw / 3,328 B gzip of JS per page; Rollup's evaluation order is correct (entries import block chunks in graph order).
- **Build**: 19–23 ms min, 22–26 ms median in a warm process (vs. 36.8 ms for (B), 17.8 ms today, in-process); **123–146 ms per cold `node` process** (103–104 ms with Bun), which Orbit would pay on every `orbit build`/`generate`, or keep a resident child process in dev.
- **Watch rebuild** after a block `.ts` / `.css` edit (`build --watch`, file write to bundle end, so including the file watcher's latency): 20.6 / 18.8 ms median (vs. 13 ms for (B) in `bench/devloop`).
- What it would have added: `manualChunks`-style chunk control, CSS loaded with dynamic chunks, `vite:preloadError`, and Vite plugins (`@tailwindcss/vite`).

### Tests (what must exist)
- **`internal/assets`** (table-driven over small inputs, like `assets_test.go`):
  - a block with `import("./heavy")`: `heavy`'s code is in `heavy-*.js` (no entry point in the metafile) and in no entry; the entry contains `import("/_orbit/heavy-`; `heavy`'s CSS is in the page stylesheet and no `heavy-*.css` file is written.
  - a module imported by a page block and by a lazy entry: its code appears in exactly one output file, imported by both.
  - order: `<First />` then `<Second />` on page 1, `<Second />` alone on page 2: page 1's entry contains both blocks, `first` before `second`, and no chunk contains block code.
  - empty chunks: a CSS file imported from scripts on two pages leaves no empty chunk and no import of one; a page whose only script imports types gets no JS asset; with a dev build, the blanked entry's source map still maps the block to its `.orbit` line:col.
  - `modulepreload` assets: the closure of a two-level chunk graph, in order; none for lazy entries or dynamic chunks.
  - stylesheets byte-identical to the expected ones of the existing tests (the CSS-order tests pass unchanged).
  - determinism: two `Bundle`s of the same input in fresh bundlers give identical `Files`; adding a page doesn't rename another page's files in a prod build.
  - diagnostics: a syntax error in a block used on three pages is reported once, at its `.orbit` position.
  - `TestDevRebuild`: chunk files in `.orbit/dev/_orbit/`, stale chunks pruned, the manifest's `modulepreload` rows.
  - source maps (`sourcemap_test.go`): page-qualified suffixes are stripped, so sources are the `.orbit` paths; chunk maps point at the imported `.ts` files.
- **Runtime**: `headTags` emits `<link rel="modulepreload">` (escaped URL), in asset order; the dev manifest decodes the kind; site tests with a `modulepreload` asset.
- **`cmd/orbit`**: `--verbose` golden with a preload row, a lazy row and the `import()` section; totals count each file once.
- **End to end**: `examples/blog` gains a dynamic import (e.g. the search page's suggestions loaded with `import()` on focus) and a module shared with the lazy `ShareButtons`; `internal/build`'s e2e test asserts the chunk files, the `modulepreload` tag in the rendered HTML, that the dynamic target isn't in any entry, and that the lazy entry imports the shared chunk instead of containing it. The loader's `bun test` suite runs unchanged.
- **`bench/devloop`**: the `css` and `ts` rows are rerun and published in `docs/performance.md`.

### User-docs changes (with the implementation)
- `styles-and-scripts.md` "Bundling and deduplication per page": replace "Each page's bundle is self-contained … not yet supported" with shared chunks, `import()` chunks, `modulepreload`, the evaluation-order rule (rule 6), and where an `import()`ed module's CSS goes; the `client:*` list: drop "Shared code is duplicated", say shared modules have one state.
- `lazy-loading.md`: remove "Shared modules are duplicated" (and its "Code splitting is planned" line); add a short "Loading code on demand with `import()`" pointer for code that isn't a component.
- `deploy.md`: chunks are immutable like the other `/_orbit/` files; the deploy-skew note and how to keep the previous `_orbit/` files (rule 12).
- `reference/cli.md`: the new `--verbose` output; `reference/runtime-api.md`: the `modulepreload` asset kind.
- `coming-from-astro.md`: dynamic `import()` works as in Astro; differences (CSS of lazy chunks loads with the page; no `vite:preloadError`).
- `performance.md`: requests per page and the dev rebuild numbers.
- `docs/DECISIONS.md`: the decided-by-rule items above.

**Plan updates on acceptance**: §5 (esbuild entries, lazy entries, the duplication caveat, the manifest's asset kinds), §4 (`Asset` kinds, `headTags`), §7.1 (no change in behavior; the CSS/JS-only rebuild numbers), §12 (the bullet removed).

### Out of scope
- Loading a dynamic chunk's CSS with it (Q3 alternative) and preloading its dependencies (Q4).
- Manual chunks or a minimum chunk size (esbuild has neither; Rollup's `manualChunks`/`experimentalMinChunkSize` have no equivalent).
- `orbit build --keep-assets` and server-side skew handling (Q5).
- Splitting for `<script is:inline>` (never bundled) and CSS code splitting between pages (each page keeps one stylesheet).
- CSP hashes for the inline loader (§12, unchanged).

### Risks
- esbuild marks code splitting as a work in progress; the ordering limitation (rule 6) is the known one, and rule 2 confines it to imported modules. A future esbuild that changes chunking may change file names and the number of chunks, not behavior; the tests pin behavior, not names.
- Request count grows with the number of distinct sets of pages that share an imported module: a site with many small shared `.ts` helpers gets many small chunks (no minimum chunk size in esbuild). Measure on nyth when ported; `--verbose` shows it.
- Deploy skew (rule 12) becomes visible to users of `import()`: docs only for now.

### Implementation notes
- `plan.go`: `copyOf` gives each block copy its specifier `orbit:script/<ID>-<i>@<page>.ts` and suffix `?orbit-script=<i>&page=<page>` (`<page>` the entry's bundle name, chosen before the page's blocks are registered and marked taken only if the page gets an entry, so names are unchanged); lazy entries use the unqualified forms. All copies of a block share one `*block` (`plan.base`): scoping, padding and the Tailwind load (a `sync.Once` per block, so `Inline` and `Process` run once per block per build) are computed once. `plan.blocks` maps a specifier to a `blockCopy{*block, suffix}`.
- `esbuild.go`: `Splitting: true`, `ChunkNames: "[name]-[hash]"`. `collect` reads each output's `imports`; `dropEmptyChunks` removes non-entry JS outputs with no code that aren't `dynamic-import` targets, deleting (prod) or blanking with spaces (dev, `"sourcemap": true`) `import"…";`/`import "…";` statements, to a fixpoint; the static closure (`import-statement`, breadth-first) of each page module gives its `modulepreload` assets; afterwards, entry JS that no asset links (empty) and every CSS output that no asset links (an `import()` target's stylesheet) are dropped. Diagnostics are deduplicated by value (file, line, column, warning, message). Source maps are rewritten in parallel, and the `.orbit` onLoad cuts the root prefix instead of calling `filepath.Rel`, both found in profiles of the copies.
- Runtime: `headTags` writes `modulepreload`; no other runtime, manifest-codegen or dev-manifest change was needed (both are generic over kinds). `cmd/orbit` `printBundles` prints the §5e layout; its totals sum every `.css` and `.js` file of `Result.Files`.
- `examples/blog`: the search page loads `src/scripts/suggestions.ts` with `import()` on the first focus of the search box; `util.ts` (LikeButton, eager on the post page) is also imported by the lazy `ShareButtons` (`flash`), so it is a preloaded chunk both import.
- Tests: `internal/assets/split_test.go` (dynamic chunk, shared module once, modulepreload and order of kinds, blocks in their entries, order, empty chunks in prod/prod+map/dev with the map still pointing at the `.orbit` line:col, stylesheets byte-identical with `noSplit`, determinism and adding a page, diagnostics once, two-level closure), `TestDevRebuildChunks`, `TestTailwindBlockOnce` (fake CLI: one load, one run, one diagnostic for a block on three pages), `TestSourceMaps` (no suffix or qualifier in sources; the chunk map points at `util.ts`), `head_test.go`, `cmd/orbit` `TestPrintBundles`, `internal/build` `TestExampleBlog`.

### Deviations from the spec, and why
- **Dev rebuild cost.** The Cost section's dev-loop row for (B) (13 ms) was measured with a `bench/devloop` binary built before the prototype's per-page switch existed (the binary predates the `ORBIT_PERPAGE` code), so it measured (A)/no qualifier. Measured for real, a style or script edit costs **16 → 26–31 ms** at 200 files (alternated with main, `-dev`, load average 9–10; the stage mode without `-dev`: 18 → 31 ms at load 0.8): the copies are re-linked and re-printed on every rebuild (esbuild re-runs plugins and re-links all modules; parsing hits its cache). Caching per block (scoped CSS, padding, Tailwind) and the two small fixes above didn't change it measurably; (B') (style blocks unqualified) measured the same in dev. Open question for the user (CLAUDE.md: build/regenerate time).
- **Prod bundling** with the per-block caching: 15 → 31 ms median (14 → 28 min) in a fresh process, vs. the prototype's 18 → 37 ms; `orbit generate` 67 → 78 ms (the bundle overlaps `go list -export`).
- `orbit build --verbose`'s `loaded with import()` section lists every JS file that no page lists, which includes chunks that only lazy entries import (the loader `import()`s those entries); logged in `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- An empty `import()` target is never removed (an `import()` of a missing file would fail); not covered by rule 8; logged.

### Measurements (Ryzen 7 9700X, 16 threads, Go 1.27.3, esbuild v0.28.2; the 200-file `bench/devloop` project; alternated with main)
- Style/script edit (`bench/devloop -dev -kinds css,ts -runs 15`, two rounds, load 9–10): main css 16 (15), ts 15–16 (15) ms; code splitting css 27–30 (25), ts 26–30 (25–26) ms. At load 33–36: main 18–20, code splitting 26–30.
- Prod `Bundle` (15 fresh processes each, load 1.4): main 15.3 median (13.7 min), code splitting 30.8 (28.2). At load 30: 17.6 vs 35.8.
- `orbit generate` (15 each, load 11): 67 (64) vs 78 (75) ms.
- Per page: 1 → 2.0 JS requests, 1,914 → 1,976 B raw, 320 → 406 B gzip (sum of per-file gzip); all JS 76.6 → 77.9 kB; `examples/blog`: one shared chunk (`util.ts`, preloaded by the post page) and one `import()` chunk.
- SSR: unchanged (`TestRenderAllocs`/`TestServeAllocs` pass unchanged; the tags are part of the per-route string).

## 6. CLI (`cmd/orbit`)
| Command | Behavior |
|---|---|
| `orbit generate` | Discover, parse and generate `orbit_gen/` (also used by CI and fresh clones). |
| `orbit build [--embed] [--verbose]` | Steps below. `--embed` (or `"embed": true` in `orbit.json`) produces a **single-binary deploy**: see step 6. `--verbose` (user request, milestone 6) prints each page's CSS/JS bundles with raw and gzip sizes, and totals; without it the build is silent. |
| `orbit dev [--port 4321] [--host]` | Dev server (§7). Listens on localhost only; `--host` listens on all interfaces (decided by coordinator: Astro behavior). |
| `orbit preview` | Run `dist/server` with the user's environment, so it listens on `Addr()` (`PORT=3000 orbit preview` picks a port). No flags. Exits with the server's exit code; errors if `dist/server` is missing. |
| `orbit new <dir> [--module path]` | Scaffold from an embedded template (`embed.FS`) into `<dir>`, which must not exist or be empty. `--module` sets the module path; the default is the directory name (user decision). `go.mod` gets `go 1.27`, **always** a `toolchain goX.Y.Z` line with the Go version the CLI was built with (`runtime.Version()`; without it, `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` fetches go1.27.0), and `tool github.com/orbit-z/orbit/cmd/orbit` (at the running CLI's version). Then run `orbit generate`, then `go mod tidy`. Generate must come first: the scaffolded `cmd/server` imports `orbit_gen/app`, so tidy fails without it.<br>**Starter**: `cmd/server/main.go`, a base layout with a scoped `<style>`, `src/pages/index.orbit` and `404.orbit`, `public/favicon.svg`, `tsconfig.json`, `src/env.d.ts`, `.gitignore` (`orbit_gen/`, `.orbit/`, `dist/`, `node_modules/`); no `Card` component, no `orbit.json` and no `package.json`. `orbit build` on it produces `dist/server` with no edits.<br>**Also scaffolds a `Dockerfile`** (plus `.dockerignore`), as a multi-stage build that works on the unmodified starter:<br>• **Build stage**: `FROM golang:<CLI's Go version>` (exact tag), `CGO_ENABLED=0 go tool orbit build --embed`. No Node or Bun is needed, because esbuild runs inside the CLI.<br>• The build stage uses `WORKDIR /src` and copies `go.mod`/`go.sum` first for a cached `go mod download` layer.<br>• **Final stage**: `FROM gcr.io/distroless/static-debian12:nonroot`, with only `dist/server` copied in, as `/server`. `ENV PORT=8080`, `EXPOSE 8080`, `USER nonroot:nonroot`, `ENTRYPOINT ["/server"]`. Typically a ~10–20 MB image.<br>• **`.dockerignore`**: `.git`, `.orbit`, `dist`, `orbit_gen`. It keeps `node_modules` in the context, because esbuild resolves npm imports from it and the image has no Node or Bun.<br>It sets `PORT`, not `ORBIT_ADDR`, so a `PORT` set by the platform (Cloud Run, Fly, Heroku) wins: `Addr()` reads `$ORBIT_ADDR`, else `:$PORT`, else `:4321` (§4). |
| `orbit lsp` | Language server over stdio (§9). |
| `orbit fmt [-l] [-d] [-w] [paths]` | Formatter (§6.1, implemented): frontmatter, `{…}` and control headers with `go/format`, script/style with `bun fmt`; never the HTML. |
| `orbit create-key` | Prints a new random 32-byte island key in base64, for `ORBIT_KEY` (§4). It's needed for rolling deploys and for several builds behind one load balancer (decided by rule: Astro has `astro create-key`). |
| `orbit doctor [--json]` | Checks the environment and project, printing ✓ / ⚠ / ✗ with a fix command for each problem. Exit code 1 on any ✗. Outside a project (a `go.mod` plus `src/`, the LSP's rule), only the environment checks run (Go, gopls, Bun, TypeScript). `--json` prints `{"checks":[{"name","status","message","fix"}]}`, one entry per check in the order below, with `status` `"ok"`, `"warning"` or `"error"` and an empty `fix` for a passing check. **Checks**:<br>• **Go**: the `go` command, the active toolchain, and that `go.mod` has `go ≥ 1.27` and a `toolchain` line.<br>• **CLI version**: with the tool directive, `go list` of the pinned CLI package (the hand-over works, no build); without it, the CLI version vs. the `go.mod` requirement.<br>• **gopls** (found on PATH): its version, and its build Go version ≥ the project's `go` directive. This is the LSP refuse rule (§9), so ✗ → `go install golang.org/x/tools/gopls@latest`. ⚠ if older than the recommended v0.24.0.<br>• **Bun ≥ 1.4.4** for `orbit check`'s TypeScript half (⚠ only, with the upgrade command).<br>• **TypeScript 7** (§9b) for `<script>` blocks in the editor, found as `orbit lsp` finds it (`internal/tsengine`): ✓ `tsc 7.0.2 (path)` or "TypeScript 7.0.2 through bun x"; ⚠ only (missing, or a `tsc` older than 7.0.2), and inside a project ✓ "not needed" when no `.orbit` file has a script the editor's TypeScript applies to.<br>• **Generated code**: `orbit_gen/` present and fresh: the project is regenerated in memory (`Generator.Plan`, no writes) and diffed with `orbit_gen/`, stale files included, except `orbit_gen/manifest/manifest.go`, which `orbit dev` keeps empty (⚠ → `orbit generate`; about 60 ms on the starter).<br>• **`.gitignore`** covers `orbit_gen/`, `.orbit/` and `dist/`.<br>• **Config**: `orbit.json` is valid; the server entry exists; `src/locals` imports no `orbit_gen/app`.<br>• **tsconfig** (§5c): when a `.orbit` file uses `.JSON(` or `set:json`, `tsconfig.json`'s `rootDirs` includes `orbit_gen/types` (⚠ with the two-line fix).<br>The LSP's old-gopls error and the CLI's version-mismatch errors point to `orbit doctor`. |
| `orbit check` | Type-check without building. **Go**: generate (including Orbit's own checks, such as unknown props), then `go vet ./orbit_gen/...`, whose output already points at `.orbit` positions and is cleaned up like build errors (§3.3). **TypeScript**: extracts each processed `<script>` to `.orbit/check/<file>.<n>.ts`, with a generated `tsconfig.json` that extends the user's and includes `src/**/*.ts` and `orbit_gen/types/*.d.ts`, with `rootDirs` `["../..", ".", "../../orbit_gen/types"]` so `./X.orbit` imports resolve to the generated client modules (§5c; errors inside them print prefixed `internal: `). Runs **`bun check --no-pretty --no-script -p .orbit/check`** (`--no-script` so a `package.json` `check` script can't take over). Bun ≥ 1.4.4's built-in type checker reports the same errors as tsc, uses all cores, and needs no `typescript` package: measured 14 ms vs 454 ms for `bun x tsc` on one file. Orbit parses its tsc-format output (`file(line,col): error TSxxxx: …`) and maps positions back to the `.orbit` line:col via a per-script offset table. **Bun ≥ 1.4.4 is required** for the TS half: if Bun is missing or older, the TS half is skipped with a notice naming the install/upgrade command, and the Go half still runs. Exit code is non-zero on any error. |

**Exit codes** (every command, following Go's `flag` package): `0` success (warnings don't change it), `1` failure (compile error, failed check, a ✗ in `doctor`), `2` usage error (unknown command or flag, missing argument; usage printed to stderr). `orbit preview` exits with the server's own code.

**Flags**: Go's `flag` syntax (`--port 3000`, `--port=3000`, `-port 3000`). `orbit build` has no build flags of its own besides `--embed`: Go build flags come from `GOFLAGS` (e.g. `GOFLAGS=-tags=prod`; with `--embed`, Orbit adds `orbit_embed`), and `GOOS`/`GOARCH`/`CGO_ENABLED` pass through to `go build` for cross-compiling.

**Positions in CLI output** are printed relative to the project root as `src/…:L:C`; Go's own `./src/…` prefix is normalized away, while plain `go build`/`go vet` still print `./src/…`.

**Version pinning**: when the project's `go.mod` has the `tool github.com/orbit-z/orbit/cmd/orbit` directive, a globally installed `orbit` hands the command to the pinned CLI, so the generator always matches the runtime version in `go.mod` (implemented, milestone 11): `go tool -n orbit` builds the pinned CLI (or finds it in the build cache) and prints its path, and the global CLI execs that binary directly (`syscall.Exec` on Unix; a child process on Windows), with `ORBIT_REEXEC=1` set to prevent loops and the toolchain's `bin` first on `PATH`, as `go tool` does. Going around the `go` command keeps Ctrl-C clean: `go tool orbit dev` exits 1 on Ctrl-C (the tool gets SIGINT twice, from the terminal and from `go`), the hand-over exits 0. It costs about 35 ms per command (`go tool orbit` itself adds about 65 ms). A global CLI is one whose main module is `github.com/orbit-z/orbit`; a `go tool` build never hands over. `orbit new` and `orbit doctor` are exempt (doctor must run when the pinned CLI doesn't build, and it reports the mismatch). Without the directive, a global CLI whose version differs from the `github.com/orbit-z/orbit` requirement in `go.mod` stops with a version-mismatch error pointing to `orbit doctor`; the check is skipped when `go.mod` has a `replace` of the module with a local directory, or doesn't require it. The tool directive adds the CLI's dependencies (esbuild, fsnotify, tdewolff) to the user's module graph; the runtime package itself stays stdlib-only.

**Fresh clones and CI**: `orbit_gen/` is gitignored by default, so a clone needs one step before anything else. Document it in `project-structure.md`:
```sh
go tool orbit generate     # creates orbit_gen/
go vet ./...
go tool orbit build
```
Alternative: commit `orbit_gen/` (replace the `orbit_gen/` line in `.gitignore` with `orbit_gen/static/`, which `--embed` writes and stays ignored). Then plain `go build ./...` works on a fresh clone; generation is deterministic, so CI can check `go tool orbit generate && git diff --exit-code orbit_gen`.

**`orbit build` pipeline**
1. Load `orbit.json` and read the module path from `go.mod`.
2. Scan `src/**/*.orbit` and parse them all, reporting every error rather than only the first. Build the import graph and check for cycles.
3. Compute scope IDs, rewrite CSS, and run esbuild into `dist/client/_orbit/`, producing the manifest.
4. Generate `orbit_gen/` (two passes with typed writes, §3.3; no type cache), the hook flavor, byte-identical to `orbit generate`. Then the build flavor (§3.5): the SCC rule over the call graph, and a third emit of each impl unit that calls or is called directly (reusing pass 2's typed writes), written to `.orbit/build/` (removed and rewritten each build) with `.orbit/build/overlay.json`.
5. Copy `public/` into `dist/client/`. A conflict with `_orbit/` is an error.
6. `go build -overlay=.orbit/build/overlay.json -o dist/server <server entry>` (the user's `main`, or the generated default; an `-overlay` in `GOFLAGS` is an error, §3.5). Errors already point at `.orbit` lines via line directives; the CLI strips generated positions and rewrites generated type names (§3.3, error output). At runtime, `app.New()` finds the `client/` directory next to the binary (in the binary's own directory, not the working directory) unless `ORBIT_STATIC` or `WithStaticDir` overrides it.
   - **Island key and build ID**: each `orbit build` generates a random island key and a random build ID and passes both with `-ldflags=-X github.com/orbit-z/orbit.buildKey=… -X github.com/orbit-z/orbit.buildID=…`, so nothing random is written to `orbit_gen/` (§4). `$ORBIT_KEY` at runtime overrides the embedded key.
   - **`--embed`**: before the server build, copy `dist/client` to `orbit_gen/static/client/` (go:embed can't reach outside the package directory) and generate `orbit_gen/static/static.go` with `//go:embed all:client` behind the `orbit_embed` build tag. The server is built with `-tags orbit_embed`, and `app.New()` then uses `WithStaticFS(static.FS)` automatically. The result is one self-contained `dist/server` binary; `dist/client` is still written for CDN uploads. The env var `ORBIT_STATIC` overrides the embedded files if set.

### 6.1 `orbit fmt` (formatter)
**Status: Implemented** (2026-10-26; `internal/format`, `cmd/orbit/fmt.go`, LSP `textDocument/formatting`; user docs `docs/reference/cli.md#orbit-fmt`, `docs/editor-setup.md#formatting`). Accepted 2026-10-26. The user chose the recommended answer to every **(Q)** point it was asked: gofmt tabs in the frontmatter; `orbit fmt` like `go fmt`/`bun fmt` (in place over `src/`, `-l`/`-d` read-only, exit 1 when unformatted); the first release covers frontmatter, expressions/control headers and script/style; Bun formatting on whenever Bun ≥ 1.4.4 is found, no switch. Settled by the coordinator with the recommendations (triage rules): `.md` files are never touched (Astro leaves Markdown to Prettier); the starter, `examples/blog`, `docs/site` and the docs' `orbit` blocks are reformatted when fmt ships and kept fmt-clean by tests; no `orbit check --fmt`; imports keep gofmt's sort. Also with the implementation: `compiler.SplitFrontmatter` skips a leading UTF-8 BOM (Go stdlib behavior; today a BOM hides the frontmatter). Small details are logged in `docs/DECISIONS.md` ("Formatter").

**Implementation notes and deviations** (the spec below is kept as designed; where the implementation differs, this list wins):
- **Syntax errors**: `File.SyntaxErrors()` returns the errors raised before `validate` (split, lexer, parser). Go code that `go/format` rejects refuses the file too; so does a `//` comment ending a control header (`{if x // c` + newline + `}`), with its own message (`a // comment can't end a {if} header; use /* … */`), since `if x // c {` can't compile. An expression ending in a `//` comment is valid (codegen puts the call's `)` on the next line), so the formatter keeps a line break before its `}`.
- **Body chunks** are formatted inside `func _() {…}` with every non-raw-string line indented one tab, then dedented, instead of `format.Source(chunk)` on the partial source: go/format's partial path rejects a leading IIFE (`func() {…}()` is parsed as a malformed declaration), and go/printer formats a comment at the left margin of a function body differently from an indented one (`//0` → `// 0`), which broke idempotence. Raw string lines are never touched.
- **Chunk joining**: a chunk that ends with a comment on its own line(s) after its last statement gets a blank line before the next chunk even when the source had none; otherwise the comment would become the next statement's doc comment on the second run and move chunks (not idempotent).
- **Indentation from the output**: an expression's continuation lines and a block's closing tag take their base indentation from the line *as formatted so far* (regions are formatted in source order), not from the source, so a region that starts on the last line of a reformatted one formats the same on the second run. Scripts and styles are therefore formatted in the same ordered pass (one `bun fmt` at a time per file; files in parallel, at most GOMAXPROCS `bun` processes overall).
- **Safety check additions**: Go regions must also keep their number of comments (gofmt drops an empty `//` right above a block statement); a control header may differ by parentheses only (gofmt drops `if (x)`'s); CSS is compared after esbuild's `MinifySyntax` too (CSS printing keeps `.5rem` vs `0.5rem` as written); a formatted block containing `</script` (`</style`) is left as written. On the corpus (starter, `examples/blog`, `docs/site`, `internal/compiler/testdata`, the docs' `orbit` blocks) no region is left as written (the parentheses rule came from `internal/compiler/testdata/parse/control.orbit`'s `{if(x)}`, the only warning before it).
- **BOM**: `SplitFrontmatter` skips a leading BOM, so a BOM file's frontmatter is found and formatted; the BOM is kept by `orbit fmt` and no longer rendered.
- **Bun notice**: printed only when some file has a script or style Bun would format (`format.Result.Skipped`), not on every run without Bun.
- **Directory walks** also skip the project's own `dist/` (build output); explicit paths are followed even if they're symlinks, entries met in a walk aren't.
- **LSP**: formats the documents the server follows (`.orbit` files under the workspace's `src/`, the open buffer); other documents and Markdown get `null`.
- **Rendered-output test** (`internal/format/render_test.go`): instead of serving pages, `examples/blog` and `docs/site` are copied, "unformatted" (spaces in the frontmatter, padded braces), and generated in memory (`Generator.Plan`) before and after formatting under `compressHTML` `"html"`, `"jsx"` and `false`; the generated Go must be equal token for token (comments, i.e. line directives, and the `.orbit` positions passed to `ErrorAt` excepted). The generated code holds every static byte and the code that writes the rest, so this covers every request, not a sample. It runs without Bun; script/style changes are covered by the esbuild safety check.
- **Docs blocks**: a ```` ```orbit ```` block whose info string has the word `unformatted` (the "Before" examples of `cli.md`) is exempt; every other block that parses on its own must be fmt-clean. Asserted by `internal/format`'s `TestCorpus` (with the starter, `examples/blog` and `docs/site`), not in `internal/docstest`; `go test ./internal/format -run TestRewriteDocs -rewrite-docs` reformats the blocks.
- **Benchmark** (`orbit fmt -l` on `bench/devloop`'s 200-file project, 40 of them with a `<script>` and a `<style>`; 16 cores, Bun 1.4.4): 0.02 s wall (0.09 s CPU) without Bun, 0.04 s wall (0.35 s CPU) with Bun; on 4 cores (`GOMAXPROCS=4 taskset -c 0-3`) 0.02 s without Bun, 0.07 s with Bun.

**Invariant: formatting never changes rendered output.** `orbit fmt` rewrites only Go code (frontmatter, `{…}`, control headers) and the content of `<script>`/`<style>` blocks; template text, tags, attributes and the whitespace between them are copied byte for byte, so the result renders the same bytes in every `compressHTML` mode (§3.3 Whitespace: whitespace inside `{…}` and inside `<script>`/`<style>` is never template text). Script/style content changes bytes but not meaning (verified, below). HTML reindentation is a later phase with its own design (below).

**First release (Q: one release or phase 1 alone)**: the three regions together, since they share the parse, the region rewriter and the tests:
1. Frontmatter with `go/format`.
2. Template expressions and control headers with `go/format`.
3. `<script>`/`<style>` content with `bun fmt` (optional, needs Bun ≥ 1.4.4).

Later: HTML reindent between block-level elements only (never inline text, `<pre>`, `<textarea>`, raw-text elements; under `compressHTML: false` even that changes output, so it would be off or opt-in there; design it then). `gofmt -s` simplifications aren't applied (`go fmt` doesn't either).

**CLI** (`cmd/orbit/fmt.go`; formatting in a new `internal/format`, used by the CLI and the LSP; no new dependency: `go/format`, `go/scanner`, the existing esbuild API, Bun as an optional external program).
- `orbit fmt [-l] [-d] [-w] [paths…]` and `orbit fmt [-l|-d] --stdin-filepath=PATH`. Version hand-over as for every command (§6), so a project formats with its pinned CLI.
- **Paths (Q)**: none → every `.orbit` file under the project's `src/` (project = nearest `go.mod`; with none, `orbit fmt: no go.mod found; pass the files or directories to format`, exit 1). A directory → its `.orbit` regular files, recursively, skipping directories named `.*`, `node_modules` and `orbit_gen`; symlinks aren't followed (gofmt). A file → formatted whatever its location, without a project (formatting is per file); a file named explicitly that doesn't end in `.orbit` is an error (`orbit fmt: x.md: orbit fmt formats .orbit files; format Markdown with bun fmt`).
- **Modes (Q)**: no flag writes changed files in place (atomically: temp file in the same directory + rename, keeping the mode), silent on success (Orbit's commands are silent; `go fmt`, `bun fmt` and `prettier --write` also write by default). `-l` lists differing files (project-relative, `/` separators, sorted), `-d` prints `gofmt -d`-style unified diffs (`diff -u` headers `--- PATH.orig` / `+++ PATH`, a small line diff in `internal/format`; the stdlib's `internal/diff` isn't importable); neither writes unless `-w` is added. `-w` alone = no flag. `--stdin-filepath` reads stdin, writes the result to stdout (or the path/diff with `-l`/`-d`); `-w` or paths with it are usage errors.
- **Exit codes (Q)**: 0 success; 1 if any file failed (syntax error, Bun error or timeout, I/O), or with `-l`/`-d` and no `-w`, any file differs (so `orbit fmt -l` is a CI check, like `bun fmt -l` and `prettier -l`; gofmt exits 0 there); 2 usage. Files are processed in parallel (GOMAXPROCS), output sorted by path; one file's failure doesn't stop the others.

**Pipeline per file** (pure function `format.Source(path, src, opts) (out []byte, diags []compiler.Diagnostic)` plus `format.Regions` for the LSP):
1. `compiler.Parse(src, Strict)`. **Refuse the file** (unchanged output, exit 1, every error printed as `file:line:col: message`) on any *syntax* error: the frontmatter split and the lexer/parser errors (unclosed tags/blocks, `{` without `}`, mismatched closers, a `//` comment ending template Go code). The `validate` pass and `Analyze`'s checks (directive misuse, unknown props, unused imports, reserved names) are semantic and don't block formatting, as gofmt formats code that doesn't type-check. Implementation: `Parse` keeps the diagnostics raised before `validate` distinguishable (a `Syntax` flag on `Diagnostic`, or `ParseSyntax`).
2. Compute the **regions** (byte spans of the original file) and their replacement text: frontmatter code chunks, the delimiter lines, each `{…}` Go region, each formatted script/style content. Regions never overlap and are applied back to front. Any region whose Go code doesn't parse (go/format error) refuses the whole file, with the error mapped to the `.orbit` position (go/scanner positions in the wrapper → offset into the region → file).
3. **Safety net** (cheap, always on): the output is re-parsed and must give the same template tree as the input (node kinds, tag names, attribute names/kinds/static values, `Text` values byte-identical); Go regions must have the same go/scanner token stream (comments excluded; kinds, and literals except number literals compared by value; auto and explicit semicolons equal; an import block compared as a sorted set of specs); script/style blocks must give the same esbuild `Transform` output with `MinifyWhitespace` and `LegalComments: none` (loader `ts`/`js`/`css`) before and after. On a mismatch the region keeps its original text and a warning is printed (`file:line:col: warning: formatting this <script> would change its code; left as is (please report this)`); warnings don't change the exit code. This catches a mangled multi-line template literal (the reindent below) and Orbit bugs, not Bun's or gofmt's own choices.
4. If the output equals the input, the file is "formatted" (`-l` doesn't list it, no write).

**Frontmatter rules.**
- The delimiter lines become exactly `---` (trailing spaces and tabs dropped; delimiters may carry them, §2.2).
- The code is split with the same scanner as `Analyze` (`anScan`/`anSplit`/`anClassify`, §2.2) into depth-0 statements, each hoisted (`import`, `type`, `const`, named `func`/method) or body. Consecutive statements of the same kind form a **chunk**; the formatter never reorders statements or moves them between chunks (hoisting is semantic; the source order is what the user reads).
- **Comments between chunks**: a line comment on the last line of a statement belongs to it. The comment lines directly above a statement (no blank line between, go/parser's doc rule, `anDoc`) belong to its chunk; any other comment run belongs to the chunk before it (to the first chunk if there's none).
- **Hoisted chunk**: formatted as a file, `format.Source("package p\n\n" + chunk)`, then the `package p\n\n` prefix is removed. A full file (not a partial one) is what makes `go/format` sort imports (`ast.SortImports`: by path within blank-line-separated groups, duplicates removed); `.orbit` imports sort with the rest (their `./`/`../` paths sort first in a group).
- **Body chunk**: `format.Source(chunk)` on the chunk alone, which `go/format` parses as a partial source (a statement list, wrapped internally in `func _() { … }`) and prints at the indentation of its first code line, which is column 0 for a depth-0 chunk. Printing at depth 0 directly (not dedenting the output of a wrapped func) keeps the continuation lines of a multi-line raw string untouched. `return err`, labels and `goto` parse fine (no type check). A body chunk that `go/format` reads as a declaration list (`var x = 1`) prints the same.
- **Joining**: chunks are joined in order; between two chunks there's one blank line if the source had at least one there, otherwise none; inside a chunk, gofmt's own rules (it keeps at most one blank line, and separates an import block from the next declaration). Leading and trailing blank lines are dropped; the code ends with one newline before the closing `---`. An empty frontmatter (`---\n---`) stays empty; a comment-only frontmatter keeps its comments (trailing whitespace trimmed).
- **Indentation**: always tabs, gofmt's (Q: the repo's docs and examples use 4 spaces today; they'd be reformatted when `orbit fmt` lands). `go/format` offers no spaces mode, and gofmt's alignment is defined with tabs.
- **Line directives and source maps** are unaffected: codegen reads the formatted file like any other (§3.3; generated code itself is never formatted).

**Template expressions** (`Expr.Code`, `Attr.Code` of `AttrExpr`/`AttrShorthand`/`AttrSpread`, in content, attributes and RCDATA):
- Formatted as a standalone value: `format.Source("package p\n\nvar _ = " + code + "\n")`; the result is the text from the expression's first token to the end of its last token or trailing comment (located by re-parsing the formatted wrapper, not by string offsets). So `{a+b}` → `{a + b}` (gofmt's top-level spacing), `{f( x )}` → `{f(x)}`. A spread formats its operand (`{...attrs}`); a shorthand is an identifier, only its padding changes.
- **Braces hug the code**: `{ title }` → `{title}`, `{ }` → `{}` (Prettier's JSX printer; the code is trimmed by the parser anyway, §2.3). Attribute forms are never changed (no shorthand introduced or expanded, quotes and `a=bare` kept).
- **Multi-line expressions** keep gofmt's line breaks (it preserves the user's breaks in calls, composite literals and binary expressions). Continuation lines are re-indented: base = the leading whitespace of the template line holding the `{`; each continuation line gets base + one *template indent unit* per leading tab in gofmt's output. Lines that start inside a multi-line raw string (found with go/scanner on the formatted text) are copied as is. The template indent unit is detected per file: the leading whitespace of the template's indented lines (outside raw-text elements and `<pre>`): a tab if the first such line starts with a tab, otherwise the smallest number of leading spaces; two spaces when nothing is indented (Orbit's starter and Prettier's default).
- A `/* */` comment inside an expression is kept (it's in the wrapper file). A `//` comment can't occur (a syntax error, refused, §2.3 integration rule).

**Control headers**: the code is formatted inside a statement, `format.Source("package p\n\nfunc _() {\n\tif " + cond + " {\n\t}\n}\n")` (`for` likewise), and taken back from the formatted text between the keyword and the body's `{`, located by re-parsing (`IfStmt.If`+2 … `Body.Lbrace`, `ForStmt`/`RangeStmt` likewise); a multi-line header is re-indented like a multi-line expression. Tags are respelled `{if c}`, `{else if c}`, `{else}`, `{/if}`, `{for clause}`, `{/for}` (single spaces, no padding; the lexer already accepts `{ if c }`, `{else  if c}`, `{ /if }`). Examples: `{for i:=0;i<n;i++}` → `{for i := 0; i < n; i++}`, `{if n,ok:=m[k];ok}` → `{if n, ok := m[k]; ok}`. A condition needs parentheses around a composite literal in both the formatter's wrapper and codegen's `if … {`, so anything that compiles formats.

**Scripts and styles** (`Script.Content`, `Style.Content`):
- **Which**: processed scripts as `.ts`; any other `<script>` with no `type` or a JavaScript MIME type (`module`, `text/javascript`, `application/javascript`, the HTML spec's list) as `.js` (it isn't compiled, so it must be JS); every other `type` is left as is (`application/json`, `application/ld+json`, `importmap`, templates; `set:json` scripts have no content). Every `<style>` as `.css`. Empty or whitespace-only content is left as is. Rule: simplest.
- **Invocation**: `bun fmt --no-script --stdin-filepath=<path>.<n>.<ext>` with stdin = the dedented content, run in the project root (the file's directory outside a project), environment inherited. `<path>` is the project-relative `.orbit` path and `<n>` the block's index among the file's scripts (or styles), the naming `orbit check` uses (`src/components/Card.orbit.0.ts`), so Bun finds the user's `.prettierrc`/`bunfig.toml [fmt]`/`.editorconfig` as for a sibling `.ts` file, and a `package.json` `fmt` script can't take over (`--no-script`). One trailing newline is removed from the output. Exit status ≠ 0 → the file is refused, with Bun's first `error:` line and its `at <name>:L:C` mapped back to the `.orbit` position (line + block start line, column + dedent): `src/components/Card.orbit:31:9: <script>: Variable declaration expected.`
- **Indentation** (block content isn't template text, but a textual re-indent could change a multi-line template literal or CSS string): the content's common leading whitespace *k* (over non-blank lines) is removed before Bun and added back after, so the block keeps its indentation and literal lines shift by −k then +k (unchanged). Whitespace-only lines are neither dedented nor re-indented. Content that starts on the tag's line (`<style> .a { color: red } </style>`) moves to its own lines with k = the tag line's indentation + one template indent unit; on one line it can't hold a multi-line literal, so that's safe. The closing tag goes on its own line at the tag line's indentation. The print width isn't reduced by k (simplest; Prettier's embed would reduce it). The esbuild safety net (above) catches the remaining cases (a multi-line literal starting on the tag's line).
- **Bun detection**: `bun --version` once per run (shared with `internal/check`); missing or < 1.4.4 → scripts and styles are skipped with one stderr notice naming the install or upgrade command (as `orbit check`), exit code unchanged (Q: on by default).
- **Timeouts and parallelism**: one `bun fmt` process per block (measured: ~1.3 ms per invocation with Bun 1.4.4 on a small block, so stdin per block beats batching through temp files), at most GOMAXPROCS at a time across files, 10 s timeout each (the file is refused with `bun fmt timed out after 10s`).
- **Caching**: none (gofmt has none; a 200-file project with one script each is ~0.3 s of Bun CPU, spread over cores). Revisit with numbers.

**Line endings, BOM, final newline**:
- **CRLF**: a file whose first line break is `\r\n` is CRLF. Regions are formatted on LF text and written back with `\r\n` line breaks; everything else keeps its bytes (template text is rendered, so normalizing it would change output under `compressHTML: false`). Mixed files follow their first line break.
- **BOM**: kept. `SplitFrontmatter` skips it (as Go's scanner does), so the frontmatter after it is found and formatted, and it isn't rendered.
- **Final newline**: the template's end is never changed (adding or removing a newline is rendered under `compressHTML: false`). A file that ends with the closing `---` and no line break gets one (the template stays empty, since the delimiter's line break isn't template text).

**Markdown (Q)**: `orbit fmt` doesn't touch `.md` files (Astro leaves Markdown to Prettier; `bun fmt` formats Markdown, YAML frontmatter included). Directory walks skip them; a `.md` named explicitly is an error pointing to `bun fmt`.

**LSP** (`internal/lsp`, `textDocument/formatting`): advertised as `documentFormattingProvider: true`. Formats the open document's buffer with `format.Regions` and returns one `TextEdit` per changed region (minimal edits, so cursors and folds outside them stay), ranges in UTF-16 via the document's line index. Full document only: no `rangeFormatting` and no `onTypeFormatting` (gopls has neither). `FormattingOptions` (`tabSize`, `insertSpaces`) are ignored (gopls ignores them; the frontmatter is gofmt's, the template's unit is detected, Bun reads its own config). A syntax error returns no edits (`[]`), since the diagnostic is already shown; a Bun error or timeout drops only that block's edit. Without Bun, script/style regions are skipped and a `window/showMessage` (Info) says so once per session. Requests don't wait for gopls or the readiness barrier: formatting is syntax-only. `.md` documents get `null`. VS Code: `package.json` sets `configurationDefaults` `"[orbit]": {"editor.defaultFormatter": "orbit-z.orbit"}`; format-on-save stays the user's choice.

**`orbit check` (Q)**: doesn't check formatting; CI runs `orbit fmt -l` as its own step (it needs no `orbit_gen/`).

**Tests** (`internal/format`, `cmd/orbit`, `internal/lsp`):
- Golden files `internal/format/testdata/*.orbit` → `*.golden` (`-update`), one case per rule above: chunk interleaving and comments, imports with `.orbit` paths, raw strings in a body and in an expression, multi-line expressions with tab and space templates, every control header form, braces padding, CRLF, BOM, missing final newline, empty and comment-only frontmatter, refused files (each syntax error kind, with positions), and script/style cases (skipped without Bun; Bun output is pinned to ≥ 1.4.4 and the goldens are regenerated deliberately).
- **Idempotence**: fmt∘fmt = fmt on every golden and the corpus (`examples/blog`, `docs/site`, `internal/compiler/testdata`, the starter, every ```` ```orbit ```` block in `docs/`).
- **Equivalence**: for the corpus, the template trees are equal (as the safety net), and the frontmatter and every Go region are equal as go/ast trees with positions and comments ignored (imports compared as sets).
- **Rendered-output equality**: the corpus pages are generated and rendered before and after formatting, under `compressHTML` `"html"`, `"jsx"` and `false`, with byte-identical HTML (processed script/style bundles compared after esbuild minification).
- `FuzzFormat`: on any input that parses without syntax errors, `Source` doesn't panic, is idempotent, and keeps the template tree.
- CLI: modes and exit codes (`-l`, `-d`, `-w`, `--stdin-filepath`, usage errors), path walking and skips, atomic writes, the Bun notice; LSP: the edits applied to the buffer give the CLI's output (UTF-16 ranges with non-ASCII text), `[]` on a syntax error.
- Once it ships, the starter (`orbit new`), `examples/blog`, `docs/site` and the docs' `orbit` blocks are reformatted, and a test keeps the starter fmt-clean (Q: also assert it for the docs' blocks in `internal/docstest`).
- **Benchmark**: `orbit fmt -l` on `bench/devloop`'s 200-file synthetic project, without and with Bun; measured and published, no target. It doesn't run in `orbit dev`/`build`, so the dev loop is unaffected.

## 7. Dev server (`internal/dev`)
- The proxy listens on `localhost:4321` (`--port`; `--host` listens on all interfaces, as Astro's `--host` does) and serves only `/__orbit/*`: `/__orbit/events` (SSE), `/__orbit/reload.js`, and the overlay.
- Everything else, including `/_orbit/*` and `public/` files, is proxied to the **user's server entry**, which runs with `ORBIT_DEV=1` on a random localhost port and serves assets from `ORBIT_STATIC` and `ORBIT_PUBLIC` (§5b). So auth, logging and custom routes (e.g. a handler for `/robots.txt`) behave exactly as in prod.
- **Reload injection**: the proxy strips `Accept-Encoding` from forwarded requests, so responses come back uncompressed even under a user gzip middleware. `text/html` responses get the reload script before `</body>`, and `Content-Length` is recomputed. Responses without `</body>`, such as `/_orbit/island/*` fragments, are left alone. A response that is still `Content-Encoding`-compressed is passed through without injection, with a one-time warning.
- Pages render per request in dev exactly as in prod; only the asset source (`ORBIT_STATIC`, `ORBIT_PUBLIC`) and the error overlay differ (§5b). Dev compiles the hook flavor in `orbit_gen/` and never the build flavor (§3.5), so a component body edit recompiles only that component's impl package and `orbit_gen/link`; allocation counts and timings differ from an `orbit build` binary (same HTML).
- `fsnotify` watches `src/`, `public/`, `*.go` in the module, and `orbit.json`, `go.mod`/`go.sum`, `tsconfig.json` and `package.json` (excluding `orbit_gen`, `dist`, `.orbit`, `node_modules`, `testdata/`, `_`- and dot-directories, and `_test.go` files), with a 50 ms trailing debounce; events during a build join the next batch, and a file created and removed within one batch (atomic saves) is dropped. Requests wait from the first file event until the rebuild and swap are done; a request whose dial is refused mid-swap is retried once against the new app. (Implemented, milestone 10.) It also watches the files matching `orbit.json` `"watch"` globs (§8), inside or outside the module: each pattern's leading literal directory, recursively when the pattern can match deeper. A change made only of watched (and `public/`) files restarts the running binary without regenerating or `go build`, then reloads; mixed with other changes, the app is restarted even when no Go rebuild was needed.

### 7.1 Live reload
Every change ends with a **full page reload**; there's no HMR. What the server does depends on the change:
- **Only `<style>`/`<script>` content**: incremental esbuild rebuild, which rewrites `.orbit/dev/manifest.json`; the running app picks up the new hashed URLs (§5). No Go rebuild. With code splitting (§5e) this step went from 16 to 26–31 ms at 200 files (`bench/devloop -dev -kinds css,ts`): esbuild links and prints each page's copy of a block. When only asset files under `src/` changed (`.ts`, `.css`), the bundles are rebuilt from the last bundle input without reloading the project.
- **esbuild is skipped** (user addition, milestone 10) when a key over the asset input (block contents, kinds, line:col, page graphs) is unchanged and no asset file changed: template, frontmatter, `Props` and `.go` edits.
- **`public/` files**: nothing to rebuild.
- **Anything else**, including **gaining or losing a scoped `<style>` or a processed `<script>`** (or toggling `is:global`), which changes the scope attributes in the template output or the esbuild entries. Also templates, frontmatter, user `.go` files, `src/locals`, `orbit.json`, `go.mod`:
  0. **`.go`-only edits start `go build` at once** (user addition, milestone 10), in parallel with regenerating, into the unused one of two binary slots (`.orbit/devserver/server-{0,1}`). If regenerating writes nothing to `orbit_gen`, that build stands; otherwise it's rebuilt after. An unneeded early build is cancelled.
  1. Regenerate only the affected packages, with typed writes (the compiled `orbit_gen/manifest` stays empty in dev, so a CSS edit never forces a Go rebuild). The type checker stays resident in the dev process and re-checks only those packages (§3.3): about 0.3 ms for a body edit.
  2. `go build "-ldflags=-s -w" -gcflags=all=-dwarf=false "-gcflags=./orbit_gen/...=-l -dwarf=false" -buildvcs=false`, with `GOGC` and `GOMEMLIMIT` removed from its environment (dev builds only; the server keeps them). No DWARF or symbol table, no inlining in generated packages (the last matching `-gcflags` pattern wins, so the `orbit_gen` one repeats `-dwarf=false`), no VCS stamping (a `git` run per build), and cmd/compile's own heap tuning, which it applies only when both variables are unset. A `-gcflags` or `-buildvcs` in `GOFLAGS` replaces Orbit's; a `-ldflags` there is merged. The resident type checker's `go list -export` gets the same `-gcflags`/`-buildvcs`, so it shares compiled packages with `go build`. Only changed packages recompile, thanks to the per-component packages. Measured in the spike (§3.3): compiling the changed package takes about 35 ms of a body-edit rebuild, the rest is the app recompile and link, and `-ldflags=-w` brought that rebuild from 190 to 148 ms. The post-MVP build-speed pass (`-s -w`, `-dwarf=false`, `-buildvcs=false`, no `GOGC`, and the runtime's island sealer kept out of `Ctx`'s type graph, see §4b) took a template edit from ~0.22 to ~0.19 s and the worst case below by ~20–25%; numbers in `docs/performance.md`.
  3. Stop the old app (SIGINT, wait for graceful shutdown up to 5 s, then kill), then start the new one on a fresh port and wait until it accepts connections. Requests during the swap wait. Running both side by side would break user apps that bind fixed ports or hold file locks.

Then it sends a `reload` event over SSE.

**Measured worst case**: a user `.go` edit that every `Props` depends on (e.g. `internal/posts`) makes `go build` recompile every generated package. At 200 components that takes about 0.75 s on 16 threads and about 1.3 s on 4 cores, even with no type check at all (spike C). It's `go build`, not typed writes; the dev build flags of step 2 are the mitigation in place. **Milestone 9 finding** (`bench/devloop`): a `.go` edit that changes only a function body costs as much as an API change, because cmd/go recompiles a package's direct importers whenever it changes, even when its export data doesn't. Results and milestone 10 mitigations are in `bench/devloop/README.md` and `docs/performance.md`.

The client (`/__orbit/reload.js`, a few lines, embedded) calls `location.reload()` on `reload`. If the SSE connection drops, it reconnects with backoff and reloads once it's back.

On a compile error, the old app keeps running and the proxy serves an overlay page for HTML requests: the cleaned-up message (§3.3, error output), `file:line:col`, and a code frame. If the new app fails to start, the overlay shows its stderr. Render errors are shown by the app's own dev error overlay (§4). The overlay stays subscribed to SSE, so fixing the error reloads automatically.

## 8. Config (`orbit.json`, all optional)
```json
{ "site": "https://example.com", "server": "./cmd/server", "embed": false, "compressHTML": "jsx", "sourcemap": false, "watch": [] }
```
- `compressHTML` (user decision; like Astro's option): `"jsx"` (**default**, user decision 2026-10-26 reversing the earlier `"html"` default, so Astro v7 sites port with the same whitespace; Astro v7's default JSX whitespace rules; `{" "}` forces a space; implemented: `orbit new`, `examples/blog`, `docs/site` and the benchmark sites use it, with `{" "}` where text must keep a space), `"html"` (HTML-aware lossless whitespace collapsing, Astro's `true`; `true` is accepted and means `"html"`, user decision) or `false` (verbatim). Applied at codegen time, so it costs nothing at runtime. `<pre>`, `<textarea>`, `<script>`, `<style>`, HTML comments and `{expr}` output are never altered. Rules in `internal/compiler/DESIGN.md`.
- `site`: the absolute site URL, exposed as `ctx.Site`. Unset gives a nil `ctx.Site` (like `Astro.site` being `undefined`); a value that isn't absolute (no scheme or host) is an error when `orbit.json` is loaded.
- `server`: the server entry package; default `./cmd/server` if it exists, else the generated one (§5b).
- `embed`: like `orbit build --embed`; the flag turns embedding on even when this is `false`.
